# BUDLUM LEAF · THE DIRECTIVE · PART 5 · THE RADIO AND THE LINK

> **Revision L.6.0 note (2026-10-12)**. `FINAL/21-stretch-stem-and-projection.md` supersedes this file where they differ. The antenna set, the radios and the latency settings of Part 21, Section 21.4 replace the B bar rules of Section 5.2. The B module seats on any edge and either face, and a 5G standalone modem is in the module.

---

## 5.1 The radio set

| The radio | The class | The part | The note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Wi-Fi 7 | tri-band, multi-link operation, 320 MHz, 2 streams minimum and 4 preferred | a combo part with the Bluetooth radio | the main path indoors |
| 5G | standalone, sub-6 GHz, 4 × 4 MIMO, carrier aggregation | the same front end with a sub-6 module | the main path outdoors |
| Millimetre wave | an antenna in package, a 15 × 15 mm block per band | optional | short range, high rate, a dock path |
| Satellite | non-terrestrial through the cellular radio | no extra antenna | the last resort, a message class |
| Bluetooth | the wallet authenticator, the pen, the keyboard, the ear part | mandatory | the answer of Ayaz, question 14 |
| Near field | the tap and the pairing | | |
| Ultra-wide band | the ranging and the find-my-module function | a candidate | Part 6 |
| Navigation | multi-constellation | | |

---

## 5.2 The antenna law for a 160 × 80 mm body

1. **The upright B bar is the primary antenna**. On the top edge (`y = +80 mm`), the upright B bar projects into air. Because the top edge runs parallel to the `X` bend axis, the antenna never bends at `R 25.5 mm`. `[DECISION]`
2. **No radiator sits over the pixel area**. The panel stack costs 6 to 10 dB and detunes elements by 8 percent. Every antenna stays in the upright B bar and perimeter frame. Gates RF-9 and RF-11 measure the rule.
3. **The AgNW/ITO mesh and `90°`-bent B stem form the RF system**. Transparent `AgNW/ITO` layers inside the `2.00 mm` body couple to the `90°` B stem (`SAR = 0.054 W/kg`). Gate RF-10 measures the ground reference.
4. **The upright B bar houses `Wi-Fi 7`, `BT 5.4`, and `UWB`**. A module on the top edge gives `+2.4 to +3.4 dBi` gain, and a detached module acts as a relay. Gate RF-13 measures both states.
5. **The top-edge gold pads bridge the RF reference**. Pad 12 grounds the upright B bar to the prism frame across the `y = +80.0 mm` seat. When detached, the B module uses its `400 Mbit/s` link.
6. The match is measured flat, at the gentle bend and at the wrap, because the bend changes the geometry. The keep-out is a 6 mm ring of thinned ground and print around every radiator.

**The part by part file is `HARDWARE/19-THE-RADIO-AND-ANTENNAS-PART-BY-PART.md`**. It carries the 21 parts, the eight radiators, the ground arithmetic and gates **RF-9 to RF-20**. `[DECISION]`

---

## 5.3 The five conflicts, and the budget of each

| The conflict | The budget | The gate | The mitigation if it fails |
|---|---|---|---|
| The cassette sits between the antennas | the loss through the cassette | RF-2 | the window extends under the cassette edge, or the antennas move to the corners |
| The metal spine near the radiator | the efficiency of each antenna | RF-1 | the ring thinning, and a 3 mm stand off |
| The charge path against the radios | the desense of each receiver with the cable attached and on the dock | MEC-6, RF-3 | the shield of the receptacle, the pad routing and a filter. **The coil left the design, so the coil schedule left with it** |
| The bend changes the match | the return loss at R 70 mm and at R 25.5 mm against flat | RF-4 | a tunable matching network, written into the record |
| The module magnet over an antenna | the gain loss | RF-1, MEC-10 | the keep-out area, or the clip of Section 5.3.1 |

### 5.3.1 The decision rule of the attach

**An attach that costs 3 dB of antenna gain is worse than a mechanical clip, because the link is the product**. If the magnet loses more than 1.5 dB, the module moves to a clip, and the measurement decides. `[DECISION]`

---

## 5.4 The link classes

| The class | The downlink | The uplink | The round trip | The radio |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C0 safe | none | none | none | the local floor |
| C0b model | a signed display list | a small intent | sub 20 ms | any |
| C1 delta | 10 kbit/s to 2 Mbit/s | below 100 kbit/s | 20 to 60 ms is good | Wi-Fi, 5G |
| C2 video | 10 to 30 Mbit/s | 100 kbit/s | below 40 ms | Wi-Fi 7, 5G |
| C3 still and 4K | 30 to 80 Mbit/s | 100 kbit/s | below 40 ms | Wi-Fi 7, millimetre wave |
| C4 camera up | 5 to 130 Mbit/s | the capture payload | the deadline classes of Part 10 | Wi-Fi 7, 5G, a near validator |
| The satellite class | measured only | | 20 to 40 ms | the message class |

`[PUBLICATION]` In 2026 bands, Wi-Fi 7 multi-link reaches a 12 ms 99th percentile and under 2 ms average at close range. Field 5G air latency is 8 to 12 ms, and an edge node 10 to 15 km away adds 1 to 5 ms. A central cloud is 50 to 100 ms, and direct-to-cell satellite is 20 to 40 ms.

---

## 5.5 The path manager, and how it chooses

```
PathCandidate { kind, rtt_ms, up_mbit, down_mbit, energy_mw, trusted, operator }
PathPlan      { primary, mirrors[2], floor }
```

| The order | The rule | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | trust | an operator that fails the bond, the stake or the record test is out, whatever its latency |
| 2 | latency | the lowest measured round trip that can carry the class |
| 3 | energy | the least milliwatt per delivered megabit |
| 4 | the mirrors | two paths carry the critical intent, so a handover is invisible |

**How it is measured**. A rolling median and a 99th percentile per path, a 1 Hz probe on a silent link, and a full measurement on every class change. The numbers are kept in the session record. `[DECISION]`

---

## 5.6 The fail-safe ladder

| The state | The trigger | The behaviour | The user sees |
|---|---|---|---|
| Full | a path at or above the class | the session at its class | nothing |
| Reduced | the round trip above the class for 1 s | the class drops one step | the class line |
| Cellular | the local path is lost | the session moves | a move note |
| Satellite | everything else is lost | the message class | the satellite class |
| No link | no path at all | the local floor and the safe screen | the state |
| Optical | the user or the lease refuses a link | the optical path of Section 5.7 | the optical mode |

**The rule**. A session never enters a stuck state, and a reduction is always visible. `[DECISION]`

---

## 5.7 The optical path

1. The Budlum tree already carries the parts in B.U.D.. The note packing crate defines compressed, encrypted payloads and deterministic QR frames. `[TREE]`
2. **The rule**. A surface shows a signed receipt or payload as optical frames on the panel. The other device reads it with no radio. `[DECISION]`
3. **The cost, measured**. A 4 627 byte ML-DSA-87 signature payload takes about 240 frames. The transfer takes a few seconds on a held screen. `[TREE]`
4. **The implementation duty**. The device calls the note packing crate and never re-derives the packing. A second field definition is a silent failure class.

---

## 5.8 How the link is tested

| The test | The method | The number |
|---|---|---|
| The free hold and the hand hold | a calibrated range, a phantom head and hand, 3 bands | the efficiency and the pattern |
| The desense set | the transmitter on, the receiver measured | the desense per band |
| The bend | flat, R 70, R 25.5 | the return loss and the efficiency delta |
| The module as a site | attached, detached, on a table | the gain and the pattern |
| The class measurement | a 30 minute trace in two cities | the median and the 99th percentile |
| The handover | a planned and an unplanned move | the interruption time |

---

## 5.9 The radio gates

| Gate | The test | The pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| RF-1 | the antenna set: the efficiency, the pattern, the window, the anchor ring, the module gain | every budget of Section 5.3, and the 1.5 dB rule |
| RF-2 | the cassette in the field | the loss inside the stated budget |
| RF-3 | the desense with the charge cable attached and on the dock | both radios inside their stated margin in both states |
| RF-4 | the bent match | the return loss at the radius inside the limit |
| RF-5 | the path manager: a slow path, a lost path, a lying operator | the plan is correct and the untrusted path is never used |
| RF-6 | the fail-safe ladder, all six states | each state is reached and each is visible |
| RF-7 | the optical path: a receipt and a small payload, no radio | the reader verifies the receipt, and the frame count and the time are in the record |
| RF-8 | the satellite class, measured in a real market | the measured rate and delay, and the claim limited to it |
| RF-9 | the eight radiators: the band, the peak gain, the efficiency and the keep-out print | each band inside its window, the gain inside 1 dB, and the print hides the metal |
| RF-10 | the tiled steel as the RF ground, from 0.7 to 60 GHz | the mosaic behaves as solid ground, and the ripple stays under 0.5 dB |
| RF-11 | the panel keep-out: a radiator under the panel, and the detune it causes | no radiator over the pixel area, and under 0.5 dB of loss from the field |
| RF-12 | the bend: a radiator across the width, and the feed across the wrap zone | the match flat and at R 25.5 mm inside the limit, and no crack after 5 000 wraps |
| RF-13 | the module slot: the diversity path, the detached relay and the 1.5 dB rule | under 1.5 dB when seated, and a link at 10 m when the module is detached |
| RF-14 | the camera link: the part's own 400 Mbit/s link and the Wi-Fi Direct pair | 400 Mbit/s coded, no dropped frame in 30 minutes, and the pairing holds |
| RF-15 | the NFC coil, its ferrite and the first seat pairing | a 30 mm read range, and the pairing completes in one touch |
| RF-16 | the SAR at 1 g and 10 g, and the power back-off | the limits of the market are met, and the back-off is in the record |
| RF-17 | the coexistence of Wi-Fi, Bluetooth, 5G, UWB and GNSS at once | every chain inside its margin, and the cans hold the transmitters |
| RF-18 | the aperture tuner over 0.7 to 3.8 GHz, and the GNSS under a hand | the band covered at -3 dB, and the position inside 3 m in the open |
| RF-19 | the millimetre wave module, optional: the scan and the rate | the beam finds the dock, and 800 Mbit/s at 5 m |
| RF-20 | the field against the radio: a part seated on the band, and the pen dock | under 0.5 dB of loss, and no desense with the charge in |

---

# BUDLUM LEAF · THE DIRECTIVE · PART 6 · THE 90°-BENT-STEM B MODULE, THE SOLE USB-C PORT, THE PEN AND THE SOUND

> **Revision L.6.0 note (2026-10-12)**. `FINAL/21-stretch-stem-and-projection.md` supersedes this file where they differ. Part 21 and `HARDWARE/05` define the B module. It is `4.00 mm` thick with a `1.00 mm` LCP bar on the top edge and no hinge. The `50 MP` camera sits in a vertical column, and the `3.5 mm` jack in the rim. USB-C is a separate part.

**The standing of this part at Revision L.5.0, of 2026-10-12**. Modeled 1:1 from `uploads/b.png` (`20.0 g`), **only the stem (`çubuk`, `8 x 32 x 4.5 mm`) bends at a `90°` right angle (`dik açıyla`)**. Its **circular ring (`yuvarlak`, `32 x 32 x 4.0 mm`) lies flat on top of the screen**. Moving the `90°`-bent stem around the 4 edges docks the B module to the **Front (`+Z`) or Back (`-Z`)** of the screen.

| The item | The state at Revision L.5.0 |
|---|---|
| the camera | **fixed inside the upper Z-stack (`z = 0.90..3.60 mm`)** of the flat `Ø32.0 / Ø24.0 mm` ring lying on the screen (`HARDWARE/05`, `13`, `20`) |
| the ports | **zero USB-C and zero buttons on the screen body** (`Qi2.2` wireless charging). **The sole `USB Type-C` (`40 W PD 3.1 + DP 2.1`) lives inside the `90°`-bent B stem** (`HARDWARE/04`, `14`) |
| the cells | **`12.0 Wh` (`3 200 mAh`) `LLZO/PEO` solid-state battery** across `Q1..Q4` (`68.0 g`) + **`0.45 Wh` bridge cell** in the B ring (`HARDWARE/11`) |
| the processing split | **Zero-CPU thin-client architecture**: `U1` Master TCON + `U2` `UWB / mmWave` SiP in the 6L B ring PCBA; compute runs on validators (`Section 6.3`) |
| the heat of Section 6.4 | `1.4 W` capture heat in the B module is isolated from the rear AMOLED by a `0.15 mm` (`1 200 W/m·K`) Cu-graphite mid-plane shield |
| the magnets and docking of Section 6.5 | `24` N52H + `64` gold leaves join `+` (`<15 µm` seam); `8` N52H (`7.2 N`) + `12` pins dock the `90°` B stem |
| the pen of Section 6.6 | **stays valid**, and it docks magnetically along the side edge |
| the sound set of Section 6.7 | **in-ring dual MEMS mics (`68 dB` SNR) and `9.0 x 3.2 x 2.2 mm` speaker (`78 dB`)** inside the flat B ring (`HARDWARE/13`) |
| the gates of Section 6.8 | the **CAM-1 to CAM-20 family of `HARDWARE/05-B-MODULE-CAMERA-AND-PANEL.md`** and **MON-1 to MON-20 of `HARDWARE/20-THE-BUDLUM-MONOGRAM.md`** |

**The rule of the retirement**. A claim about the retired module is struck, and the replacement is named. The sections below keep their text until the next block re-issues them. Every row that names three cameras, a 20 Wh cell or a bulky bottom battery pack is **struck**. `[DECISION]`

---

## 6.1 The retired module, and the Revision L.5.0 architecture

**This section is re-issued**. The bulky external bottom battery pack is cancelled. The **`2.00 mm` (`68 g`) body** splits across `+` into **four `17 g` solid-state quadrants (`Q1..Q4`, `12.0 Wh`, `Qi2.2`)**. The **`90°`-bent-stem B module (`20.0 g`, 32 parts)** bends only its stem (`USB-C` + antennas) by `90°` along any of the 4 edges. Its circular ring (`50 MP` camera, rear `Ø22.4 mm` AMOLED, `U1` TCON, `U2` `UWB / mmWave` SiP) lies flat on the screen (`88.0 g` total).

| The property | The value at Revision L.5.0 |
|---|---|
| the thickness | each of the 4 screen-battery quadrants (`Q1..Q4`) is **`2.00 mm`** (`68.0 g` total). The flat B ring is **`4.00 mm`** (`r = 0.55 mm`) and its `90°`-bent stem is `8.0 x 32.0 x 4.50 mm` |
| the camera | **one** 1-inch-class sensor (`12.8 x 9.6 x 0.65 mm`, `50 MP`), **fixed inside the upper Z-stack (`z = 0.90..3.60 mm`)** of the flat ring (`HARDWARE/05`) |
| the rear panel & acoustics | **no rotating parts**. Lower Z-stack (`z = 0.00..0.90 mm`) holds the **`0.15 mm` shield**, **`Ø22.4 mm` round LTPO AMOLED** (`480 x 480 px`), and `0.35 mm` sapphire |
| the cells & `<3 s` Hot-Swap | the 4 quadrants hold **`4 x 3.0 Wh = 12.0 Wh` (`3 200 mAh`) `LLZO/PEO` cells** (`<3 s` swap per `EU 2023/1542` Art. 11), and the B ring holds a **`0.45 Wh`** bridge cell |
| the identity | every `+` seam quadrant and the `90°` B module answer a cryptographic Dead-Pad challenge in **3 ms** before power switches on |
| the mass | **17 g** per quadrant (`68 g` 4-Quadrant body) and **88 g** complete with the `20 g` `90°`-bent-stem B module |
| the rows struck | all legacy rotating-barrel, 317-dot matrix, and bulky bottom battery pack designs are permanently removed. `HARDWARE/20`, `HARDWARE/13`, `HARDWARE/15` and `HARDWARE/05` specify the live hardware |

---

## 6.2 The one camera, and what happened to the other two

**One lens does the work of three, and the crop does the rest**. The order of Ayaz asks for **one large round camera instead of three**. The three folded stacks of revision L.2 are therefore struck, and the single fixed camera of the module takes their place. `[DECISION]`

| The role that the three cameras covered | What covers it at revision L.3 |
|---|---|
| the light, the everyday photograph | the 1 inch class sensor at **f/1.5**, with **5.2 times** the light collecting area of the standard phone sensor |
| the room, the group, the landscape | the **66 degree** diagonal field of the 10.5 mm lens, which is **28.4 mm equivalent** |
| the far, the portrait | the **crop**: 2 times keeps 12.5 MP at **56.8 mm equivalent**, and 3 times keeps 5.6 MP at 85.2 mm |
| the self portrait | the **panel**: the camera looks forward, the round AMOLED on its back shows a live preview, and the user frames with the main lens |

**The honest reading**. A crop is not a telephoto lens, and the program says so. What the single fixed camera buys is a larger sensor, more light and one lens to certify. What it costs is the optical zoom, and that cost is accepted and recorded in Part 20, Section 20.3.4. `[DECISION]`

**Why folded optics are the answer**. A straight stack grows along the optical axis, so its height becomes the focal length. A folded stack turns the axis with a prism, and the path runs across the plate. That is how 17.4 mm of path stands inside 3.40 mm. `[DECISION]` gate CAM-1 and gate CAM-3 replace this with a drawing and a measurement.

**Why the path folds, and where it runs**. The module folds its optical path inside the ring: **3.4 mm in and 14.0 mm across**. That puts **17.4 mm of optical path inside 3.40 mm of depth**. A straight path of 16.0 mm cannot stand in a body of 4.00 mm, so the prism is not a preference. It is the only geometry that fits. The eight elements sit in four groups, and two of them are aspheres. `[DECISION]` **The fallback ladder**: if the partner cannot hold 8 elements in 3.40 mm, a 6-element path fits the same 4.00 mm ring.

---

## 6.3 The processing split, in three zones

### 6.3.1 Zone 1: the sensor and its immediate silicon

| The work | The power |
|---|---|
| the readout of 50 MP at 12 bit | 0.20 to 0.25 W in a still |
| the binning to a 12.5 MP everyday frame and a 2 MP preview | inside the same budget |
| the black level, the defect map, the lens shading | the calibration comes from the record |
| a two frame HDR merge | in the sensor logic |
| the mechanical and the optical stabilisation | 0.05 to 0.15 W |

**The rule**. Zone 1 has no operating system, no user code and no writable firmware beyond a signed update.

### 6.3.2 Zone 2: the sentinel on the ring board

| The work | The number |
|---|---|
| the remosaic of the quad pattern | a fixed function block |
| the compression of a capture to a 10 bit intra stream | 12 to 30 MB per still, and 8 MB at 12.5 MP |
| the focus stack, the gyro timeline, the motion metadata | the payload the validators need |
| the frame selection of a burst | 8 to 30 frames ranked locally |
| the video encode, 4K120 | 1.2 to 2.0 W, the largest single term. 8K is coded in the part, and the link never sees raw 8K |

**The rule**. The sentinel **prepares a payload**; it cannot invent a result, cannot paint an image, and cannot sign a photograph as a verified inference.

### 6.3.3 Zone 3: the validators

| The work | The note |
|---|---|
| multi frame stacking, denoise, deblur | the computational photograph at the paid effort tier |
| the portrait depth, the semantic masks, the relight | the operator models under the lease |
| the super resolution and the tone mapping | the final image |
| the archive, the search, the recall | B.U.D., under the user authority, with Pollen in the read path |
| the verification | the operator, the model and the tier are in the record |

### 6.3.4 The four latency classes of a photograph

| The case | What the user sees | The uplink | The time |
|---|---|---|---|
| a preview | local, 2 MP at 30 frames per second, never leaves the device | 0 | instant |
| a fast still | a locally tone mapped image, replaced when the processed one arrives | 3 to 8 MB | 1 to 3 s near, longer far |
| a full still, 50 MP | the printed preview with a progress mark, then the full image | 12 to 30 MB | 2 to 6 s near, 10 to 30 s far |
| a night burst of 20 frames | the same, with the mark | 200 to 500 MB | 20 to 90 s near, minutes far |
| a 4K120 video | a local encode, stored in B.U.D. | 30 to 80 Mbit/s | live |

**The rule of the honest camera**. A capture that needs a long uplink states the time and the class **before** the shutter. `[DECISION]` (the answer of Ayaz, question 28.)

**The provisional pipeline**. The module runs a local provisional pipeline (demosaic, two-frame HDR, tone map, light denoise) reaching 60 to 75 percent of reference quality. The user gets an immediate photograph labelled `local provisional`. The validator result then replaces the view while the record keeps both. `[HYPOTHESIS]` gates CAM-9 and DST-5.

---

## 6.4 The heat of the monogram

| The case | The sensor and the plate | The sentinel | The link | The total | The time |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| the viewfinder only | 0.15 W | 0.25 W | 0.05 W | 0.45 W | continuous |
| one still, metered | 0.35 W | 0.45 W | 0.20 W | **1.00 W** | 0.5 s |
| a night burst of 20 frames | 0.60 W | 0.70 W | 0.90 W | 2.20 W | 2 to 4 s |
| a 4K60 video | 0.50 W | 1.30 W | 0.60 W | 2.40 W | continuous |
| a 4K120 video | 0.70 W | 2.00 W | 1.20 W | 3.90 W | continuous, derated |

**Where the heat goes**. The module contacts the top edge over **128 mm²**. Its **1 280 mm²** shell acts as a fin, and the body is a **256 cm²** radiator. The part is the hot spot and the surface spreads it. Twelve mechanisms make the pair survive, and they are written in Part 11. They include the graphite spreader, the `<3 K/W` interface, sensor power reduction, hub encoding, and duty rules. They also include temperature-aware capture, cell thermal isolation, dark-frame recalibration, and honest surface state reporting.

---

## 6.5 The magnets, the guests and the keep-outs

| The item | The rule |
|---|---|
| The pen dock | at the rail end of the long edge, away from the monogram |
| The stabiliser of the camera, inside the ring | **no magnet within 8 mm** of the sensor carriage, and the ring wall carries the shield |
| The antenna windows of the leaf | no magnet over a window; the pattern keeps a 6 mm clear ring |
| The guest parts | a guest card or a key fob must not sit on the field band; the case states it |
| The measurement | with the pen attached, the monogram holds its image quality, and the part holds at R 60 mm; gates CAM-7 and B-10 |

---

## 6.6 The pen

| The property | The value |
|---|---|
| The class | **active**: its own radio, pressure, tilt, a low latency ink path |
| The charge | it attaches magnetically to the field band and charges there; a five minute attach gives about six hours of writing |
| The connection | Bluetooth is mandatory; a wired path exists through the USB-C of the rail module |
| The mass | 12 to 17 g |
| The first product | **the pen ships with the first product** `[DECISION]` |
| The latency | the ink follows the touch layer at 3 to 8 ms locally |

---

## 6.7 The sound set

| The part | The placement | The number |
|---|---|---|
| The microphone array | three microphones in the rail module, spaced for a beam | a 30 dB front to back ratio target `[HYPOTHESIS]` |
| The speaker | one small full range driver in the rail module | 1 W class, for a call and a prompt |
| The jack | a 3.5 mm receptacle in the rail module | a wired headset with no adapter and no radio |
| The rule | the faces gain no sound hardware; a Leaf 160 with the rail removed is a silent surface with a haptic and a screen | `[DECISION]` |

**The privacy rule of the sound set**. The microphone follows the lease rule of Part 11. A capture without a lease is refused, and the module declares the sensor state in hardware.

---

## 6.8 The camera gates

| Gate | The test | The pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| CAM-1 | the optical design: 6 elements in 4 groups, a folded path of **17.4 mm inside 3.40 mm** | the drawing, the measured modulation transfer, the CRA under **25 degrees**, and gate CAM-3 |
| CAM-2 | the sensor: the readout, the binning, the HDR merge, metering | the power, the time and the quality of Section 6.3.1 |
| CAM-3 | the sentinel: the remosaic, the compression, the focus stack, the metadata | the payload sizes of Section 6.3.2, and no invented result |
| CAM-4 | the four latency classes | the times on a near and a far validator |
| CAM-5 | the lease and the sensor declaration: the four refusals, the state on the panel, the record | all six behave as specified |
| CAM-6 | the heat of Section 6.4, the duty rules, the cell temperature | the curve, the derate, a cell inside its limit |
| CAM-7 | the magnets: the pen dock, the attach of the part, the stabilisers | the monogram holds with the pen attached, and the part holds to R 60 mm in the wrap |
| CAM-8 | a new module on an old leaf, and a lost module revoked | both behave as specified, the record updated |
| CAM-9 | the local provisional pipeline against the validator reference: a day shot, a night shot, a document | the local image is at least 60 percent of the reference, and it is labelled |

**The extended camera and rear round panel gates (`CAM-14` to `CAM-20`) of revision L.4.1**. They are defined in `HARDWARE/05-B-MODULE-CAMERA-AND-PANEL.md` alongside `MON-1` to `MON-20` of `HARDWARE/20-THE-BUDLUM-MONOGRAM.md`. `[DECISION]`

| Gate | The test | The pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| CAM-14 | the front ring camera assembly: mass and flush fit at `z = 1.60..4.00 mm` | **21.0 g** total B module mass, and front sapphire window flush within **0.03 mm** |
| CAM-15 | the B module link: `5 Gbit/s` wired on top-edge pads and `400 Mbit/s` wireless off-device | no dropped frame in 30 minutes, and no loss of pairing |
| CAM-16 | the 2-axis piezo OIS and autofocus stage inside the `Ø24.0 mm` ring bore | `±1.5°` OIS stroke (`3.5 EV` stabilization) and `12 cm` to infinity focus in **15 ms** |
| CAM-17 | the rear round AMOLED panel (`Ø22.4 mm` active, `220 x 220 px`, `250 ppi`, `1-60 Hz`) | `600 nits` peak, `<20 ms` selfie viewfinder latency, and `8 mW` at `1 Hz` clock |
| CAM-18 | the folded optics: 8 elements in 4 groups on the folded `3.4 + 14.0 mm` path | modulation transfer `>45%` at `120 lp/mm` center, CRA under **24 degrees**, focus at `0.12 m` |
| CAM-19 | the in-ring acoustics and flash: `2 x MEMS` mics (`68 dB` SNR), speaker (`78 dB SPL`), dual-tone LED | all sit inside the `Ø24.0 mm` ring bezel, sealed to **IP68**, zero parts in the concave valley |
| CAM-20 | the waterproof bore seals: front sapphire and rear polycarbonate O-rings | passes **IP68** (`1.5 m`, `30 min`) and `1.5 m` drop on 6 faces |

# BUDLUM LEAF · THE DIRECTIVE · PART 7 · THE CLEAR FAMILY, THE FRAME AND THE FIELD

---

## 7.1 The requirement law

1. **The user sees the world with the user's own eyes**. A Budlum Leaf adds light; it does not replace the world with a camera image. `[DECISION]`
2. A video pass-through view is refused as a base mode. A camera copy adds delay and limits resolution, colour and dynamic range.
3. **The see-through layer never blocks the world:** the transmission of the clear part is at least 45 percent. `[DECISION]`
4. **(the answer of Ayaz, question 4)** The see-through capability is **not a property of the glasses**. It is a property of the whole family, and every member can use it.

---

## 7.2 The Clear Leaf panel

| The property | The value |
|---|---|
| The transmission of the clear area | 60 to 80 percent |
| The transmission of the whole panel, pixels included | **45 to 70 percent** |
| The brightness towards the eye | 300 to 800 nits |
| The pixel aperture | 30 to 50 percent of the area |
| The substrate | a polymer film, or a thin glass where the part does not bend |
| The density | the same 508 ppi as the P80 and the P160 |
| The thickness | 0.35 mm |
| The lifetime | 10 000 to 20 000 h, at a lower duty cycle than a surface panel |

---

## 7.3 The four uses of one capability

| The use | What it is | The number |
|---|---|---|
| **the Leaf Frame lens** | a clear panel bonded to a prescription lens; the world light passes through, the panel emits towards the eye | field is 60 to 90 degrees (`pixels across / field across`); Frame uses a `2 400 ppi` 4K microdisplay for `60 ppd` (Part 15, H-1) |
| **the Leaf Window** | a carried member with two clear panels held like a pane of glass to overlay marks, words, or labels on the world | the overlay is a text and a marker class; the alignment is coarse without a camera and fine with one |
| **a surface over glass** | the same panel on a window, a glass door, a shop front, a machine window, a car glass or a museum case | the information is added, the world stays behind it |
| **the dimmer** | an electrochromic layer that drops the transmission to 20 to 35 percent on demand | a family part on every clear panel, for contrast in the sun and for a calm mode |

**The order of the clear products (the answer of Ayaz, question 9): the Frame lens is first**. `[DECISION]`

---

## 7.4 The daylight proof, and the honest limit

```
L_eye = L_world × T + L_display
```

| The scene | The world at the eye through a 60 percent panel | The display must add | The verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| an office, 300 cd/m² | 180 cd/m² | 180 to 400 cd/m² | easy |
| a cloudy street, 2 000 cd/m² | 1 200 cd/m² | 1 200 to 2 500 cd/m² | at the limit of the clear panel |
| a sunlit street, 10 000 cd/m² | 6 000 cd/m² | 6 000 to 15 000 cd/m² | not reachable without the dimmer |
| a sunlit street, the dimmer at 25 percent | 2 500 cd/m² | 2 500 to 5 000 cd/m² | reachable for a text class overlay |

**The conclusion, written as a claim rule**. Without a dimmer, a clear panel is an indoor and cloudy-day part. With the dimmer, it carries a text-class overlay in direct sun. A full-colour image in direct sun requires the waveguide variant, and that limit is written into the record. `[DECISION]`

---

## 7.5 The Frame

| The property | The value |
|---|---|
| The class | a Leaf with a hub of class H1, one radio, one secure element, **no camera in the base model** |
| The mass | 30 to 40 g, the front below 17 g |
| The energy | a 2 to 3 Wh cell in the temple: 4 to 8 h with the display on |
| The power | 0.4 to 1.2 W with the display on |
| The charge | **a contact pair in the case, 5 to 10 W**. No coil, per decision R-1 |
| The prescription | the lens carries the prescription, or a clip in lens does; the range and the cost are in the record |
| The input | a temple touch strip, a gesture strip, a voice pick up, the wallet through the normal path |
| The autonomy | a prompt, a message, a map, a translation, a call and the wallet. A film and a game belong to a surface or to the Field mode |
| The privacy | the base model records nothing because it has no camera; an optional module carries a hardware light that no program can paint |

**The social rules**. Recording is refused in stated contexts, with a visible indicator and no hidden capture. No private-display claim exceeds the physics: a faint combiner reflection can be seen in a dark room. `[DECISION]`

---

## 7.6 The Field mode

### 7.6.1 What it is

A Leaf 160 or a Leaf 160² has two faces. A lens spacer with an interpupillary adjuster holds the device at the eyes, and each face serves one eye. The surface becomes a wide, bright stereo window of 85 to 100 degrees. `[DECISION]`

| The property | The value |
|---|---|
| The spacer | 28 to 45 g |
| The field of view | 85 to 100 degrees diagonal |
| The density | about 20 to 25 ppd per eye on a Leaf 160: enough for a scene, low for fine text (Part 15, H-1) |
| The latency target | below 45 ms of motion to photon through the session, the link and the panel |
| The thickness effect | **none**: the leaf stays 4.00 mm, the cassette is unchanged, and the spacer is an accessory |
| The isolation rule | one action enters, one action leaves, a program cannot place the user inside, and the world is one gesture away |

### 7.6.2 The angle table, and why a user wants it

| What the user looks at | The width | The distance | The angle |
|---|---|---|---|
| a Leaf 160, held in the hand | 160 mm | 300 mm | 30° |
| the Field mode | 160 mm per eye at the eye | 20 mm | 85 to 100° |
| a Leaf 1400 | 1 400 mm | 2 500 mm | 31° |
| a 100 inch screen | 2 214 mm | 2 500 mm | 48° |
| the Field angle, on a wall | 7 150 mm | 3 000 mm | 100°, which is a 320 inch image at three metres |

**The sentence that sells it**. The room surface spans 31 degrees at home, and the carried surface spans 30 degrees in the hand. The Field mode spans 100 degrees and fits the same pocket. `[DECISION]` (the answer of Ayaz, question 25: a cinema mode.)

### 7.6.3 The four reasons a person uses it

1. The size that no room allows: a hotel room, a seat, a bed, a small flat.
2. A private and a silent screen: one person watches, nobody else hears or sees.
3. The depth a flat surface cannot show: a stereo photograph, a stereo video, a model.
4. The same device, session and wallet: a film moves into Field mode without copying.

**What it is not**. Not a second world, not an avatar, not a room that replaces the room. There is no account to open and no wall to build.

### 7.6.4 The relation to the clear family

A clear face in the Field mode is refused by design. In Field mode the user chose the film, and a transparent face would show the room behind it. A clear face is the world plus a note; the Field mode is the note alone. `[DECISION]`

---

## 7.7 The clear family gates and the Field gates

| Gate | The test | The pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| CLR-1 | the clear panel: the transmission, the brightness, the aperture, the lifetime, the bend | the table of Section 7.2 |
| CLR-2 | the Frame optics: the field of view, the eye box, the interpupillary range on 30 users | the image is complete for every user; the field is at least 70 degrees |
| CLR-3 | the daylight case of Section 7.4, with and without the dimmer | a text class overlay in the sun, and the honest limit in the record |
| CLR-4 | the Frame mass and the balance | the stated mass, no pressure point after two hours |
| CLR-5 | the prescription, the drop and the scratch of a lens | the stated lifetime and the service route |
| CLR-6 | the Leaf Window alignment, with and without a camera | a coarse label holds; a fine label needs the lease |
| CLR-7 | the family rule: the same session, the same manifest and the same wallet on a clear surface | a session moves from a clear surface to an opaque one with no lost state |
| CLR-8 | the eye safety | the photobiological risk group, the luminance cap in a dark room, the rest prompt |
| CLR-9 | the privacy and the social rules | the indicator works, the refusal works, the claim list holds |
| CLR-10 | the surface over glass: the install, the clean, the removal | no damage to the glass, and the calibration holds after a re-install |
| FLD-1 | the spacer: the mass, the fit, the interpupillary range, the eye relief | the table of Section 7.6.1 |
| FLD-2 | the angle: the measured field per eye and the overlap | 85 to 100 degrees, a comfortable stereo overlap |
| FLD-3 | the latency: motion to photon | below 45 ms, with the class stated |
| FLD-4 | the density claim: a film, a photograph, a model, a text page | the claim holds, and a text page is stated as a poor case |
| FLD-5 | the move: a session from a flat surface to the Field and back | no lost state, no stuck frame, the sound follows |
| FLD-6 | the isolation rule: enter, leave, and a program that tries to hold the user | one action in, one action out, the refusal works |

---

# BUDLUM LEAF · THE DIRECTIVE · PART 8 · THE FIRMWARE, AND HOW IT IS BUILT

---

## 8.1 The shape

```
leaf/firmware/
  boot/       the signed image, the A and B slots, the safe screen
  link/       LF-1  the radio set, the path manager, the fail-safe ladder
  stream/     LF-2  the decoder, the panel and tile scheduler, the class set
  surface/    LF-4  the local scene renderer, the input, the labels, the manifest
  authority/  LF-3  the secure element, the session keys, the leases, the records
  tests/      the gate tests, one file per gate id
```

Six crates, one workspace, a fixed function target. **No allocator beyond a static pool, four tasks, no dynamic code loading**. `[DECISION]`

| The resource | The budget |
|---|---|
| The flash image | 4 MB, signed, two slots |
| The working memory | the static pools of Section 8.4, inside the 1 GB of the hub |
| The tasks | `link`, `stream`, `surface`, `authority`, fixed priorities |
| The safe screen | on the panel within 60 ms of power on |

---

## 8.2 The boot chain, step by step

| Step | The time target | What happens | The failure behaviour |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 20 ms | power on, the firmware signature is checked against the secure element | a bad signature boots the B slot; a bad B slot shows the recovery screen |
| 2 | 60 ms | the safe screen is on the panel, drawn by the firmware: the battery state, the wallet surface, the class line | it does not depend on the link, the anchor, the wallet being online or the cassette |
| 3 | 150 ms | the link comes up, the path is chosen, the trust anchor is compared with the stored anchor | a wrong anchor shows a stated state and offers a re-anchor through a second channel |
| 4 | 300 ms | the session is offered: the surface shows the last session or the surface menu | |
| 5 | | a missing cassette, a counterfeit part, an expired lease and a refused operator each produce a **stated visible state**, never a silent success | |

**The rule**. The safe screen is a hardware function of the firmware and the panel. A Leaf is never a brick. `[DECISION]`

---

## 8.3 LF-1 · the link layer

```text
PathCandidate { kind: Wifi|Cell|Mmwave|Satellite|Relay, rtt_ms: u16, up_mbit: u16,
                down_mbit: u16, energy_mw: u16, trusted: bool, operator: Option<OperatorId> }
PathPlan      { primary: PathCandidate, mirrors: [PathCandidate; 2], floor: LocalFloor }
```

**The selection rule, in code-shaped words**.

```text
fn choose(candidates) -> PathPlan:
    let trusted = candidates.filter(|c| c.trusted)
    let usable  = trusted.filter(|c| c.can_carry(class_of(session)))
    let best    = usable.min_by(|c| (c.rtt_ms, c.energy_mw))
    let mirrors = usable.filter(|c| c != best).take(2)
    PathPlan { primary: best, mirrors, floor: LocalFloor::safe_screen() }
```

**The energy policy** is a pure function of the state of charge, the temperature, the class and the user setting. **It never silently downgrades a class**: a change is a visible event on the surface.

| The state of charge | What happens |
|---|---|
| on charge | the challenge answers at the highest tier |
| above 60 percent | the normal tier |
| 20 to 60 percent | the reduced tier, and the session states it |
| below 20 percent | the essential tier: the wallet, the safe screen, the message |
| below 5 percent | the reserve: the safe screen and the optical path only |

---

## 8.4 LF-2 · the stream layer, and the memory proof

```text
FrameClass  = C0Safe | C0bModel | C1Delta | C2Video | C3Still | C4CameraUp
FrameHeader { session, seq: u64, class: FrameClass, bytes: u32, digest: [u8;32],
              operator, model: Option<ModelId>, effort: Option<EffortTier> }
```

```
one 4K frame, 10 bit, 4:2:0               = 12.4 MB
a codec holds 3 to 8 reference frames      = 37 to 100 MB
the decode context, the tile buffers       = 30 to 60 MB
an 8K reserve at four times the frame      = 240 MB
the floor for 4K with headroom             = 384 MB
the shipped value                          = 1 GB, at about 1.5 g and 0.35 W active
```

**The scheduler rules**. The decoder writes only changed regions and addresses the panel at the manifest refresh rate. It never exceeds the panel temperature policy. **A full frame write on a static page is a defect**. `[DECISION]`

**The class fallback chain**. The order is C4 → C3 → C2 → C1 → C0b → C0. A class that a path cannot carry is **refused at start**, never degraded in silence.

---

## 8.5 LF-3 · the authority layer

```text
Wallet ── signs ──▶ SessionGrant { scope, term, energy_policy, operators, classes }
SessionGrant ── derives ──▶ SessionKey { id, expires, scope }
SessionKey ── signs ──▶ the ordinary actions, one at a time

Lease { purpose, model, sensors[], classes[], expiry, retention, subject }

ComponentRecord { part_class, part_id, origin, measures, history, signatures, reference }
```

| The transaction class | The example | The path |
|---|---|---|
| local | the input echo, the cursor, the safe screen | the firmware, no signature |
| session | a message, a payment inside the grant | the session key, one signature over the intent |
| high value | a new operator, a new module, a part change, a revocation | the wallet, and a second authority when a module is present |

`[TREE]` ML-DSA-87 uses a 2 592 byte public key and a 4 627 byte signature. Therefore the device signs once per session and never once per tap.

**The set change rule**. A validator set change needs a new out-of-band anchor. The device stores one anchor (checkpoint height, block hash, validator set hash) and refuses network-only set changes. `[TREE]`

---

## 8.6 LF-4 · the surface layer

### 8.6.1 The local scene renderer

```text
SceneModel { version: u64, glyphs: GlyphCache, ops: [Op], palette: Palette,
             images: [ImageRef], signature: [u8;64] }
Op = Rect | Text | Path | Image | Blend
```

| The rule | The reason |
|---|---|
| the renderer draws only a signed model | no local computation is shown as a verified result |
| it cannot label a document, a lease or an inference | the labels belong to the panel layer |
| a model that fails its signature is refused, and the last good model stays on the surface | a silent blank is forbidden |
| it reports the drawn version to the session | the operator knows what the user sees |
| the parts: a glyph cache, a vector fill, an alpha blend, an image blit, a colour engine | about 2 mm² of logic and 2 MB of cache |

### 8.6.2 The reprojection unit

```text
Reprojection { frame: FrameHandle, motion: Motion, mode: Shift|Scale|Rotate, max_ms: 40 }
```

Rules. It runs only on the last decoded frame and stops when artefacts pass the threshold. It never runs across a scene cut, and the session marks the cuts.

### 8.6.3 The four labels of the panel layer

The evidence label of a mixed surface, the class line, the battery and energy state, and the camera state. **A page cannot cover a label**, and a page that tries is refused and recorded. `[DECISION]`

### 8.6.4 The input map

| The input | The path | The latency |
|---|---|---|
| touch and pen | the mesh layer, the hub, the local echo | 3 to 8 ms |
| proximity and gesture | the mesh and a ranging radio | 10 to 20 ms |
| gaze | a sensor under a lease, or the eye region in the Field mode | stated per case |
| voice | the module microphone array, masked on the device, then the intent | stated |
| the wallet | the secure element, one action | local |

---

## 8.7 The refusal set

| No. | The case | The behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | a model that is not the leased model | refuse, keep the last good model, record |
| R2 | a purpose mismatch | refuse, show the purpose, record |
| R3 | an expired lease | refuse, offer a renewal with one action |
| R4 | a sensor the lease does not name | refuse, the hardware light stays off |
| R5 | a part with a bad record | refuse the part, show the failing field |
| R6 | a path whose operator fails the trust test | never use, and say why on the class line |
| R7 | a class the path cannot carry | refuse at start, never degrade in silence |
| R8 | an unsigned or a badly signed scene model | refuse, keep the last good model, record |
| R9 | a pressure to read without a grant | refuse, with no override `[TREE]` |
| R10 | a page that tries to cover a label | refuse the cover, draw the label, record |

---

## 8.8 How the firmware is built: the work order

| Step | The package | The dependency | The gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | the workspace, the pool allocator, the four tasks, the record format | none | the build and the style checks |
| 2 | LF-3 the key chain against a bench secure element | 1 | SW-6 on a bench |
| 3 | LF-4 the scene renderer and the labels | 1 | DST-1, SW-11 on a bench |
| 4 | LF-2 the decoder and the scheduler against a recorded stream | 1 | SW-3 on a bench |
| 5 | LF-1 the path manager against a link emulator | 1, 2 | SW-1, SW-8, SW-9 |
| 6 | the boot chain, the A and B slots, the safe screen | 1 to 5 | the boot matrix, R1 to R5 |
| 7 | the camera path, the sentinel contract, the lease | 2, 4 | CAM-2 to CAM-5 |
| 8 | the mode and the manifest, the Field mode | 3, 4 | DSP-9, FLD-5, FLD-6 |

**The coding rules**. One work package, one branch, the test written with the code, no merge without a gate. A surface counts as existing only when it is wired from the module root and covered by a gate. The `src/sdk/` precedent is the standing warning. `[DECISION]`

---

## 8.9 The firmware gates

| Gate | The test | The pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| SW-1 | the Leaf node profile in a device class emulator | answers inside the challenge period and follows the battery table |
| SW-2 | the finality budget on the secure element | inside the stated time, energy and storage |
| SW-3 | the frame classes over an impaired link | every class inside its budget, an undecodable class refused at start |
| SW-4 | a lease, its four refusals, a revocation | all six behave as specified, with the times measured |
| SW-5 | a handoff, clean and unplanned | no stuck screen, no lost state, the token rules hold |
| SW-6 | the signature budget and the wallet path | the bytes and the times match the table, per action |

---

# BUDLUM LEAF · THE DIRECTIVE · PART 9 · THE NETWORK AND THE BUDLUM INTEGRATION

---

## 9.1 The split of work

| Where | What runs | The artefact |
|---|---|---|
| **On the Leaf** | the Leaf Firmware: the link, the stream, the wallet and the surface | `leaf/firmware/`, a fixed function image, no user code |
| **On the validator** | Budlum OS and the session host: the page engine, the model, the game, the encoder | the Budlum tree as it stands `[TREE]` |
| **In B.U.D**. | the user data, the session state, the manifests, the records | `bud/`, Pollen, the storage module `[TREE]` |
| **In the wallet authority** | the authorization, the session key chain, the leases | `crates/wallet-core`, `src/account_abstraction/` `[TREE]` |

---

## 9.2 The Budlum tree, as the program reads it

| The artefact | What it gives the Leaf | The state |
|---|---|---|
| `src/network/mobile.rs` | a phone node profile: battery aware challenge policy, NAT traversal, self-hosted serving, stateless sync | the Leaf extends it, it does not replace it |
| `src/light_client/mod.rs` | one trust anchor, a finality certificate, a post-quantum blob, a sync plan | the device is a light client with a wallet |
| `crates/wallet-core` | BIP39, ML-DSA-87, the seed rule, the wallet and relayer separation | the authority layer |
| `crates/note-packing` | one definition of a note field element | called, never re-derived |
| `src/relayer/` | the signed intent, the solver bid, the policy envelope, the settlement status | the reachability path |
| `src/gateway/passport.rs`, `atlas` | the identity surface, the evidence cards, the Pollen summary | the identity and evidence layer |
| `crates/budscan` | the browser with the classification rule and the four fetchers | the window of the device |
| `src/pollen/` | the consent rule, the content gate, the strict refusal | the read path |
| `src/account_abstraction/threshold_mldsa.rs` | a t of n multisig authorization | the high value path |
| `src/ai_inference/effort.rs` | the effort tiers | the promise about hardware |
| `bud/` | the lossless container | the archive |
| The constitution | the six invariants | the boundary of every Leaf change |

**The invariants that no Leaf change bends**. `[TREE]` No read without a Pollen grant; no governance override; no administrator whitelist; island isolation; no private key custody; no plaintext on an evidence surface.

---

## 9.3 The session, and its objects

| The object | The content | Who signs | Where it lives |
|---|---|---|---|
| The session | id, the user authority, the class set, the decoder profile, the effort tier, the energy policy, the deadline, the counter | the wallet authority | B.U.D. under the user authority; the device holds a hash |
| The binding | the operator, the term, the notice period | the operator and the user | B.U.D. |
| The resume token | single use, short lived, one named operator | the user authority | the wire only |
| The lease | the purpose, the model, the sensors, the classes, the expiry, the retention | the user authority | B.U.D., the operator holds a reference |
| The job | the deadline class, the checkpoint, the mobility rule | the session key | B.U.D. |
| The record | the part, the measures, the history, the signatures | the part and the wallet | B.U.D. |

**The flow of one delta frame**.

```
input            -> the device encrypts and sends the intent, duplicated on two paths when the link is unstable
the operator     -> renders the delta against the acknowledged state, with a monotonic sequence number
the wire         -> the delta frames at class C1, with the session id and the class
the device       -> the hub decodes, the scheduler writes the changed region, the panel shows it
the local floor  -> the echo of the touch, the cursor and the wallet never wait for the network
```

**The operator choice**. Trust comes first, latency second, and cost third (cost is deferred; units are effort-tier milliseconds and class bytes). A validator within 200 km keeps the round trip under 10 ms; an edge node 10 to 15 km away delivers 1 to 5 ms. `[PUBLICATION]`

---

## 9.4 The wallet, the keys and the signature budget

1. The wallet signs once per session, not once per tap.
2. The chain runs: wallet → session grant → scoped session key → ordinary actions.
3. A t of n authorization is t separate verifications, one by one. `[TREE]`
4. A Leaf with a module and a battery sheet holds three authorities. A high-value action requires two of them.
5. The relayer carries an intent and holds no key. Neither side ever signs for the other.
6. The optical path carries a signed receipt with no radio. The note packing crate is **called, never re-derived**.
7. A lost module is revoked with one wallet action, and the network sees it inside one challenge period.

---

## 9.5 What the AI can do

1. **Lubot is the family assistant**. It reads and answers, and it never acts without a grant. `[TREE]`
2. **The device masks first**. A secret is masked on the device before a question leaves it.
3. **The camera works only under a lease**, and the sensor state is a hardware light.
4. **The effort tier is a hardware promise**. A request above the advertised tier is refused, and the device shows the refusal.
5. **Every read is recorded and every read costs**; the record is a B.U.D. object under the user authority, readable by the user at any time.
6. **The four refusals are a feature**. They cover model mismatch, purpose mismatch, expired lease, and unnamed sensor.

---

## 9.6 The research orders RG-1 to RG-17, with the acceptance test of each

| No. | The order | The acceptance test |
|---|---|---|
| RG-1 | the Leaf node profile beside Mobile Self, with a battery and challenge table and the joule cost of an answer | the profile answers inside the challenge period at every battery state, and the energy per answer is measured |
| RG-2 | the finality budget on two candidate secure elements: the BLS aggregate and the post-quantum blob | the time, the energy and the storage are measured, and the out-of-band anchor rule is written |
| RG-3 | the display transport: C0 safe, C1 delta, C2 video, C3 still, plus the new C0b model class | each class is measured on an impaired link, and a class is refused at start when the path cannot carry it |
| RG-4 | the session object, the single use resume token, the grace period and the failure set | a clean and an unplanned handoff both pass, with no stuck frame |
| RG-5 | the perception lease: the object, a short default, one action revocation, the declaration trail, four fail-closed cases | all six cases behave as specified, and the hardware sensor state is proven |
| RG-6 | the component record fields: class, identity, origin, measures, history, signatures, reference | a part is accepted, refused and revoked, each with the field that decided |
| RG-7 | the transport byte budget per action: a payment, a message, a lease, an inference request | the bytes are measured, and the optical frame count is computed from them |
| RG-8 | the wallet to session grant to session key chain, with one action revocation | the chain is proven on a bench, and a revocation takes one action |
| RG-9 | the reverse battery policy: the class follows the cell state, never a silent downgrade | the five states of Section 8.3 are proven over 24 hours |
| RG-10 | the relayer reachability and the fee plan behind a carrier address translation | an intent settles, and the path is in the record |
| RG-11 | can the device serve storage honestly, and what the shard rule is | the measurement, and the honest answer either way |
| RG-12 | the optical transfer must call the note packing crate | the packing is called, and a second implementation is refused at review |
| RG-13 | the part anchor and the lost module revocation | one action revokes, and the network sees it inside one challenge period |
| RG-14 | the Budscan render target: the split, the verifier as a light client, the page class, the visible class statement | a page renders at the class it declares, and the verifier is the light client |
| RG-15 | the telemetry schema: six groups, under the Pollen rules | a granted read succeeds, an ungranted read is refused and logged |
| RG-16 | the evidence label drawn by the panel, which a page cannot cover | the cover attempt fails, and the label renders on every page class |
| RG-17 | one term one meaning, and the stub rule | the term list is enforced, and a stub surface is refused at review |

---

## 9.7 The delegation boundary

| The device keeps | The network takes |
|---|---|
| the panel scan out and the timing controller | the page engine and the layout |
| the hardware decoder | the heavy compute and the models |
| the secure element and the wallet authority | the consensus and the settlement |
| the radios | the storage and the shard duty |
| the safe screen and the local floor | the consent path, through Pollen |
| the local scene renderer and the reprojection | the browser engine, through Budscan |
| the manifest and the labels | the identity and the evidence surface |

**The rule**. A duty that crosses the line is a defect, and the review says which line it crossed. `[DECISION]`

---

## 9.8 The network gates

| Gate | The test | The pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| SW-7 | a component record and a part refusal | the device refuses a mismatched part and shows the reason |
| SW-8 | the reverse battery policy over 24 hours | the class follows the state in all five cases |
| SW-9 | a challenge answer on charge and off charge | the energy in joules, and the earned credit |
| SW-10 | a relay path behind a carrier address translation, with a path change | the intent settles and the path is in the record |
| SW-11 | a page with mixed evidence, and a page that covers a label | the labels render, the cover attempt fails |
| SW-12 | an optical receipt with no radio | the reader verifies the receipt; the frame count and the time are in the record |
| SW-13 | a telemetry read with and without a grant | the granted read succeeds, the ungranted read is refused and logged |

# BUDLUM LEAF · THE DIRECTIVE · PART 10 · THE DISTANCE DOCTRINE

**The order that created this part**. *Everything runs on the validator, and the far validator case must be solved so distance is not a problem*. `[DECISION]`

---

## 10.1 The two-budget rule

| The budget | What it covers | The time | Where it is served | Does the distance matter |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **The interaction budget** | the input echo, the cursor, the drag, the typing, the menu, the wallet, the gaze pointer | under 10 ms | **inside the device** | **no** |
| **The processing budget** | the finished photograph, the re-encode, the full layout, the answer of a model, the search | 1 s to 60 s | **anywhere on the network** | **no** |

**The rule**. A function is judged against the budget that the human sense uses. The eye tracks motion, so the interaction budget is hard. Nobody watches a photograph being developed, so the processing budget is soft. **Putting a photograph on the interaction budget was the error, not the validator**. `[DECISION]`

---

## 10.2 The five mechanisms that make distance irrelevant

| No. | The mechanism | What it removes from the network path | The remaining latency | The gate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **the local floor** | the input echo, the cursor, the wallet approval, the safe screen | zero | SW-4, MEC-9 |
| 2 | **the local scene renderer** | text, the interface, lists, the keyboard, the buttons, the menus | zero | DST-1, DST-2 |
| 3 | **the reprojection unit** | the delay of a scroll, a pan, a zoom, a tilt | zero to 8 ms | DST-3 |
| 4 | **the near validator** | the near part of the session: the cache, the composition, the buffer, the relay | 1 to 5 ms, wherever the user is | DST-4, DST-5 |
| 5 | **the honest class ladder** | nothing: it states where the fidelity falls, visibly | stated | DST-6 |

### 10.2.1 The local scene renderer, in detail

The network does not send pixels for an interface. It sends a **document model**: a signed, versioned display list of text runs, shapes, image references, colours and a difference against the acknowledged version. The hub renders it locally at 144 Hz.

| The property | The value |
|---|---|
| The parts | a glyph cache, a vector fill, an alpha blend, an image blit, a colour engine |
| The silicon | about 2 mm² of fixed function logic in the hub, and 2 MB of cache |
| The power | 0.15 W at work, 0.02 W on a static page |
| A keystroke | 3 to 8 ms, measured locally, with no network in the path |
| A scroll | 4 to 10 ms locally for a text surface |
| The limit | a photograph and a video are not rendered locally; they arrive as a stream |

### 10.2.2 The reprojection unit, in detail

The hub holds the last decoded video frame and shifts, scales, or rotates it locally at up to 144 Hz. When the true frame arrives it replaces the projection. The latency that it hides is 10 to 40 ms. Artefacts appear at frame edges after a large move, so reprojection stops below the quality threshold. It never runs across a scene cut.

### 10.2.3 The near validator

| The property | The value |
|---|---|
| What it holds | the cache of static content, the document model, the video buffer, the input predictor, the relay path |
| What it does not hold | the user's data in the clear, a wallet key, a signing authority, the ability to alter a verified result |
| The power class | 3 to 15 W, a mini computer or a small board |
| Who runs one | **anyone, as a Budlum validator**: it registers, it earns and it stakes like any other. There is no separate near node system `[DECISION]` (the answers of Ayaz, questions 7 and 18) |
| The trust rule | the frames carry the hash of the staked validator, and the input transcript is checked; an agent that alters a frame is detected and excluded |
| The dock and the rail | they carry a mains path by default and a **validator board as an option** (the answer of Ayaz, question 32) |

---

## 10.3 The nine far-validator mechanisms

| No. | The mechanism | The number |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The split**: the device and the module deliver a complete image; the validator delivers the reference | the local provisional pipeline reaches 60 to 75 percent of the final quality |
| 2 | **Provisional first**: the local image is stored at once and labelled `local provisional`; the validator result replaces the view and the record keeps both | the user is never without a photograph |
| 3 | **A deadline class per job**: interactive still at 5 s, standard still at 60 s, or deferrable job waiting for a good link | stated before the shutter |
| 4 | **Job mobility**: a render job is checkpointed and can be picked up by a nearer validator or by the user's own later. The pipeline is deterministic and signed, so the result is identical | RG-4 |
| 5 | **The validator in the room**: the dock, the stand or the rail can carry a validator board | Volume 4-B, and the answer of Ayaz, question 32 |
| 6 | **Predictive prefetch and cache**: the working set lives near the user; the far validator computes only new work | the renderer and the agent |
| 7 | **Deferrable scheduling**: a job of the class "no hurry" rides the cheap path and the idle capacity | the night burst |
| 8 | **The optical and the no-link path**: a signed receipt or a small payload as optical frames | RG-12 |
| 9 | **The class statement per market**: no global latency figure; the measured class of each market, and every reduction visible | DST-5, DST-6 |

### 10.3.1 The answer in one paragraph

**The far validator is not a problem because the user never waits for it**. The user touches, and the device answers in a few milliseconds from a renderer inside the device. When the user takes a photograph, a local provisional image exists immediately at three-quarters quality. The finished validator image arrives later, as stated before the shutter. A live conversation with a large model uses a near validator and streams at the delta class. `[DECISION]`

---

## 10.4 The class ladder, with the numbers

| The case | The input to the eye | The text and the interface | The video | The 3D and the Field | What the surface states |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| a validator or a near node inside 15 km | 2 to 6 ms | local | 20 to 45 ms glass to glass | 20 to 45 ms | the full class |
| inside 200 km | 4 to 10 ms | local | 30 to 60 ms | 30 to 60 ms | the full class |
| inside 1 500 km | 6 to 14 ms | local | 40 to 90 ms, buffered | 60 to 120 ms, degraded | a note in the class line |
| across a continent, with a near node | 2 to 8 ms | local | 40 to 90 ms, smooth | 40 to 70 ms | the full class for the interaction, a video note |
| across a continent, no near node | 8 to 20 ms | local | 150 to 300 ms: a page and a message are usable, a motion is poor | not offered | the reduction |
| a satellite only | 25 to 60 ms | local | not offered | not offered | the message class |

`[PUBLICATION]` Light in fibre travels about 200 km per millisecond. A near validator 10 to 15 km away is 1 to 5 ms, and a central cloud is 50 to 100 ms. `[HYPOTHESIS]` The glass to glass columns, measured by gate DST-5 in two markets.

---

## 10.5 What must be built for this doctrine

| No. | The item | The owner | The gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | the local scene renderer and the reprojection unit in the hub | the firmware line | DST-1 to DST-3 |
| 2 | the local provisional camera pipeline in the module | the camera line | CAM-9 |
| 3 | the job object: a deadline class, a checkpoint, a mobility rule | the network line | RG-4 |
| 4 | the state words on the surface: `local`, `near`, `far`, `deferred` | the surface line | DST-6 |
| 5 | the market measurement in two cities | the verification line | DST-5 |

---

## 10.6 The distance gates

| Gate | The test | The pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| DST-1 | the local scene renderer against a reference engine: a text page, a table, a menu, in three languages | the difference is below the stated threshold, and a page cannot be faked as a verified result |
| DST-2 | the input to the eye time for a keystroke, a scroll and a tap | the values of Section 10.2.1, measured |
| DST-3 | the reprojection artefacts on a scroll, a pan, a zoom and a scene cut | the stated quality, and the automatic disable |
| DST-4 | a malicious near validator: an altered frame, a replayed frame, a forged class | each case is detected and the node is excluded |
| DST-5 | the glass to glass time in two markets: near, far with a near node, far without one | the table of Section 10.4 |
| DST-6 | the class line in each of the six cases | the user sees the class, and a reduction is visible |
| DST-7 | the dock as a near validator: a Leaf 160 at 1 m from the dock, the compute 8 000 km away | the interaction values of Section 10.4, and the video inside the stated band |
| DST-8 | the energy of the distance work: the renderer, the reprojection, the radio | each term measured, and the sum inside the accounting of Part 4 |

---

# BUDLUM LEAF · THE DIRECTIVE · PART 11 · THE HEAT DOCTRINE

> **Revision L.6.0 note (2026-10-12)**. `FINAL/21-stretch-stem-and-projection.md` supersedes this file where they differ: the local renderer is a fixed-function compositor in `U1` with four jobs. No program runs on the device. Part 21, Section 21.5.6.

**The order that created this part:** *look at what can be done so that it does not heat up.* `[DECISION]`

---

## 11.1 The numbers

| The surface | The area | A 4 W load | The rise without a spreader |
|---|---|---|---|
| the module face | 111 cm² | 0.036 W/cm² | 10 to 18 °C above ambient |
| the leaf, both faces | 256 cm² | 0.016 W/cm² | 3 to 6 °C above ambient |
| the leaf plus the module, coupled | 367 cm² | 0.011 W/cm² | 2 to 4 °C above ambient |

**The conclusion**. The leaf is an excellent radiator because it is large, thin and two-faced. **The engineering problem is not the total heat; it is the local hot spot in the module**. `[DECISION]`

---

## 11.2 The twelve mechanisms

| No. | The mechanism | The how | The number |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Keep the heavy compute off the module** | the computational photograph runs on the validator | a local compute block would need 5 to 15 W and would turn a 4 mm module into a 60 °C surface |
| 2 | **A spreader inside the module** | a 30 to 50 µm graphite or annealed copper layer under the sensors, bonded to the shell | gate MEC-11 measures the path |
| 3 | **A thermal bridge at the interface** | a soft thermal pad at the contact band between the module and the spine | a target below 3 K/W |
| 4 | **A latent buffer** | 1 to 4 g of an encapsulated phase change material in the shell | about 200 J per gram: a 4 W burst of 60 s is 240 J |
| 5 | **The cell away from the sensor block** | a gap band and a thermal wall inside the module; the cell temperature is in the record | the cell is not heated by the camera |
| 6 | **Sensor-side power reduction** | binning and line skipping in video, an in-sensor HDR merge, a lower readout rate where the class allows | about 0.15 W per camera in video against 0.25 W in a full readout |
| 7 | **The encode moves to the hub where the class allows** | the sentinel compresses lightly (3 to 6 to 1) and the hub does the full encode | the heat moves from 111 cm² to 256 cm²; the flex carries 2 to 4 Gbit/s |
| 8 | **Duty and rate rules** | a 4K120 take of up to 10 s with a 20 s cooldown, or a 15 minute cap with a derate to 4K60 | measured by gate MEC-12 |
| 9 | **A temperature aware capture policy** | the camera refuses a 4K120 start above a stated module temperature and says why | the same honesty rule as the uplink wait |
| 10 | **A dark frame recalibration on the sensor temperature** | the temperature is carried in the record | a hot sensor drifts, and the number is known |
| 11 | **Air and radiation rules** | no insulating layer over a tile or a panel; the module shell is matte and dark | a matte surface radiates better than a gloss |
| 12 | **The ambient rule** | the thermal test set runs at 35 °C ambient, in a hand, in the sun and in a bag | gate MEC-11 |

---

## 11.3 The thermal budget of a capture

| The case | The total | The time | The rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| the viewfinder | 0.45 W | continuous | no limit |
| one still, three cameras metered | 1.65 W | 0.5 s | no limit |
| a night burst of 20 frames | 2.85 W | 2 to 4 s | no limit; the uplink can continue after the capture, and the capture itself is short |
| a 4K60 video | 2.50 W | continuous | no limit inside a 35 °C ambient |
| a 4K120 video | 4.00 W | continuous | a 10 s take and a 20 s cooldown, or a 15 minute cap and a derate to 4K60, whichever the gate measures as safer |

**The surface states what it is doing**. `cooling`, `warm`, `capture limited` are three visible states, and each says the reason. `[DECISION]`

---

## 11.4 The charging heat

| The path | The input | The loss at 80 percent | The mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| wired, USB-C | 45 to 65 W | 9 to 13 W | the spreader of the cell field, and the derating curve |
| the dock and the rail, by contact | 25 W | **about 0.1 W**, and 0.31 W at a worn pad | gold pads on the fixed edge, spring pins in the dock |
| the wireless paths | **withdrawn** | n.a. | the order of Ayaz: no wireless charging. The 5 W that the induction link lost at 25 W stays out of the body |

**The derating curve**. Full power holds below 38 °C of the surface. Power derates linearly to 20 percent at 45 °C and stops above 48 °C with a message. `[DECISION]`

---

## 11.5 The thermal gates

| Gate | The test | The pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| MEC-11 | the module path: the spreader, the interface pad, the latent buffer, at 35 °C ambient, five capture classes | the module rise, the leaf rise and the cell temperature inside their limits |
| MEC-12 | the capture policy: a hot start, a long take, a cooldown, the message | the refusal works and the user sees the reason |
| MEC-6 | the charge heat at 10, 25, 45, 60 and 80 W, seated and removed | the derating curve and a surface inside the safety limit |
| CAM-6 | the camera thermal set, the duty rules, the cell temperature | the curve, the derate, a cell inside its limit |

---

# BUDLUM LEAF · THE DIRECTIVE · PART 12 · THE VERIFICATION AND THE CLAIM POLICY

---

## 12.1 The five steps of a claim

| Step | What it is | The cost | What it proves | The exit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | the model and the simulation | the least | a number is plausible | a written model with its assumptions |
| 2 | the coupon | low | the material behaviour | a measured coupon set |
| 3 | the sub-assembly | medium | the pair works | a working cassette, module or panel pair |
| 4 | the prototype | high | the member works | a working Leaf 160 |
| 5 | the pilot | the most | the product works in a hand, in a city | a service network and a claim file |

**The rule**. A claim is published only at the step that supports it, and the step is named in the claim's record. "A result without data is an opinion." `[DECISION]`

---

## 12.2 The complete gate index

| The family | The gates | The subject |
|---|---|---|
| The structure, the mass, the heat | MEC-1 to MEC-13 | wrap/slip rule, mass, battery sheet, drop, spine, charge heat, seal, radio coupling, energy, pen, B module heat, capture policy, and prism edges (`B-1..10`) |
| The energy | ENR-1 to ENR-6 | the tile penalty, the cell samples, the gauge and the record, the abuse set, the slide cycles, the charge curve |
| The panel and the light | DSP-1 to DSP-10 | panel, addressing, seam, tile service, power model, daylight, variable refresh, bend, manifest, cut, and corner |
| The radio | RF-1 to RF-8 | antenna set, battery in the field, cable/dock desense, bent match, path manager, ladder, optical path, and satellite class |
| The camera | CAM-1 to CAM-9 | the optics, the sensors, the sentinel, the latency classes, the lease, the heat, the magnets, the renewal, the provisional pipeline |
| The clear family and the Frame | CLR-1 to CLR-10 | clear panel, Frame optics, daylight, mass, prescription, window alignment, family rule, eye safety, privacy, and surface over glass |
| The Field mode | FLD-1 to FLD-6 | the spacer, the angle, the latency, the density claim, the move, the isolation rule |
| The firmware | SW-1 to SW-6 | the node profile, the finality budget, the classes, the lease, the handoff, the signature budget |
| The network | SW-7 to SW-13 | the record and the refusal, the battery policy, the challenge energy, the relay path, the labels, the optical receipt, the telemetry |
| The distance | DST-1 to DST-8 | the renderer, the input time, the reprojection, the near validator, the market measurement, the class line, the dock, the energy |

**The count**. The history is 90 at this part's first writing, then 103, then 110, then 115. **At Revision L.5.0 the count comes from `tools/check_crossrefs.py` (gate PRG-15) against `tools/canon.json`**. Each gate has a written pass condition, an instrument and a raw data rule. The verification line owns the register. `[DECISION]`

---

## 12.3 The raw data rule and the record format

1. **One file name rule**. `GATE-ID__member__serial__date__instrument.csv`.
2. **One raw data format**. Use a plain text table with units in the header and a calibration comment block.
3. **One instrument per quantity**, with a calibration date inside its validity window.
4. **One record per gate in B.U.D.**. Store it under program authority, readable by the device owner.
5. **The negative results are kept**. A gate that fails keeps its data, and the failure is in the register with a date and an owner. A gate that passes keeps the data that proves it.

**The record fields of a gate**.

```text
GateRecord { gate_id, member, serial, date, instrument, calibration_date,
             conditions, raw_pointer, result, margin, owner, decision }
```

---

## 12.4 The laboratories and the rigs

| The asset | What it measures | The class |
|---|---|---|
| the bend rig | a cycle at a radius, with an image, a capacity and a touch report | a servo rig with an optical window |
| the coupon station | the stack strain, the adhesive creep, the gel behaviour | a universal test machine with a climate chamber |
| the cassette rig | 1 000 cycles, the contact resistance, the latch force | a custom rig |
| the wired and contact charge bench | the USB-C path at 40 and 25 W, the contact pads at 25 W, the pad loss at 0, 20 000 and 50 000 mates | a calibrated bench with a mating machine |
| the antenna range | the pattern, the efficiency, the desense, the bent match | an accredited range |
| the display metrology set | the brightness, the colour volume, the uniformity, the seam, the refresh | a calibrated photometer and a camera |
| the cell cycler and the abuse cell | the capacity, the cycle life, the abuse set | an accredited laboratory |
| the link impairment bench | the latency, the loss, the jitter, the class behaviour | a network emulator |
| the secure element bench | the boot, the signature timing, the energy, the anchor | a bench with a current probe |
| the mass and the thickness metrology | the gram, the micron, the flatness | a calibrated balance and a micrometer stage |
| the camera bench | the modulation transfer, the power, the heat, the magnets | an optical bench and a thermal camera |

---

## 12.5 The claim policy

### 12.5.1 The three tiers of a claim

| The tier | The support | The wording |
|---|---|---|
| **Proven** | a gate with raw data on a prototype or a pilot | a number, with the member and the configuration |
| **Designed** | a model with a written assumption and a named gate | "the design target is X, and gate Y measures it" |
| **Studied** | a study with a source and a date | "the study found X, and the case is stated" |

### 12.5.2 The forbidden words and phrases

Forbidden claim terms: *all day*, *unbreakable*, *zero latency*, *no hardware*, *VR ready*, *metaverse*, and *a private display*. Forbidden device-class words: *phone*, *tablet*, *television*, *monitor*, *laptop*, *computer*, and any unverified superlative. `[DECISION]`

### 12.5.3 The claim rules that follow from the physics

| The claim | The rule |
|---|---|
| the thickness | always names the part: the leaf 4.00 mm, the module 4.00 mm, the attached stack 8.00 mm |
| the mass | always names the cassette and the module state |
| the runtime | always names the member, the cassette and the session class |
| the sharpness | always states the angular density at the intended distance |
| the link | never a global latency figure; the measured class of the market |
| the camera in the sun | the honest limit of Part 7, Section 7.4 |
| the clear panel | the transmission number, and the dimmer state |

---

## 12.6 The verification gates of the program itself

| Gate | The test | The pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| PRG-1 | the number consistency: every quantity appears once per volume | no contradiction across the directive |
| PRG-2 | the tag check | no number without a tag, no claim without a gate |
| PRG-3 | the name check | no forbidden word in a claim, a file name or an identifier |
| PRG-4 | the drawing check | an overlap, an overflow, a covered text, a stale footer: all zero |
| PRG-5 | the plan check | no block without an owner, no gate without a data route |
| PRG-6 | the claim check | every public sentence maps to a gate or to a study |

## 13.1 The phases

| Phase | The length | The work | The exit |
|---|---|---|---|
| **PG-0 Prove** | 30 days | the partner short list, the samples of a panel and a clear panel, the bench of the anchor verification, the bend coupon | every open item has a decision number, and the first measurements exist |
| **PG-1 Material** | 3 months | the coupon set: the cover and the coat, the adhesive, the barrier, the cell tile, the spine, the gel | MEC-1, MEC-2 and DSP-1 pass on coupons |
| **PG-2 Panel** | 5 months | P80 and P160 panels with driver, seal, and 144 Hz rate; cut trial with `R 3.20 mm` corner and `r 0.80 mm` edges; tile seam | DSP-2, DSP-3, DSP-4, DSP-10 pass |
| **PG-3 Leaf A** | 6 months | the first Leaf 160: the cassette, the spine, the weld, the hub, the firmware on a bench | MEC-3 to MEC-9 pass on a working member |
| **PG-4 Link** | 4 months, in parallel | the node profile, the session, the handoff, the lease, the record set, the anchor | SW-1 to SW-9 pass |
| **PG-5 Surface** | 5 months, in parallel | the local renderer, the reprojection, the labels, the manifest, the mode change | SW-10 to SW-13, DSP-9, DST-1 to DST-3 pass |
| **PG-6 Module and Frame** | 8 months, in parallel | the three folded cameras, the module with the cell and the pen dock, the clear lens and the Frame | CAM-1 to CAM-9, CLR-1 to CLR-9 pass |

| **PG-8 Pilot** | 6 months | the pilot of 100 units, one service workshop, the claim file, the Turkish certification | the first sale |

**The critical path**. The panel and the battery sheet. Everything else runs in parallel because each written interface can be built against a mock. `[DECISION]`
