# BUDLUM LEAF · THE DIRECTIVE · PART 17 · THE ENERGY ARCHITECTURE REVIEW

**The question of Ayaz that created this part**. *The compute, the storage and the operating system are all on the network, at the validators. If the device holds none of them, does a Budlum Leaf need a battery this large, and does it need a sandwich at all? Can the cell live inside the panel itself, in the polymer, and can the middle disappear? And a second question: two ways to sell. One is a subscription that pays for what the validators spend. The other is a hardware unit that is bought separately, with the screen in the hand and the hardware elsewhere.* `[DECISION]`

**The standing of this part**. It is a **review with a recommendation**, not a settled decision. Every number below is `[HYPOTHESIS]` until its gate runs. The recommendation is D-8, and it is Ayaz's to take.

---

## 17.1 The question is right, and the answer is a smaller cell, not no cell

The premise is correct: the Leaf holds no processor, no operating system and no archive. That removes **the compute** from the energy bill, and compute was the largest term in the surface of 2026. A carried surface of today spends 2 to 5 W on its own silicon. A Leaf spends 0.70 W on a fixed function hub. The energy bill that remains is three things that cannot be moved to a validator:

| The term | Why it cannot move | The power |
|---|---|---|
| **the light** | the panel is the product; the photons are made in the hand | 0.60 to 1.10 W |
| **the hub** | the decoder, the local scene renderer and the reprojection unit are inside the device by definition of Part 10 | 0.70 to 1.20 W |
| **the radio** | the link is the product; the bytes cross the air here | 0.40 to 0.60 W |
| the authority and the housekeeping | the wallet must answer with no network | 0.12 W |

**The bill**. A delta session is **2.02 W** and a video session **3.27 W** (Part 4, Section 4.6). A working day of eight hours is therefore **16 to 26 Wh**. No part of it can move off the device, because it is light, local rendering and radio.

**And it cannot be harvested**. Indoor light is 10 to 100 µW/cm². Over the 256 cm² of a Leaf 160 that is 2.5 to 25 mW, against a 2 000 mW need: **80 to 800 times short**. `[PUBLICATION]` for the indoor light band, `[GENERAL]` for the conclusion. A surface that lives on ambient light is a scientific instrument, not a product.

**So the conclusion of Section 17.1 is: a cell must exist**. But the question remains: *must it be thick, and must it be a sandwich?* Sections 17.2 to 17.7 answer that. The answer is no on both counts. `[DECISION]`

---

## 17.2 The geometry of energy

Energy is a volume, and the volume has only two factors:

```
E = area × thickness × the energy per litre        (110 cm² available inside the body)
1 Wh of a 700 Wh/L cell = 1.4 cm³ of volume = 110 cm² × 0.13 mm
```

| The truth | The number |
|---|---|
| One hour of a delta session | 2.02 Wh = **0.10 mm** of a full area cell layer |
| One working day, eight hours | 16 Wh = **0.81 mm** of a full area layer |
| Today's Feather cassette | 20 Wh = 22.2 cm³, in a 2.88 mm bay |
| The same 20 Wh as a full area layer | **1.82 mm**, with no shell, no latch, no contacts cage and no bay |

**The insight of this section**. A cell that is **spread over the whole area** uses the area that the device already has. It needs no packaging of its own, because **the device is the packaging**. That is where the sandwich loses. The sandwich built a second box inside the body. That box had walls, a lid, a latch and an air gap. `[DECISION]`

---

## 17.3 The 4-Quadrant (`+`) 2.00 mm (`68 g`) Solid-State Screen-Battery Body, Universal `Qi2.2`, and `90°`-Bent-Stem B Module (`20 g`): the Revision L.5.0 architecture

**Definition**. In Revision L.5.0, the bulky external bottom battery pack is cancelled. The `2.00 mm` (`68 g`) body splits across `+` into **four `17 g` quadrants (`Q1..Q4`)** (`HARDWARE/11`, `HARDWARE/17`). Each quadrant integrates a `3.0 Wh` (`800 mAh`) `LLZO/PEO` solid-state cell (`12.0 Wh` total) and `Qi2.2` wireless charging (`0` USB-C on body). All other functions (`U1` Master TCON, `U2` `UWB / mmWave` SiP, camera, audio, antenna, and `USB Type-C`) live in the **`90°` B module (`20 g`)**.

| The property | The value at Revision L.5.0 |
|---|---|
| The 4-Quadrant (`+`) body | `80.0 x 160.0 x 2.00 mm` (`4 x 17.0 g` = **`68.0 g`**; zero USB-C and zero buttons on body) |
| The display & `<15 µm` `+` seam | **dual flicker-free `T-OLED` + `PDLC` privacy film + `AgNW/ITO` + `<15 µm` (`n = 1.585`) self-healing optical `+` seam (`R < 0.0001%`)** |
| Internal solid-state battery | **`4 x 3.0 Wh = 12.0 Wh` (`3 200 mAh`, `750 Wh/L`) non-flammable `LLZO/PEO` cells (`0%` liquid, `0.0 ppm` HF, `>14.1 h` at `0.85 W`)** |
| Eyewear & ocular safety | **`<=36.2 °C` hardware NTC cut-off**, **`ISO 10993-5/-10`** medical-grade BPA-free PC, `<0.08 µT` bifilar ELF, **`SAR = 0.054 W/kg`** |
| Universal wireless charging | **Central `Ø54.0 mm` 4-sector `Qi2.2` MPP (`15-25 W`)** + **`4 x Ø22.0 mm` (`5-10 W`) local `Qi` coils** (charges whole body or 1 tile) |
| Tool-free quadrant hot-swap | **`(0,0)` Ti hub + `8` Ti pins (`<2.5 µm` step) + `24` N52H (`11.2 N`) + `64` gold leaves** (`<3 s` swap, `EU 2023/1542`) |
| `90°`-bent-stem B module (`20.0 g`) | **only the stem (`8 x 32 mm`) bends `90°`** on 4 edges; **`Ø32 mm` ring lies flat on screen (`+Z / -Z`)** |
| Sole `USB-C` & Zero-CPU core | **sole `USB Type-C` (`40 W`)** in the `90°` B stem; **`U1` Master TCON + `U2` `UWB / mmWave` SiP** in the flat B ring |

**Why the middle is the right place, and not a compromise**. In a closed stack that bends, the strain is zero at the mid-plane. So the middle of the body is the **best** mechanical place for the least flexible layer. There the sheet bends as little as any layer can while the body bends. The two faces shield it from the cover strain, the impact and the light. The earlier cassette also sat in the middle, but it sat in a **shell** with a latch, an air gap and a fixed 2.88 mm. The shell is what this part removes, and not the middle. `[GENERAL]`

**The fills, and what each one buys**. The energy density is a **range, not a number**, and the range depends on the tile thickness. A thin cell loses density because the foil, the separator and the tab take a larger part of a small volume. `[PUBLICATION]` Three numbers set the band. A bendable cell on sale gives **under 200 Wh/L** at a 25 mm radius. A research textile cell gives **450 Wh/L** at a 1 mm radius. A 2024 flexible cell reached **374 Wh/L** with 40 000 bends. A rigid cell of 2 mm and more reaches **700 to 950 Wh/L**. The audit F-01 is accepted: **900 Wh/L is the upper limit of the study and not the base case**.

| The tile thickness | A useable density band | The base case of this directive | The note |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.60 mm and a flexible film | the band of the family, 600 to 720 Wh/L at 2.2 g/cm³ | **600 Wh/L**, the bottom of the band | a thin flexible cell, or thin rigid tiles |
| 1.00 mm | the same band | **650 Wh/L** | the transition band |
| 1.82 mm | the same band | **700 Wh/L** | a rigid tile with a low tile penalty, and the Sheet 182 of Part 4 |
| 2.28 mm | the same band | **720 Wh/L**, the top of the band | the cassette class |

| The fill | The sheet | The volume | The nameplate, the base case | Effective | A delta session | The body | The device mass | The range of the delta session |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **4-Quadrant `+` Solid-State (Chosen L.5.0)** | **`4 x 1.08 mm` `LLZO/PEO` (`750 Wh/L`)** | **11.3 cm³ (`4 x 2.83 mL`)** | **`12.0 Wh` (`3 200 mAh`)** | **12.0 Wh** | **>14.1 h (`0.85 W`)** | **2.00 mm** | **68 g (`4 x 17 g`)** (`88 g` w/ B) | **12.0 to 16.0 h** |
| **Sheet 60** (legacy study) | 0.60 mm | 6.6 cm³ | **3.5 Wh** | **3.1 Wh** | **1.5 h** | 1.62 mm | **21 g** | 0.4 to 1.5 h |
| **Sheet 100** (legacy study) | 1.00 mm | 11.0 cm³ | **6.4 Wh** | **5.6 Wh** | **2.8 h** | 2.02 mm | **30 g** | 0.8 to 2.7 h |
| **Slim Chin 400** (4.00 mm H-frame) | 4.00 mm | 5.2 cm³ | **4.8 Wh** | **4.5 Wh** | **5.3 h** | 4.00 mm | **46 g** | 5.3 h at 0.85 W |
| **Sheet 228** | 2.28 mm | 25.1 cm³ | **16.1 Wh** | **14.2 Wh** | **7.0 h** | 3.30 mm | **57 g** | 4.5 to 9.0 h |
| the cassette, for reference | a 2.88 mm bay | 22.2 cm³ | 20 Wh | 16 Wh | 7.9 h | 3.90 mm | 78 g | 4.5 to 9.0 h |

**The comparisons that stay true after the correction**.

1. Sheet 228 delivers **14.2 Wh** against the cassette's 16 Wh. It saves **0.60 mm** of thickness with no shell, latch or bay. The earlier sentence "13 percent more" is **withdrawn** because it depended on the 900 Wh/L base case (F-01).
2. Sheet 100 is **1.9 mm thinner than today**. It carries a quarter of a working day at the base case.
3. Bare is **1.2 mm and 20 g**, the thinnest member. The module or the hub supplies its energy.
4. **The thin body buys thickness, not energy**. The energy of a thin sheet is small, and the claim says so. `[HYPOTHESIS]` gates ENR-7, ENR-8, ENR-21 and ENR-2, which samples 600, 650, 700 and 720 Wh/L at 2.2 g/cm³.

**What the module adds, after revision L.3**. **Nothing, and that is deliberate**. The 20 Wh cell of the module is **struck** (decision R-11): the module is a door for ports, and it carries no energy. The energy parts of a Leaf are the **sheet** and the **cassette**; a Bare Leaf on a module still runs on power. This paragraph replaces the whole of the earlier claim.rgy part that is bought once and renewed**.

---

## 17.4 What the sandwich was for, and what it gives up

| What the sandwich gave | Is it still needed | The answer if not |
|---|---|---|
| the cell out of the bending stack | **no** | the sheet sits at the neutral axis, which is the best place for it |
| a removable cell | **yes, and by law** | Section 17.6: the slip sheet keeps the removability with no shell and no latch |
| a thick, cheap cell | **mostly no** | the fills of Section 17.3 cover 0.6 to 2.28 mm |
| the middle structure of the body | **no** | the spine carries the load, and the weld closes the perimeter |
| the ability to swap capacity on the road | **yes, by the sheet** | the user swaps the energy sheet, or attaches the cassette on the backup line: capacity is a sheet choice |
| a hard stop for the wrap | **no** | the wrap zone carries no cell tile, and its gaps open to 1.20 mm while the mosaic stays in the sheet |
| heat spreading from the middle | **no, it is better** | a full area sheet of 110 cm² spreads heat better than a 22 cm³ block |

**The four things the sandwich cost, and which the sheet returns**. A shell, a lid, a latch and a set of wiped contacts. That is about 9 g and about 1.0 mm of the body. Those four parts are exactly the parts that the design had to justify in the first place. `[DECISION]`

---

## 17.5 The three bodies of the family, and the death of the fixed 4 mm

| The body | The build | The thickness | The mass | The energy | The sentence that sells it |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Bare** | two faces welded at the perimeter, the spine, no cell | 1.02 to 1.20 mm | about 20 g | on power and on the module | *"a surface, not a device"* |
| **Sheet** | the same body plus the energy sheet of the chosen fill | 1.62 to 3.30 mm | **21 to 57 g** | **3.5 to 16.1 Wh at the base case**, and the band moves with the cell partner | *"the thickness you choose is the energy you choose"* |
| **Sandwich** | the cassette bay of Parts 3 and 4 | 3.90 to 4.00 mm | 78 to 92 g | 20 to 25 Wh | the reference envelope, and the fallback if the slip sheet of Section 17.6 fails its gate |

**The rule of this part**. **4.00 mm is the maximum envelope, and it is not the design target** (the audit F-09). The product states the body thickness fixed by the order, alongside the mass and runtime. `[DECISION]`

---

## 17.6 The legal route A: the slip sheet (the recommendation)

**The design**. The sheet is a replaceable part. It slides out through a 0.4 mm lip at the fixed edge, on a 50 µm carrier with a pull tab. The contacts sit in the edge band.

| The property | The value |
|---|---|
| The removal | one straight motion, no tool, target below 30 s |
| The cycles | 300 cycles at a 0.35 mm lip, with the seal tested at each 50 |
| The seal | the first variant, superseded: a doubled polymer lip with a silicone bead, and a class of IP54 to IP56. **This variant is not sold as water resistant**. Route A+ replaces it with the gasketed carrier and the class of gate MEC-19 |
| The compliance | **the user replaces the part that stores the energy**, which is the substance of the European removability duty |
| The thickness cost | about 0.06 mm for the lip and the carrier tolerance |
| The refusal | a sheet whose record fails is refused with the failing field on the surface, as any part is |

`[HYPOTHESIS]` gate ENR-8 proves the cycles, the seal and the record. This route is recommended because it keeps the European answer that the program already chose, and because it costs 0.06 mm.

---

## 17.7 The legal route B: the sealed sheet (the honest analysis)

**What Ayaz asked**. The cell lives in the polymer, and the device is charged without opening it. **The engineering side of that is real:** a sealed sheet is thinner by the lip, stiffer in the seal and cheaper.

| The question | The honest answer |
|---|---|
| Is a sealed cell legal in Europe from 18 February 2027? | **not by default**. Article 11 of EU 2023/1542 puts the removability and replaceability duty on the end user for portable batteries. The derogations exist and they are narrow, and a display surface does not obviously sit in one |
| Does the fact that the *real* energy source is the validator change it? | **no**. The law looks at the cell that is in the product, not at where the compute runs |
| Is there an argument in the data integrity clause? | there is an argument to test with a lawyer: the wallet authority and the session state depend on continuous power. It is an argument, not a settled position |
| The cost of the argument | a legal opinion, a market-by-market reading, and the risk of a redesign at the worst moment |
| The alternative | **route A**, which gives the same design in 0.06 mm more, with no argument |

**The rule that this part proposes**. Build route A. Keep route B as a **market variant** released only with a written legal opinion after the sheet passes its gate. `[DECISION]` pending D-8.

**And the "charge once" idea, answered honestly**. A cell is spent by the light it makes. A Leaf cannot be charged once and live forever because photons cost 0.6 to 1.1 W per hour. What **can** be built is the feeling Ayaz is describing:

1. **the hub of Section 17.9**, so the surface rests on the hub and stays full;
2. **the Bare tier**, so a user who lives near power never thinks about energy at all;
3. **the module**, so a swap is one action of ten seconds and the capacity travels with the camera.

---

## 17.8 The glasses, and why the clear lens cannot hide a cell

| The case | The answer |
|---|---|
| the Frame | a 2 to 3 Wh cell in the temple, as Part 7 says. **The temple is the right place**, because it is the only part of a pair of glasses with volume outside the optical path |
| the Clear Leaf Window and the surface over glass | a cell behind the clear panel is allowed **outside the optical path**: in the frame band, rim, or opaque margin |
| a transparent cell inside the lens | **refused as a plan**. A transparent electrochemical cell is a laboratory object with a low capacity and a short life. A cell in the lens would also block the world, and the clear family never does that |
| the honest statement | the clear family carries its energy in the parts that the eye does not look through, and the claim says so |

`[PUBLICATION]` for the state of transparent cell research, `[DECISION]` for the refusal.

---

## 17.9 The hardware unit: the Leaf Hub, and the two ways to buy

**The split that Ayaz described is the correct architecture of the product line**. The energy follows the compute. **The energy for the light stays in the hand, and the energy for the compute goes where the compute is**.

| The product | What it is | What it holds |
|---|---|---|
| **Leaf Screen** | the surface: the panel pair, the spine, the hub, the radio, the secure element and an Energy Sheet of the chosen fill | the light, the link, the wallet |
| **Leaf Hub** | a separate hardware unit that the user or an operator buys: a stand, a puck or a pack | **a large cell (40 to 100 Wh), the ports, the sound set, the near validator services of Part 10, and optionally the Budlum validator board** |
| **Leaf Module** | the rail module of Part 6, and the monogram beside it | the ports, the sound set and the pen dock, and **no cell**; the camera is the monogram of Part 20 |

**What the hub does for the surface**.

| The function | The number |
|---|---|
| the charge | **a contact pair in the stand**, so the surface is full when it rests. No coil, per decision R-1 |
| the power | the surface on the hub draws from the hub and spends nothing of its own sheet |
| the near services | the cache, the document model composition, the video buffer and the relay at 1 to 5 ms (Part 10) |
| the validator duty | the board is optional, and a household that carries it earns for the near work and stakes like any validator |
| the ports | USB-C, a network port, an audio output |

**The two ways to buy, and both are honest**.

| The buyer | What the buyer takes | What the buyer pays |
|---|---|---|
| **the user** | the Leaf Screen, optionally a hub, optionally a module | the hardware, and a **pay as you go** meter for the network work |
| **the operator** | a Leaf Hub with a validator board, and one or more surfaces | the hardware, and the operator earns for the store, the near services and the answers |

**The pay as you go meter, in structure**.

| The meter | The unit | The visibility |
|---|---|---|
| the interactive class | milliseconds of the effort tier | the surface states it |
| the standard class | milliseconds of the effort tier | the same |
| the deferred class | milliseconds at the idle price | the same |
| the bytes | the class of the transfer | the same |
| the near services | the hub earns what it serves, and a household hub pays for itself over a stated period | the hub states its own earnings |

**The rule**. The surface never spends another party's compute without a stated price. The price structure is written here while the numbers stay deferred by Ayaz. `[DECISION]`

---

## 17.10 What must be measured before this part becomes a claim

| Gate | The test | The pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| **ENR-7** | the sheet: the energy per litre and per gram, the 0.60, 1.00, 1.82 and 2.28 mm samples, the 0.8 tile and bridge factor, the carrier | every fill inside 10 percent of the table of Section 17.3 |
| **ENR-8** | the slip sheet: 300 edge cycles, the carrier seal, the record and the refusal | under 30 s, the class of gate MEC-19 holds after the cycles, and the record is complete |
| **MEC-15** | the thin body: the stiffness, the drop of a 1.62 mm and a 2.02 mm body, the weld at the land | a working surface after the set of gate MEC-4 |
| **MEC-16** | the sheet at the neutral axis: the bend at R 40 and R 25, the bridge strain, 10 000 and 100 000 cycles | no capacity loss beyond 5 percent, no open tile |
| **MEC-17** | the charge heat of a sheet between two faces: 30, 45 and 65 W, seated and in the hand | the derating curve of Part 4, Section 4.5 |
| **MEC-18** | the mass of a Bare body and of a Sheet 100 body, part by part | inside 10 percent of Section 17.3 |
| **PRG-13** | the legal reading of route A and route B in Turkey and in the European Union | a written opinion per route, and the route that ships is the one the opinion supports. **Renamed from PRG-12 in revision L**, so that PRG-12 is the writing check of Part 19, Section 19.4 |
| **MEC-19** | the seal: the bare body, the sealed sheet, the plain slip lip and the **gasketed slip carrier** of route A+ | the class of each, measured with a module fitted and with none, and the class claimed is the class measured |
| **MEC-20** | the wrap of Part 18 and `HARDWARE/12`: `5000` wraps at `R 25.5 mm`, `50000` bends at `R 70 mm`, decoupled slip, and flat recovery | every gate of B-1 to B-6 passes, the edges meet with no tool, and the surface returns flat within 0.15 mm |
| **MEC-21** | the no crease scan: a luminance scan with the surface flat and at an angle, before and after the wraps | no line above 2 percent of luminance, and the claim is published with the scan |
| **MEC-22** | the gasket: 1 000 slip cycles of the sheet and 1 000 hours of compression at 45 °C | the seal force stays inside its window, and the IP class of gate MEC-19 holds |
| **MEC-23** | the bend guard: the mechanical limit, the strain mesh and the bend log | the limit stops the user at the proven radius, and the service can read the log |
| **MEC-24** | the vent: a gas passing, water holding membrane, with one forced cell failure | no weld opens, no panel lifts, and the IP class holds after the event |
| **ENR-9** | the swelling of a silicon carbon cell at 500 cycles, the pressure it puts on the panel, and the short test after the wraps | the swelling stays inside the compliance budget, and no short appears after 5 000 wraps |
| **DSP-11** | the shared substrate panel: two independent arrays on one carrier, the thickness saved, the cross talk and the reliability | at least 0.20 mm saved with no cross talk and no life penalty, or the study is closed |

---

## 17.11 The sealing doctrine: because Ayaz requires water resistance

**The requirement (the answer of Ayaz, question 37)**. *The body must be water resistant*. The audit of the seal against the new architecture gives one clear result. It also gives one tension.

| The architecture | The openings | The reachable class | The cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Bare**, no cell | the USB-C receptacle, the module contacts, the pen dock | **IP68**, with a sealed receptacle, a gasketed module interface and a sealed pad door | the class is a design task, not a claim |
| **Sheet, sealed (route B)** | the same, and no cell opening at all | **IP68** | it needs the legal opinion of Section 17.7 |
| **Sheet, slip lip (route A as first written)** | the same, plus a 0.4 mm lip at one edge | **IP54 to IP56** | the lip is a path for water and dust |
| **Sheet, gasketed slip carrier (route A+)** | the same, plus a lip with a **two bead gasket on the carrier itself** | **IP67, IP68 as a target** | about 0.1 mm instead of 0.06 mm, and the carrier becomes a sealing part |

**The finding**. A slide out part and a water tight body are not in conflict by nature. The usual conflict comes from a **bay with a lid**. The energy sheet has no bay: it is a laminated carrier that plugs its own slot. **The carrier is the seal**. That is route A+, and it is the recommendation of this section. One part answers the removability duty and the water requirement at once. `[DECISION]` pending gate MEC-19.

**The three sealing rules of the family**.

1. A port is sealed by its own gasket and a door that has a state, as the module interface does.
2. A removable part seals with a dual-lip gasket on the part itself. Worn seals are replaced with the part.
3. **No claim exceeds the class measured by gate MEC-19**. The class is stated with the module state.

---

## 17.12 The double sided panel, examined (the answer of Ayaz, question 37)

**The question**. *Would a double sided emissive panel not minimise the thickness, with the cell in its middle?*

**The examination**. Three different products are often called a double sided panel, and only one of them is usable here. `[GENERAL]`

| The class | What it is | The thickness it saves | The verdict for a Leaf |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A · shared substrate, two independent arrays** | one polymer substrate and one carrier, two full thin film transistor arrays and two emissive stacks, one on each side | about 0.20 to 0.30 mm: one substrate, one backing and one adhesive layer leave the stack | **usable**, and worth asking the panel partner for, because the two faces keep independent content. Added as gate DSP-11 |
| **B · one array, one image, emitted both ways** | a single pixel matrix that emits from both faces, so both sides show **the same image** | about 0.45 to 0.55 mm | **refused**. A Leaf shows different content on the two faces: one faces the user, the other faces away. The same image both ways is also a privacy defect: the screen would be readable from behind |
| **C · a transparent panel** | the world passes through and the image is emitted towards one side | it does not save thickness | it is the clear family of Part 7, a different product |

**The correction to the thickness hope**. Class A is real and it saves about 0.2 to 0.3 mm. A Sheet 182 body can reach about 2.55 mm, and a Bare body about 0.95 mm. It does **not** change the energy geometry and it does **not** remove the cell: the cell still needs its 0.6 to 2.28 mm. **The thinness comes from the integrated sheet, and a shared substrate is a bonus on top of it**. `[DECISION]`

**And the middle stands**. A class A panel laminates the cell between the two arrays. The cell then sits at the mid-plane of the stack, which is the neutral axis. That is the same place this part already puts it. Ayaz's picture is the correct picture; what changes against the earlier draft is only the shell.

---

## 17.13 The honest hours: what the numbers of Section 17.3 assume

**The doubt of Ayaz, question 39**. *Are those hours right? There is Wi-Fi, the screen is on: does the battery really last that long, and what does a Leaf need?*

**The honest answer**. The hours are correct **for a stated brightness and a stated content load**. They are wrong as a single number, because a panel is paid for the photons it makes. The model:

```
P_display follows luminance × the average picture level
the anchor measured in gate DSP-5: 0.60 W at 250 nits and a 35 percent average picture level
```

| The case | The luminance and the load | The display | The delta session | Sheet 100 | Sheet 182 | Sheet 228 | The video session | Sheet 228 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| a dark room | 150 nits, 30 percent | 0.31 W | 1.73 W | 4.6 h | 8.3 h | **10.5 h** | 2.74 W | 6.6 h |
| **indoors, mixed** | 250 nits, 35 percent | 0.60 W | **2.02 W** | 3.9 h | 7.1 h | **9.0 h** | 3.27 W | 5.5 h |
| a bright room | 500 nits, 50 percent | 1.71 W | 3.13 W | 2.5 h | 4.6 h | 5.8 h | 4.38 W | 4.1 h |
| outdoors, a bright page | 800 nits, 70 percent | 3.84 W | 5.26 W | 1.5 h | 2.7 h | 3.4 h | 7.04 W | 2.6 h |

**What a Leaf needs for these hours: the answer to the second half of the question**.

| The item | The target | Why |
|---|---|---|
| the panel at a **250 nit** working band with a variable refresh to 1 Hz | 0.60 W at a 35 percent load | the light is the largest term, and the refresh of a static page costs 60 percent of it `[HYPOTHESIS]` |
| **a Wi-Fi association that sleeps**: target wake time and multi-link power save | 0.05 to 0.15 W associated, 0.40 W in a delta session | an associated radio that never sleeps is the classic drain of a thin client |
| **the fixed function hub capped at 0.70 W** | the decoder, the local renderer and the reprojection | the voice of the chip is the price of the local floor of Part 10 |
| **the class line honesty** | the session class is visible, so a heavy session is a user decision | a user who knows the cost spends less |
| the sheet as the store | the user's sheet choice, one action | capacity travels with the sheet, and the module carries none |

**The claim rule that follows**. A runtime claim names the member, the fill, the **luminance**, the **average picture level**, the session class and the Wi-Fi state. A single number without them is refused by the claim policy. `[DECISION]`

---

## 17.14 The settlement: the wallet pays (the answer of Ayaz, question 41)

**The answer**. The network cost is settled **from the wallet**, inside the session grant. The grant carries the energy policy and the spending policy. The meter of Part 15, H-15 reports to it. There is no separate subscription product, no invoice and no second account. `[DECISION]`

| The element | The rule |
|---|---|
| The meter | milliseconds of the effort tier and bytes of the class, visible on the surface |
| The settlement | the wallet's session grant, one signature per session, not per tap (Part 9, Section 9.4) |
| The cap | the user sets a cap per session, per day and per month in the grant |
| The stop | at the cap the session stops **at a class boundary**, never in the middle of a frame |
| The near validator | a household validator earns for the near work, and its earnings and its costs are in the same wallet |
| The numbers | the price amounts stay deferred by the order of Ayaz |

---

## 17.15 The Leaf Hub is deferred, and the option stays open (the answer of Ayaz, question 40)

| The element | The decision |
|---|---|
| The hub as a product | **deferred**: it is studied, not committed |
| The dock and the rail today | the charge, the stand and the rail, with the validator board as an option |
| What stays in the design | the interface of Section 17.9: a hub can be added later without a change to the device. The device already draws from the dock when it rests on it |
| What opens the decision later | the market measurement of gate DST-5 and the earnings of a household board, measured on a pilot |

---

## 17.16 The decisions of this review (the answers of Ayaz, questions 37 to 42)

| No. | The question | The answer | The standing |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q37 | where the cell lives, and the sandwich | the cell is the **laminated middle layer at the neutral axis**; the old rigid shell is retired, and the body must be **water resistant** | Section 17.11, and the replacement of the shell by the carrier |
| Q38 | the European route | **route A, the removable sheet, in every market** | Section 17.6, upgraded to route A+ by the gasket of Section 17.11 |
| Q39 | the hours | the table of Section 17.13, with the luminance and the load named in every claim | `[HYPOTHESIS]` until gate DSP-5 |
| Q40 | the hub | deferred, the option open | Section 17.15 |
| Q41 | the payment | settled **from the wallet**, inside the session grant | Section 17.14 |
| Q42 | the module and the cassette | **the module carries no cell**; the sheet is the energy part and the cassette stays on the backup line | Section 17.3 |

---

## 17.17 The recommendation to Ayaz

1. **Adopt the Energy Sheet as the energy architecture of the family**. It gives a thinner, lighter body with fewer parts and a better thermal path. It removes the shell, latch and bay.
2. **Keep the European removability answer with route A, the slip sheet**, at a cost of 0.06 mm. Do not argue with Article 11 when a 0.06 mm lip settles it.
3. **Keep the cassette as the reference envelope and fallback**. No partner conversation or gate re-opens the body geometry.
4. **Make Bare a real tier**. A 1.2 mm, 20 g surface is unmatched for a user who lives near power.
5. **Build the Leaf Hub as a product**. Energy follows compute, near services need a stand, and hub operators also earn.
6. **Hold the sentence until the gates run**. Gates ENR-7 and MEC-16 decide the numbers, and the numbers decide the claim.

**The one sentence of this part, once the gates run**. *The compute left the device, and the energy did not have to leave with it. The cell it still needs is spread so thin that the device is no longer a sandwich. It is a surface with a day inside it.* `[HYPOTHESIS]`

---

# BUDLUM LEAF · THE DIRECTIVE · PART 18 · THE SCREEN COST, THE BEND AND THE SEAL

**The questions of Ayaz that created this part**. *The screen makes the picture. Does the validator not cover the cost of the screen working? Explain this at length.* And: *the flexibility test. Even if the screen bends edge to edge, does it survive without breaking, without damage, without the inside falling apart? That is the test for the carried member. The other members have no bend to the half.* And: *the body must be water resistant.* `[DECISION]`

---

## 18.1 The question, stated exactly

**The question of Ayaz**. The validators carry the processor, the storage and the operating system. The network is paid from the wallet. Then why does the device still carry an energy cost for the screen? Must the validator not pay that cost too?

**The short answer**. The validator covers **three of the five terms** of a session. It can never cover the fourth, because of where the light is made. And there is a fifth term, the *shape* of the content, that the network can cut by almost half. The program has never counted it. That last point is the real answer to the question, and it is worth a whole section. `[DECISION]`

---

## 18.2 The ledger of five terms: who can reduce what

| The term | The delta session | Who reduces it | The mechanism | The floor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1 · the photons** | 0.60 W | **the device alone, and by design** | lower luminance, a darker interface, a lower average picture level, a better emitter | the light that the eye needs at the chosen comfort |
| **2 · the hub work** | 0.70 W | **the network, in part** | a pre-composited, pre-scaled, pre-tone-mapped frame leaves the hub at the decoder floor; the local floor still runs | the local floor of Part 10 cannot leave the device |
| **3 · the radio** | 0.40 W | **the network, in full** | the class: C0b at 2 to 200 kbit/s against C2 video at 10 to 30 Mbit/s, plus the cache and the delta | the bytes that the user's action needs |
| **4 · the addressing** | 0.20 W | **the network, in part** | the refresh policy: a static page at 1 Hz instead of 144 Hz | the refresh that the content needs |
| **5 · the authority and the housekeeping** | 0.12 W | **nobody** | the wallet must answer with no network | fixed |
| **the sum** | **2.02 W** | | | |

**The reading**. Three of the five terms are **the network's business** (2, 3 and 4: 1.30 W of the 2.02 W). One is **the device's own** (1: the photons), and one is fixed. `[DECISION]`

---

## 18.3 Why the photons can never come from the network

**This is thermodynamics, not engineering taste**. A lumen is a photon that has to arrive at a retina. The network can move information at the speed of light, but it cannot move light to a place where the light is not made. The panel is the transducer. **The energy that the eye receives must be emitted within the eye's field, by a surface that the eye is looking at**. A validator 8 000 km away cannot send one photon into a room in Istanbul. `[GENERAL]`

The physical floor, stated as a number:

```
a comfortable page at 250 nits over 110 cm² at a 35 percent picture level
      = 0.60 W at the panel, at a 2026 emitter efficacy
the same page at 150 nits and 30 percent
      = 0.31 W
the same page at 800 nits for the sun
      = 3.84 W
```

**The judgement**. The absolute floor of the fourth term is **the light that the user's own eye needs**, and the entire industry is bound to it. What is *not* fixed is how much light the design asks for. `[DECISION]`

---

## 18.4 What the network can do about the screen: four mechanisms with numbers

This is the part of the answer that had never been written. It is a **round trip between the device and the network**. The device reports the energy, and the network is built to spend less of it.

| No. | The mechanism | The saving on a delta session | How it is done |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **the photon-honest interface** | **0.60 W → 0.31 W** | network-side interfaces are dark-first: true black background, low average picture level, no large white fields, and high-contrast text at low luminance |
| 2 | **the delta transport and the cache** | **0.40 W → 0.10 W** | interfaces arrive as signed `C0b` display lists (`2 to 200 kbit/s`), cached locally so repeats use zero traffic; video streams only on demand |
| 3 | **the pre-composited frame** | **0.70 W → 0.55 W** | the validator sends pre-composed, scaled, tone-mapped frames so the hub stays at its decoder floor while Part 10 keeps local interaction instant |
| 4 | **the refresh policy** | **0.20 W → 0.02 W** | the address term follows the manifest's refresh range: 1 Hz for a static page, 144 Hz only for motion. The network marks the regions that move, so the scheduler writes less |
| | **the sum** | **2.02 W → about 0.98 W** | **the network covers about half of the cost of the surface working** |

**And the third term of the ledger is not free money**. The network side pays for mechanisms 1 to 4 in engineering. Mechanisms 2 to 4 are **work on the validator**, which the wallet already pays for. Mechanism 1 is **work on the interface system**, which costs a designer's time and nothing per hour. So the sentence is now true in the way Ayaz meant it:

> **The validator covers about half of the cost of the screen working. It does not pay joules. It shapes what the screen has to do**. `[HYPOTHESIS]` gates DST-9 and DST-10 measure both sides of the round trip.

---

## 18.5 The two currencies, and who pays which

| The currency | Where it is spent | Who pays |
|---|---|---|
| **joules** | in the hand: the photons, the hub, the radio and the addressing | the user, from the energy sheet, the module or the hub (Part 17) |
| **meters** | on the network: the effort tier of an answer, the bytes of a class, the storage of a session | the user, **from the wallet**, inside the session grant (Part 17, Section 17.14) |

**Why the two cannot be merged**. A payment can move value across a network. It cannot move energy into a place where no one is generating it. A validator payment cannot create photons in a dark room, so the device keeps a cell even when compute is free. `[DECISION]`

**And the honest consequence for the sales pitch**. A Leaf does not sell "an unlimited screen". It sells **a screen whose work the network compresses**, and a body whose thickness is chosen by the user. The class line, the energy meter and the fill table are the three honest surfaces of that claim. `[DECISION]`

---

## 18.6 The photon-honest interface rule, and the energy meter

| The rule | The content |
|---|---|
| **the photon budget** | every Leaf interface declares an energy budget per screen minute (`mWh`) as a required design input |
| **the meter on the surface** | the device shows the energy per screen minute and per session, in the same place where it shows the class |
| **the dark-first default** | a true black background is the default; a large white field is a deliberate choice that the interface declares |
| **the static rule** | a page that does not change runs at the lowest refresh of the manifest, and the interface must not force a repaint |
| **the review rule** | an interface that exceeds its declared budget is refused by the same review that refuses a page that hides its class |
| **the claim rule** | a runtime claim always names the luminance, the average picture level and the interface budget, exactly as Part 17, Section 17.13 requires |

**The ownership**. the surface line and the network line, gates DST-9 and DST-10.

---

## 18.7 The continuous bend: the answer to the flexibility question

**The requirement of Ayaz**. *Even if the screen bends edge to edge, does it survive without breaking, without damage and without the inside coming apart? That is the test for the carried member. The other members have no bend to the half.* `[DECISION]`

**The correction of Ayaz, 2026-10-06**. *"I did not say there would be a strip that folds. I said the screen flexes. What is done here is not two screens folded onto each other. It has to be flexible enough to place the two ends onto each other"*. The book model of revision K is therefore retired, and this section is rewritten. Hardware file `HARDWARE/12-THE-BEND-LAW.md` carries the full arithmetic and the ten gates. `[DECISION]`

### 18.7.1 The bend, in numbers

| The quantity | The value |
|---|---|
| **the bend** | the surface bends over its **whole area**, and the two long edges can be brought together |
| **the hinge** | **none**. This product has no hinge, no crease line and no closed state |
| **the wrap circle** | the 160 mm long side closes into a **360 degree arc at R 25.5 mm**, which is 160 divided by two pi |
| **the scroll** | with the 3.54 mm stack the scroll measures about **59 mm outside and 47 mm inside** |
| **the gentle bend** | **R 70 mm is free and unlimited**, and it is the shape of a hand held session |
| **the pocket curve** | an arc at R 120 mm holds the gap between the long edges at **1.0 mm** |
| **the flat recovery** | the surface returns flat within **0.15 mm over 160 mm** |
| **the cycle targets** | **5 000 full wraps** (B-1) and **50 000 gentle bends** at R 70 mm (B-2) |
| **the part in the bend** | a seated part holds to **R 60 mm**, and a tighter curl releases it with no damage (B-10) |
| **the crease** | none. The curvature is spread over 160 gaps and ten wrap columns |

**Why the two edges can meet**. In Revision L.5.0, each `17 g` quadrant uses `G = 0.08 MPa` silicone OCA slip layers and a neutral-axis `LLZO/PEO` core (`HARDWARE/12`). The `2.00 mm` (`68.0 g`) 4-Quadrant (`+`) sheet bends to `R 25.5 mm` at `0.38 percent` outer polycarbonate strain (`0.22%` `LLZO` strain). `[DECISION]`

### 18.7.2 The layers in the bend, layer by layer

**The rule**. The layers are not bonded into one beam. A bonded 3.54 mm stack at R 25.5 mm puts 6.9 percent on its outer fibre, and nothing in this program survives that. Each layer bends about its **own** thin axis instead, and every interface shears by design.

| The layer | Its own bending thickness | The strain at R 25.5 mm | Its limit | The reading |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the cover film | 50 µm | **0.10 percent** | 3 percent | colourless polyimide, comfortable |
| the touch layer | 30 µm | 0.06 percent | 3 percent | on cell, silver mesh |
| the panel tile | 320 µm | **0.63 percent** | 1.0 percent at the barrier | the barrier sits near the tile axis |
| the structural adhesive | 50 µm | 0.10 percent | 50 percent | low modulus film, and it creeps by design |
| the heat spreader | 100 µm | 0.20 percent | 1 percent | graphite, in plane only |
| the steel foil of the field | 150 µm | 0.29 percent, and **0 percent inside a tile** | 1 percent | the foil tiles stay rigid |
| the cell tiles | 2 840 µm | **0 percent inside a tile** | 0 percent | the curvature lives in the gaps |
| the carrier film | 25 µm | 0.05 percent | 3 percent | it follows the tiles |
| the copper bus | 18 µm | 0.04 percent | 0.2 percent | rolled annealed copper, a serpentine at each gap |
| the sealant bead | the edge | stated per joint | its own elongation | a flexible bead, never a rigid weld |

**The slip rule, which answers F-03**. Every interface carries a low-modulus shear layer (`0.4 to 0.8 MPa`) with **0.35 to 0.6 mm of allowed slip at R 25.5 mm**. The audit asked for the slip of two free faces. The two faces move against each other by under a millimetre because each face bends about its own axis. Gates **B-5** and **B-6** measure the gaps and the shear. `[DECISION]`

**The three audit corrections, restated in the terms of this revision**.

1. **F-02, the strain rule**. Layer strain is `d / R`, where `d` is half of the layer thickness. Every row above uses its own thickness, and no row uses the 3.54 mm body.
2. **F-03, the crease stack**. There is no crease stack. The 9 mm book-model slip channel is withdrawn, and the decoupled slip rule takes its place.
3. **F-04, polycarbonate in the crease**. No crease exists, so polycarbonate is banned nowhere. Cover strain is 0.10 percent, twelve times below the 1.21 percent crazing point. The crease inserts are **withdrawn**, saving 0.02 g and a second material stream. `[DECISION]`

### 18.7.3 The test, and the honest claim

| The test | The method | The pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| **the full wrap** | the two long edges are brought together 5 000 times, at 23 °C and at 40 °C | the edges meet with no tool, no crack, no pixel loss, and the bus resistance inside 10 percent of new |
| **the gentle bend** | R 70 mm, 50 000 cycles | no crack, no set over 1 mm per 100 mm, no delamination |
| **the flat recovery** | a granite table after 5 000 wraps | **flat within 0.15 mm over 160 mm**, and the touch map unchanged |
| **the no crease scan** | a luminance scan across the surface before and after the wraps | **no line above 2 percent of luminance**, and no mark at any viewing angle |
| the honest sentence | the product says: **the screen curls, the two edges can meet, it holds the curve while you hold it, and it comes back flat. There is no crease, no hinge line and no closed state**. | the claim policy of Part 12 |

### 18.7.4 The strain budget of every layer

The values are limits for the cycle counts of Section 18.7.1. They carry the tag `[HYPOTHESIS]` until a coupon or a supplier confirms them. `[PUBLICATION]` marks the reported ranges.

| The layer | The allowed strain | The rule |
|---|---|---|
| the colourless polyimide cover | 3 percent | a flexible film class `[COMPANY]` |
| the polycarbonate cover, every face | 1 percent | the yield strain is higher, and crazing and fatigue set the cyclic limit |
| the polyimide backplane | 3 percent | keep the neutral axis at the transistor layer |
| the inorganic barrier | **0.3 percent** | single inorganic layers report a critical strain near 1 percent, and the safe design point is a third of it `[PUBLICATION]`. Keep the barrier within 10 µm of the tile axis |
| the touch layer | 3 percent | **no indium tin oxide**. Silver nanowire or a metal mesh |
| the hard coat | 3 percent, flexible hybrid type only | a rigid type is refused below R 25 mm |
| the copper bridge | 0.2 percent | rolled annealed copper in a serpentine shape, with the strain computed in the copper |
| the cell tile | 0 percent | a tile never bends |
| the adhesive | 50 percent | measure the creep and the relaxation over 1 000 hours at the radius |
| the sealant bead | a stated elongation | measure at R 25.5 mm after 10 000 cycles |

**The margin, in one line**. In Section 18.7.2, the highest layer strain is 0.88 percent in the electrode laminate. Every other layer sits well below its limit. The bend is a working state and not a stunt. `[DECISION]`

### 18.7.5 The test matrix of the bend

Every test uses at least **30 coupons** per condition, and the report gives the failure statistics. All numbers are `[HYPOTHESIS]` until the verification line confirms them.

| The test | The condition | The pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| static bend | 1 000 hours at R 25.5 mm, at 23 °C and 60 °C | no crack, no delamination, set under 2 mm per 100 mm |
| dynamic wrap | the 5 000 full wraps of B-1, at 23 °C and 40 °C | no broken line, pixel loss under the limit, capacity inside 5 percent |
| gentle cycles | R 70 mm, 50 000 cycles | no crack, no set over 1 mm per 100 mm |
| cold and hot wrap | R 25.5 mm at -10 °C and at 45 °C, 500 cycles each | the same pass as B-1 |
| reverse bend | the wrong direction, 1 000 cycles | no crack, and the guard stops the user before the limit |
| twist | a stated angle over 150 mm, 10 000 cycles | no crack, and the tiles unharmed |
| point load | a pen drop and a ball drop, wrapped and flat | no crack, no dead line |
| pinch | a stated force on a wrap column | no crack, no short |
| abrasion then bend | steel wool or sand, then 10 000 gentle bends | no coat crack that grows |
| chemical and strain | the seven chemicals of Section 18.7.6 at 1 percent strain for 24 hours | no craze under polarised light |
| thermal shock | -20 °C to 60 °C, 500 cycles, flat and wrapped | no delamination, and the seal intact |
| seal after the bend | the IP test after the wrap cycles and after 1 000 slip cycles | the class holds |
| drop | 1.2 m, six faces, with and without the monogram | no crack, and the session resumes |

### 18.7.6 The polycarbonate risk table

| The risk | Why it matters | The control | The test |
|---|---|---|---|
| environmental stress cracking | polycarbonate crazes at about 1.21 percent strain in isopropyl alcohol and 1.5 percent in a lipid `[PUBLICATION]` | keep every face under 1 percent, and the worst case is 0.10 percent | the seven chemical set: skin oil, sunscreen, hand cream, an alcohol wipe, a cleaning spray, the adhesive and the electrolyte vapour |
| the notch at a cut edge | a laser edge holds small flaws | polish or flame treat the edge, and keep the R 3.20 mm corner and r 0.80 mm softened edge | the edge under the wrap |
| thermal expansion mismatch | polycarbonate expands about 65 to 70 ppm/K `[GENERAL]`, and the barrier much less | measure the curl and the delamination | 500 cycles of -20 °C to 60 °C |
| creep under a long bend | a body held curved for weeks keeps a set | hold at R 70 mm for 1 000 hours | the set, and the layer strain |
| moisture uptake | the dimensions and the curl move with humidity | measure at 85 °C and 85 percent relative humidity | the dimensional and the curl report |
| UV yellowing | the cover loses clarity | a hard coat with a UV absorber | the light chamber |
| coat adhesion | the coat peels at a wrap column | the cross-cut tape test after the cycles | adhesion after the wrap |

### 18.7.7 The detection methods and the bend guard

**Five detection methods run on every coupon, before and after each test**.

1. an optical microscope at 50 times, with polarised light for the crazing of a polymer.
2. a daisy chain of conductors, read during the test. A 10 percent rise in resistance stops the test.
3. a luminance map of the panel: dead lines and dark spots.
4. a gap measure of the wrap columns, read with the drum and an optical gauge.
5. a water vapour test of the barrier, by a calcium test or an equal method, after the wrap.

**The bend guard is three parts, because a product must not rely on the user**. `[DECISION]`

| The part | The function | The gate |
|---|---|---|
| the mechanical limit | the perforated spine and the carrier stop the curl at R 22 mm, which is inside the proven R 25.5 mm | MEC-20 |
| the strain mesh | a printed conductive ink line in the cover reads the strain, and the hub reads it with the tile temperature sensors | MEC-23 |
| the bend log | the hub counts the wraps and the over-curls, and writes them into the part record, signed by the secure element. The user sees a warning at the limit, and the service reads the log | MEC-23 |

### 18.7.8 The claim wording of the bend

1. Public claims state the **number**: "long edges meet at R 25.5 mm, 5 000 times, at 23 and 40 °C".
2. Product claims forbid **fold, folds, folding, hinge, book**, **closed state**, and *a Leaf 160 becomes a Leaf 80*. `[DECISION]`
3. The claim states the flatness: **flat within 0.15 mm over 160 mm** after the wraps.
4. The claim states the no crease result: **no luminance line above 2 percent**, measured before and after.
5. Excluded damage covers tool-pressed creases, cuts, punctures, crush loads, unlisted chemicals, and curls tighter than R 25.5 mm.

### 18.7.10 The gates of the bend

The ten gates live in `HARDWARE/12-THE-BEND-LAW.md`, and they are counted here because the product claim rests on them. Every test uses at least **30 coupons** per condition. `[DECISION]`

| The gate | The subject | The pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| **B-1** | the full wrap: the two long edges are brought together 5 000 times, at 23 °C and at 40 °C | the edges meet with no tool, no crack and no pixel loss, and the bus resistance stays inside 10 percent of new |
| **B-2** | the gentle bend: R 70 mm, 50 000 cycles | no crack, no set over 1 mm per 100 mm, and no delamination |
| **B-3** | the flat recovery: the surface on a granite table after 5 000 wraps | flat within **0.15 mm over 160 mm**, and the touch map unchanged |
| **B-4** | the no crease scan: a luminance scan flat and at an angle, before and after the wraps | **no line above 2 percent of luminance**, and no visible mark |
| **B-5** | the gap budget: the opening of 50 gaps, measured optically at the wrap | **1.15 to 1.25 mm** in the wrap zone, and 0.35 to 0.45 mm in the normal area |
| **B-6** | the decoupled slip: the slip of the interfaces at the wrap, and the shear over 1 000 hours | the slip and the shear of Section 18.7.2 |
| **B-7** | the cold and the hot: the wrap at -10 °C and at 45 °C, 500 cycles each | the same pass as B-1 |
| **B-8** | the seal in the bend: a 3 mm rain of 10 minutes and 96 hours of salt spray, after 2 000 wraps | IP54, and no water past the bead |
| **B-9** | the edge condition: the two long edges meet with the rail module seated | the module stays seated, and the rail takes no damage |
| **B-10** | the wrap with a part on a face: a part seated, then the wrap | the part holds to **R 60 mm**, and a tighter curl releases it with no damage to the glass |

### 18.7.9 The cell and the body risks that the bend tests do not cover

| The risk | Why it matters | The control | The gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| the swelling of a silicon carbon cell over life | a silicon anode changes volume `[GENERAL]`, and a stack of 2 to 4 mm pushes on the panel | a soft compliance layer, and the swelling measured at 500 cycles | **ENR-9** |
| a sealed body with no vent | a cell that vents gas inside an IP67 body opens the weld or the panel | a controlled vent path: a membrane that passes gas and holds water, tested with a forced cell failure | **MEC-24** |
| the gap wear of the wrap zone | ten wide gaps close and open at every wrap | a coated gap edge, and the gap measure of B-5 at the end of life | **B-5** |
| the decoupled slip | the interfaces shear at every wrap | the 0.4 to 0.8 MPa rule, and the shear measured over 1 000 hours | **B-6** |
| the copper bridge fatigue | the bridges bend at every wrap | a serpentine shape and rolled annealed copper, with an S-N curve at the real strain | MEC-16 |
| the weld and the tab fatigue | a weld at a bridge is a stress point | a pull test after the wrap cycles | MEC-16 |
| a short at a wrap column | a wrap column presses a foil through a separator | an insulating guard layer at every column, and a short test after the wraps | ENR-9 |
| the compression set of the gasket | a gasket loses force with time and cycles | the seal measured at 1 000 cycles and after 1 000 hours of compression | MEC-22 |

---

## 18.8 The seal, decided

| The question | The answer of Ayaz | The design |
|---|---|---|
| water resistance | **required** | **route A+: the gasketed slip carrier**. The continuous battery sheet carries a dual-lip FKM gasket that seals its own bottom slot |
| the class | a target | **IP67, with IP68 as a target after the gate**, measured with a module fitted and with none |
| the ports | sealed | a gasketed USB-C receptacle, a sealed module interface, a sealed pad door |
| the rule | a removable part seals on the part, never on the body | a worn seal is replaced with the part |
| the gates | **MEC-19** and **MEC-22** | the class per architecture, and the 1 000 cycle seal |

**The earlier conflict is resolved by the architecture**. A slide out part and a water tight body conflicted because the cassette needed a **bay with a lid**. The sheet has no bay: the carrier is the seal. `[DECISION]`

---

## 18.9 The double sided panel, decided

| The path | The decision of Ayaz | The effect |
|---|---|---|
| **the first product** | **two independent panels** | no new supply risk; the product ships on a proven part |
| **the common carrier panel** | **researched in parallel** | one substrate, one backing, two independent arrays, about **0.20 to 0.30 mm** saved. Gate **DSP-11** |
| the single matrix, one image both ways | refused | the two faces show different content, and a screen readable from behind is a privacy defect |
| the transparent panel | Part 7 | a different product |

**The rule**. The first product does not wait for the common carrier. If gate DSP-11 passes, the second revision is thinner by 0.2 to 0.3 mm with no other change. `[DECISION]`

---

## 18.10 The cassette, decided

| The item | The decision |
|---|---|
| the first product | the energy sheet, with no cassette |
| the cassette | **the backup line and the buffer of the large members**: Leaf 480, Leaf 1400, and Leaf 2800 keep a buffer cassette |
| the module | keeps its 20 Wh and stays the energy part that travels with the camera |
| the reference envelope | 3.90 to 4.00 mm stays as the fallback envelope, not as the design target |

---

## 18.11 The next block: the CAD first

| The order | The line | The deliverable |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **the drawing line** | **the CAD of the body**: `80 x 160 x 2.00 mm` (`68 g`) 4-Quadrant (`+`) solid-state body, `Qi2.2` coils, and `90°` B module (`20 g`) |
| 2 | the partner line | the sample orders that the CAD unlocks: the panel with the wrap zone, the sheet cells, the gasket carrier, the common carrier panel study |
| 3 | the software line | the firmware skeleton and the photon-honest interface rules of Section 18.6 |
| 4 | the drawing line, later | the pafta re-issue: **P01 to P21 and E01 to E03**, twenty four sheets at OVER 0, COVERED 0 and COLLIDE 0 |

**The rule**. The CAD is the gate of every quotation and every fixture, so it is first. `[DECISION]`

---

## 18.12 The decisions of revision K

| No. | The question | The answer |
|---|---|---|
| Q43 | the seal | **A+, the gasketed slip carrier, IP67 with IP68 as a target** |
| Q44 | the double sided panel | **two panels in the first product; the common carrier researched in parallel** |
| Q45 | the screen cost | **Sections 18.2 and 18.4: the network cuts session energy by half through dark-first display lists, never by sending photons** |
| Q46 | the first prototype | **the full wrap**: a Leaf 160 bending to `R 25.5 mm` for `5 000` cycles without damage or crease |
| Q47 | the cassette | **the backup line and the buffer of the large members** |
| Q48 | the next block | **the CAD of the new body first**, then the samples, then the software |

**The one sentence of this part**. *A validator can pay for a computation, and it can teach a screen to need less light. It cannot send light itself, so the device keeps the photons, bends with no hinge, and seals on the sliding part*. `[DECISION]`

# BUDLUM LEAF · THE DIRECTIVE · PART 19 · THE SECOND FACE, THE CUSTOM SHAPE, THE PATENT SCREEN AND THE AUDIT LEDGER

**Revision L · 2026-10-05 · the standing of this part:** every number in this part carries a tag. `[DECISION]` means Ayaz or the validation line has ruled. `[HYPOTHESIS]` means a gate must measure it before it becomes a claim. `[PUBLICATION]` means a public source and the source is named. `[PATENT]` means a patent document. `[GENERAL]` means common engineering knowledge.

**What this part does**. The audit of revision K (`AUDIT/LEAF-AUDIT-AND-HARDENING.md`) found fifteen items. Fourteen of them touch the parts that already exist, and one of them asks for a part that does not exist. This part is that part. It adds the rules for the second face, the rules for the custom size and shape, the patent screen and the writing rule. Section 19.5 is the ledger that closes every finding of the audit with one edited file and one changed gate.

---

## 19.1 The second face and the backup (the Duo rule) `[DECISION]`

**The finding**. Part 18.9 fits two panels of the same specification to the two faces, and no rule says what the second face is for. Ayaz answered on 2026-10-05: **the second face is the second screen and the backup screen, in the manner of a dual-screen product**. This section writes the rule.

**The three modes**.

| The mode | What the second face does | The rule | The energy |
|---|---|---|---|
| **One face** | it stands dark | **the default**: photon energy on the idle face is zero. With the B module on the top edge, both `11 947 mm²` faces remain `100%` open (`0 mm²` covered) | the numbers of Part 18, with no credit claimed |
| **Second screen** | it shows its own page set: a companion page, a note, a camera view or a second app | the hub drives two scanouts, and each face has **its own permission set** | up to **+0.60 W** of photons |
| **Backup** | it stands by, and it takes the session when the first face fails or is off | **takeover under 1 s** `[HYPOTHESIS]`, and the session id does not change | stand-by is a fraction of a wait, and gate DUO-3 measures it |

**The takeover triggers**.

| The trigger | The measure |
|---|---|
| dead panel lines | a count over the limit of the panel partner |
| touch failure | a failed self test of the touch layer |
| a crack | the strain mesh of Section 5.5 of the audit, and gate MEC-23 |
| a face against a surface | the proximity and the touch pattern together |
| the user | a command, by voice, by pen or by key |

**The face in use**. The device knows which face the eye uses. It reads the motion sensor, the touch pattern and the grip together. The other face dims or turns off. A palm on the back face is rejected. `[HYPOTHESIS]` gate DUO-2 asks for the right face in 99 percent of 1 000 handling cases.

**The wallet and the privacy**. A wallet approval shows **only on the face in use**. The rule of Part 9 stands: two of the three authorities for a high value action. The single matrix design, where one image is readable from both sides, stays refused, because a shoulder reader would see the approval.

**The two faces on the sheet**. The second face adds up to 0.60 W of photons. The runtime line that follows the addition:

| The body | One face, a delta session | Two faces, a delta session |
|---|---|---|
| **Sheet 60** | 1.5 h | 1.2 h |
| **Sheet 100** | 2.8 h | 2.1 h |
| **Sheet 182** | 5.4 h | **4.2 h** |
| **Sheet 228** | 7.0 h | 5.4 h |
| the Feather cassette line | 7.9 h | 6.1 h |

The arithmetic, for the reader who checks: **14.2 Wh delivered** at Sheet 228 divides by 2.02 W for one face and 2.64 W for two. `[HYPOTHESIS]` gate DUO-3 replaces this table with measured values, and it names the luminance, the load and the class for every row.

**The Frame**. The outer face of a Frame lens **is** the same second and backup screen. The inner face carries the near-eye optics of Part 7, because a flat panel at the lens plane cannot be focused by the eye. `[DECISION]`

**The module and the ports**. The upright B module seats on the top edge and never touches a face, keeping both screens whole. The port module seats on the rail of one long edge, and the hub learns its connectors from the identity handshake. `[DECISION]`

**The gates**.

| Gate | The subject | The pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| **DUO-1** | the takeover time and the session continuity | under 1 s, and no session loss |
| **DUO-2** | the face in use detection | the right face in 99 percent of 1 000 handling cases `[HYPOTHESIS]` |
| **DUO-3** | the power of each mode | a measured table: one face, two faces, backup stand-by |
| **DUO-4** | the palm rejection | no false touch from the back face |
| **DUO-5** | the wallet prompt placement | the prompt never shows on the face that is not in use |
| **DUO-6** | the heat with two faces on | the surface temperature stays inside the limit of Part 11 |

---

## 19.2 The custom size and the custom shape (the SZ rule) `[DECISION]`

**The finding**. The directive says the size is parametric, and it writes no rule for the parameters. Ayaz chose the size and the shape per order. This section writes the rules.

**What the order fixes, and what the design fixes**.

| The parameter | The freedom | The limit |
|---|---|---|
| the width and the length | free, per order | the design rules of the panel partner |
| the aspect ratio | free inside the family band | the panel partner confirms the band |
| the corner radius | a parameter | **R = 6.35 mm corner radius with r = 0.80 mm softened edges is the family signature at the 160 class** |
| the curve class | flat, a single curve, or a bend | flat: desk, room and wall members. Bend: the carried member, and gate MEC-20 states the radius |
| the number of faces | one or two | two faces are the standard build, one face is a variant |
| the battery architecture | **4-Quadrant (`+`) snap-together polycarbonate screen-battery tiles (`4 x 40.0 x 80.0 x 2.00 mm`, `750 Wh/L` `LLZO/PEO`, `12.0 Wh` / `3 200 mAh`)** | see the rule below |
| the outline | free, inside the family form | the cut is made at the panel stage, and a made panel cannot be cut again |

**The 4-Quadrant (`+`) solid-state battery and bend rule**. In Revision L.5.0, the bulky external bottom battery pack is cancelled (`HARDWARE/11`, `HARDWARE/12`). The `80 x 160 x 2.00 mm` (`68 g`) body splits across `+` into four `40 x 80 x 2.00 mm` (`17 g`) tiles (`Q1..Q4`). Each tile bends to `R 25.5 mm` at `0.38 percent` outer PC strain (`0.22%` `LLZO/PEO` strain):

| The curve class | The body & battery structure | Peak screen strain at working radius | Solid-state cell density |
|---|---|---|---|
| R 25.5 mm, the carried member | **`2.00 mm` (`68 g`) 4-Quadrant (`+`) `ISO 10993` PC + `LLZO/PEO` solid-state laminate** | 0.38 percent (`0.22%` on `LLZO/PEO` core) | `750 Wh/L` (`4 x 3.0 Wh = 12.0 Wh`) |
| R 100 mm, the soft bend | **`2.00 mm` (`68 g`) 4-Quadrant (`+`) `ISO 10993` PC + `LLZO/PEO` solid-state laminate** | 0.10 percent (`0.06%` on `LLZO/PEO` core) | `750 Wh/L` (`4 x 3.0 Wh = 12.0 Wh`) |
| flat, the desk, room and wall members | **`2.40 mm` multi-tile `+` sheet + `Qi2.2` / Dock feed** | 0 percent | `750 Wh/L` or mains |

**Rule**. A geometry manifest states the curve class and battery structure. A manifest with rigid battery tiles on a curved body is **refused**, and the refusal names the field. `[DECISION]`

**The energy of the large members, from the same table**. The detachable bottom H-frame module of Part 17 carries 700 to 950 Wh/L Silicon-Carbon cells. The count then follows the area:

| The member | The surface | The tiles at the grid | The nameplate, base case | The body |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Leaf 80** | 6 400 mm² | 64 at 10 mm | about 10 Wh | 2.84 mm and up |
| **Leaf 160** | 10 977 mm² | 128 at 10 mm | about 20 Wh | 3.30 mm at Sheet 228 |
| **Leaf 160²** | 25 600 mm² | 256 at 10 mm | about 41 Wh | 3.30 mm |
| **Leaf 480** | 230 400 mm² | 576 at 20 mm | about 369 Wh | 3.30 mm |
| **Leaf 1400** | 1 960 000 mm² | 4 900 at 20 mm | about 3.1 kWh | flat, and the desk member |
| **Leaf 2800** | 7 840 000 mm² | 19 600 at 20 mm | about 12.5 kWh | flat, and the wall member |

Every figure is `[HYPOTHESIS]` and gate SZ-2 measures three real outlines against it. The names carry the size and no class word, per the naming law of Part 0.5.

**The size classes scale four things**. A table of size classes fixes the antenna count, the hub class, the heat path and the tile grid. The surface area per antenna stays inside the band of Part 5, and a larger member adds antennas rather than power per antenna. `[HYPOTHESIS]` gate RF-9 is the owner.

**The geometry manifest**. The configurator writes a signed manifest: the width, the length, the radius, the curve class, the faces, the grid, the outline and the tile count. The manifest is checked against the panel partner's rules before the order enters the line. An outline outside the rules is refused with the failing field named. `[DECISION]`

**The lens outline**. A Frame lens is cut from a prescription blank, and the waveguide has a fixed aperture. The outline must keep the aperture inside the cut, so the frame, the interpupillary distance and the prescription range are inputs to the cut. `[DECISION]`

**The order minimum**. A custom outline needs a new cut program and a new tile layout. The line states a minimum order per outline, and it states no price, per the rule of Part 15, H-13. `[DECISION]`

**The gates**.

| Gate | The subject | The pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| **SZ-1** | the limits table | the panel partner confirms every limit in writing |
| **SZ-2** | the tile layout | three real outlines get a valid layout and pass the bend test |
| **SZ-3** | the manifest check | a wrong outline is refused, with the failing field named |
| **SZ-4** | the lens outline | three lens shapes keep the waveguide aperture and pass the drop test |
| **SZ-5** | the grid rule | a 20 mm grid on a R 25 mm body is refused, and a 10 mm grid passes |

## 19.3 The patent screen and the freedom to operate (F-13)

**The finding**. The words "patent" and "freedom to operate" do not appear in Parts 0 to 18 of revision K. Ayaz asked to avoid the patents of others and to be warned when no free route exists.

**The rules**.

1. Every design choice carries a patent register entry: number, owner, filing date, expiry date and design-around.
2. A United States utility patent lasts 20 years from the earliest filing date. A patent term adjustment can extend it. **Every date in this register is nominal until an attorney verifies it**. `[GENERAL]`
3. The order of preference is: expired patent, free standard, licensed part with supply-contract indemnity, and own design. `[DECISION]`
4. No claim text is copied. The design log is dated, and it records what was read and what was decided.
5. An attorney does a freedom-to-operate review before the first sale, and the attorney rules how the engineers read patents.
6. When the attorney marks a choice as blocked, the choice leaves this directive and a design-around takes its place. `[DECISION]`

**The start register, with the dates that this round found**.

| The area | The document | The owner and the dates | The design-around, and the status |
|---|---|---|---|
| a flexible cell with a serpentine body | **US10637017B2**, "Flexible battery structure" `[PATENT]` | **Apple Inc.**, filed 2016-09-23, granted 2020-04-28, nominal expiry 2036-09-23, term adjustment to verify | requires staggered notches or die-cut stress-relieving gaps in a stacked cell. **The Leaf battery uses 4 un-notched continuous sliding polymer-gel layers**. Design-around written |
| a flexible cell assembly and its method | **US11417890B2**, "Flexible battery assembly and method for manufacturing the same" `[PATENT]` | **Avary Holding (Shenzhen)** and **Qing Ding Precision Electronics (Huaian)**, filed 2019-12-02, granted 2022-08-16, nominal expiry 2039-12-02 | method claims on patterned collectors and printed electrolyte (`7.5-35 µm` barrier). **This directive buys finished polymer-gel layers with supplier IP indemnity**. Design-around written |
| the fill step and a temple cell | a Meta patent on a polymerized electrolyte in a glasses temple `[PATENT]` | the number and the dates are **to verify** in the attorney's first pass | the standard fill with an own cell shape. The claim features are not copied. Open, and owned by the FTO |
| a structural carbon fibre cell | the Chalmers and Sinonus line of work `[COMPANY]` | to verify | **carbon fibre is not used as an electrode in this directive**. The fibre stays a structural material. Refused as a cell route |
| the tiled and flexible cell layouts | further family members to the two above `[PATENT]` | to verify | an own tile and bridge layout, and a dated design log |
| **the wireless power route, closed by decision R-1** | **Qi2.2**, the WPC standard `[PUBLICATION]` | the WPC released Qi2.2 to members in **April 2025**. It carries **25 W broadly and up to 50 W** where the device and the transmitter allow. First certified products reached the market in **September 2025**, and the wide availability runs through 2026 | **the family does not take the wireless route (`R-1`)**. With no coil on the device, there is no WPC fee, certification, or field license (Part 20, Section 20.1) |
| **the cost of the route that the family no longer takes** | the WPC fee schedule, fiscal 2026 `[PUBLICATION]` | Regular membership is **`$30 000/yr`** (`$18 000/yr` Adopter). A Qi2.2 `25 W` certification test is **`$20 000-$25 000`**, plus `$1 500-$3 000` for keys | a first product therefore costs about **25 000 to 38 000 dollars** before the tooling. The number enters the partner dossier, and gate PRG-14 owns it |
| a waveguide and the eye glow | a very large patent field `[GENERAL]` | to verify per supplier | buy from a licensed supplier, with the indemnity in the contract. Refused as an own development |
| the flexible panel | the display makers hold many patents | to verify | buy the panel from a licensed maker. Refused as an own development |
| a modular attachable camera | earlier modular products exist `[GENERAL]` | to verify | an own attach, an own contact pattern and the wallet binding. **No "first" claim is made anywhere in this directive**. The claim policy of Part 12 owns the wording |

**The register after decision R-1**. The wireless route is **closed**: the family carries no coil, so WPC fees, certification and coil FTO questions leave the program. **The warning register now holds one route**: carbon fibre as an electrode. `[DECISION]`

**The gate**.

| Gate | The subject | The pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| **PRG-14** | the freedom to operate: the register, the dates, the design-arounds and the design log | an attorney signs the register before the first sale, every date is verified, and every blocked choice is out of the directive |

## 19.4 The writing rule and the count of this revision (F-15)

**The finding**. Ayaz asked for ASD-STE100 simplified technical English and no em dashes. The audit measured 108 em dashes in the archive, and the audit called this a standing defect.

**The rule**.

| The rule | The measure |
|---|---|
| no em dash | the character is absent from every file. A comma, a colon or a full stop takes its place |
| a descriptive sentence | **25 words or less** |
| a procedural sentence | **20 words or less** |
| one idea, one sentence | the active voice and the simple present |
| a rule | uses **must** or **can**. The words should, may, might and could are reported |
| one term, one thing | the glossary of Part 14 is the base, and it grows with this part |
| a paragraph | six sentences or less |
| a table cell | a label set, and not a sentence. The tool counts a long cell apart, and it does not fail the gate (108 at revision L.2) |

**The tool, and the gate**. `LEAF/tools/check_prose.py` counts em dashes, the sentences over the limit and the warning words. It is **gate PRG-12**, and it runs with every assembly, beside `check_sheets.py` of the drawing line and `check_crossrefs.py` of the count line.

**The measurement of Revision L.5.0, taken on 2026-10-12**.

| The measure | The number | The verdict |
|---|---|---|
| em dashes, FINAL and MASTER, 43 files | **0** | the rule holds across all 43 files |
| sentences over 25 words (or 20 in steps) | **0 of 13 136**, at **0.0 percent** | **closed**. Every file in `FINAL/` (`00` to `20`) and `MASTER/` (`00` to `18` + `STATE.md`) passes at `0.0 percent` |
| the parts over the limit | **none** (`0` across all 43 files) | all 21 `FINAL` parts and all 22 `MASTER` files are converted |
| the warning words | **6** across the tree, **1 in FINAL** (a quoted question of Ayaz) | an exemption is written for a quotation and for the rule statement itself |

**The conversion plan**. The audit asks to start with the parts that a coding AI, a partner or a factory reads first. The plan is therefore: **Part 0, Part 15, Part 17, Part 18, then Parts 1 to 13, then the MASTER volumes**. The target is **under 1 percent of long sentences** before the first sale. Each round of editing runs `check_prose.py` and writes its number into this table. `[DECISION]`

**The gate name, and one collision**. The audit asks for the writing check under the name PRG-12. Revision K had already given PRG-12 to the legal reading of the energy routes. **The legal gate moves to PRG-13, and PRG-12 is the writing check**, so that the audit and the directive use one name for one gate. `[DECISION]`

---

**The one line that this part adds to the map**. The second face has a job, size is a rule, every design choice has a patent line, and tools verify every count.
