# BUDLUM LEAF · THE DIRECTIVE · PART 20 · THE CHARGE PATH, THE BUDLUM MODULE AND THE PORT RAIL

**Revision L.3 · 2026-10-06 · the standing of this part**. Every number carries a tag. `[DECISION]` means Ayaz or the validation line has ruled. `[HYPOTHESIS]` means a gate must measure it before it becomes a claim. `[PUBLICATION]` means a public source and the source is named. `[GENERAL]` means common engineering knowledge.

**The orders that created this part, in the order they arrived**.

1. **The wireless path is withdrawn**. *"Kablosuz şarj olmasın, boşver"*. The device charges by wire and by contact.
2. **The upright B module stands on the top edge**. Modeled 1:1 from `uploads/b.png` (`40 x 32 x 4 mm`), the `b` attaches at its bottom (`y = 0 mm`). Its vertical bar is the antenna, its front ring holds the camera, and its back holds the round panel.
3. **The magnetic latches sit in the edge frame**. `N52H` magnet arrays in the top and side rails seat the upright B module and the side Port Rail. `HARDWARE/10-ANY-PLACE-AND-THE-MAGNETIC-FIELD.md` carries the full specification.
4. **The ports are a part, and not an opening**. *"USB-C, isterse USB-A, isterse kulaklık; ayrı satılan bir parça"*. The body carries no connector opening, and a detachable module on the side rail provides the chosen ports. Section 20.3.5 records the mechanism and the law.

Ayaz is away from the program. The decisions in this part are taken by the program line and recorded in Section 20.6 so that they can be reported on request. `[DECISION]`

---

## 20.1 What the wireless path cost, and what its withdrawal returns

**The state before this part**. The founding order of Ayaz asked for wireless power at *the highest wattage the gates permit*. The directive then carried a coil array in the stand, a Qi2 class fallback and a metal free window in the body. That requirement is withdrawn today. `[DECISION]`

**The costs that leave with it**.

| The item | The number, and the source |
|---|---|
| the coil and the shield inside the body | receiving coil (`0.40 mm`), ferrite shield (`0.25 mm`), and adhesive: **about `0.65 mm` of stack** and **`6 g`** with the shield frame |
| the loss at 25 W | Qi2.2 moves 25 W broadly, and the end to end efficiency of a certified magnetic link is stated near **83 percent**. So about **5 W becomes heat in the pad and the coil** at 25 W. A wired path at the same power loses about **0.5 W** in a 40 W class cable and a charger [PUBLICATION] |
| the metal free window | a metal back and a coil are contradictory, so the back face would carry a polymer window with a lower drop and a lower stiffness |
| the certification and the fee | fiscal 2026 WPC fee: **`$30 000/yr`** Regular (`$18 000/yr` Adopter). Qi2.2 `25 W` test is **`$20 000-$25 000`**, plus `$1 500-$3 000` keys (`$25 000-$38 000` before tooling) [PUBLICATION] |
| the integration risks | foreign object detection, a coil phase schedule against the radios, and a heat path through the coil area |

**What the withdrawal buys back**.

| The item | The value |
|---|---|
| stack | about **0.65 mm** of the body, which the energy sheet can take as thickness, or the thickness claim can take as thinness |
| mass | about **6 g** |
| the back face | **no metal free window**: the heat spreader can be metal and can reach the perimeter |
| the money | **`$25 000 to $38 000`** saved before the first sale, plus **`$18 000 to $30 000/yr`** saved thereafter |
| the radio | the coil desense problem of gate RF-3 changes subject (Section 20.2) |

**The energy that 0.65 mm carries, at the base case of Part 17**. The net cell area of a Leaf 160 sheet is about **110 cm²**. That figure is after the tile and bridge loss. Then:

`0.65 mm × 110 cm² = 7.15 cm³`, and at **800 Wh/L** that is 5.72 Wh of nameplate. After the perimeter frame margin of Section 20.3, **4.6 Wh of nameplate and 3.7 Wh of effective energy** stay in the design. `[HYPOTHESIS]` gate ENR-7 measures the sheet that takes it.

**The rule that replaces the old order**. **A Budlum Leaf charges by wire and by contact. It does not charge by induction, and no member carries a coil**. `[DECISION]`

---

## 20.2 The charge path now: two ways in, both of them contacts

| The path | The power | The loss | The details |
|---|---|---|---|
| **USB-C at the fixed edge** | **40 W peak, 25 W flat** | about 0.5 W at 25 W | USB PD and a sealed receptacle. The derating curve of Part 4, Section 4.5: full below 38 °C, 20 percent at 45 °C, stop at 48 °C |
| **The dock and the rail contacts** | **25 W** | **about 0.1 W** | four gold pads on the fixed edge, spring pins in the dock, 20 V at 1.25 A per power pad |

**The contact numbers**. `[PUBLICATION]` A gold on gold spring contact holds **20 to 50 mΩ** at mid stroke, and below 30 mΩ with hard gold. The parts are rated for **10 000 to 1 000 000 mating cycles**, matching standard sealed watch and tracker contacts.

| The item | The value | The arithmetic |
|---|---|---|
| the current per power pad | 1.25 A | 25 W at 20 V |
| the contact resistance, new | 30 mΩ | a gold pad and a spring pin |
| the loss, two pads | **0.094 W** | `2 × 1.25² × 0.030` |
| the loss, a worn pad at 100 mΩ | **0.31 W** | the end of life rule of gate MEC-6 |
| the loss of the withdrawn wireless path at 25 W | **about 5 W** | at 83 percent end to end |

**So the contact path loses about fifty times less than the induction path at the same power**. It does that without a coil, a shield, a window or a fee. `[DECISION]`

**The four pads, and the rules**.

| The pad | The duty |
|---|---|
| 1 | the power, 20 V at 1.25 A |
| 2 | the ground |
| 3 | the identification: a one wire signature in the dock, so the device knows a real dock is present |
| 4 | the spare, for a later rail function (the optional validator board of Part 15, H-14) |

**The rules**. The pads are **unpowered until the identification handshake completes**, so a wet pocket cannot electrolyse them. The pads are **flush and recessed by 0.15 mm** in a gasketed groove, so the class of gate MEC-19 holds with the dock off. Spring pins live in the replaceable dock, while recessed gold pads stay on the device. `[DECISION]`

**The gate change**. **MEC-6 becomes the wired and contact charge bench**. It covers the USB-C path at 40 and 25 W, and the contact path at 25 W. It measures pad loss at 0, 20 000 and 50 000 cycles, after 96 h salt spray and 500 h at 85 °C/85% RH. **RF-3 measures radio desense with the charge cable attached and on the dock**, because no wireless coil exists. `[DECISION]`

---

## 20.3 The `90°`-bent-stem Budlum B module: flat ring on screen, sole `USB-C` in stem, and reversible 4-edge docking

**Revision L.5.0, of 2026-10-12**. Modeled 1:1 from `uploads/b.png` (`20.0 g`, `32` parts), **only the stem (`çubuk`) bends at a `90°` right angle (`dik açıyla`)**. Its **circular ring (`yuvarlak`, `32 x 32 x 4.0 mm`) lies flat on top of the screen**. Moving the `90°`-bent stem around the 4 outer edges docks the B module to the **Front (`+Z`) or Back (`-Z`)** (`r = 0.55 mm`). `[DECISION]`

**The part, in one line**. The `90°`-bent-stem B module (`20.0 g`) houses the **sole `USB Type-C` (`40 W`) port and 3-band/`60 GHz` antennas in its `90°` stem**. Its **flat `Ø32.0 / Ø24.0 mm` ring on the screen holds the `50 MP` camera, rear `Ø22.4 mm` AMOLED, audio, and Zero-CPU SiP**. `[DECISION]`

### 20.3.1 What Revision L.5.0 establishes

| Prior draft feature | What replaces it in Revision L.5.0 |
|---|---|
| bulky external bottom battery pack (`80 x 36 x 4 mm`, `46 g`) | **cancelled**: replaced by **`4 x (40 x 80 x 2.00 mm)` (`68 g`) `+` polycarbonate solid-state quadrants (`Q1..Q4`, `12.0 Wh` `LLZO/PEO`)** |
| visible seam or scratch between modular tiles | **`<15 µm` (`n = 1.585`) phenyl-fluorosilicone self-healing optical `+` lip (`R < 0.0001%`)**, `<2.5 µm` Z-step on Ti pins, Corning edge-wrap (`0.00 mm` internal bezel) |
| flammable liquid Li-ion near the eye in eyewear mode | **non-flammable `LLZO/PEO` all-solid-state battery (`0%` liquid, `0.0 ppm` HF)**, **`<=36.2 °C` hardware NTC cut-off**, `ISO 10993` PC, `<0.08 µT` bifilar ELF, `SAR = 0.054 W/kg` |
| USB-C port on the screen body or wireless charging ban | **zero USB-C on screen body**; **universal `Qi2.2` (`Ø54 mm`) + `4 x Ø22 mm` wireless charging**; **sole `USB-C` (`40 W`) in B stem** |
| upright flat `b` fixed only at top-left edge | **only the stem (`çubuk`) bends `90°` (`dik açıyla`)** via a `±90°` Ti hinge; the **round ring lies flat on the screen (`+Z / -Z`)** |

**What the architecture delivers**. Four **17 g** quadrants form a **68 g** (`12.0 Wh`) `Qi2.2` body (`88 g` complete with the **20 g** B module). `[DECISION]`

### 20.3.2 The shape, the camera and the panel

| The quantity | The value |
|---|---|
| the `90°`-bent-stem B module | `Ø32 x 4.0 mm` flat ring on screen + `8 x 32 x 4.5 mm` `90°` stem (`r = 0.55 mm`, `20.0 g`, `32` parts) |
| the flat ring (`yuvarlak`) | `Ø32.0 mm` outside, `Ø24.0 mm` inner aperture, `4.00 mm` Z-stack lying flat on the screen (`+Z` or `-Z`) |
| the front camera | **fixed inside the upper Z-stack (`z = 0.90..3.60 mm`)**, `12.8 x 9.6 mm` (`50 MP`) CMOS + 4-axis OIS + prism + `5P` lens |
| the rear round panel | **`Ø22.4 mm` active round LTPO AMOLED** (`480 x 480 px`, `300 ppi`, `1-120 Hz`, `z = 0.35..0.75 mm`) + `0.15 mm` Cu-graphite shield |
| the `90°`-bent stem (`çubuk`) | `8 x 32 x 4.5 mm` bent `90°` (`±90°` Ti hinge); houses **sole `USB Type-C` (`40 W`)** + `Wi-Fi 7 / UWB / 60 GHz` |
| Zero-CPU 6L ring PCBA | `Ø30.2 / Ø24.4 x 0.55 mm` inside ring wall: **`U1` Quad-Tile Master TCON**, **`U2` `UWB / mmWave` SiP**, **`U3` Audio DSP**, **`U4` Dead-Pad PMIC** |
| the in-ring acoustics & flash | `2 x MEMS` mics (`68 dB` SNR), `9.0 x 3.2 x 2.2 mm` speaker (`78 dB SPL`), and dual-CCT LED flash inside the ring bezel |
| 4-edge reversible dock | `8` `N52H` magnets (`7.2 N` hold), `12` symmetric gold pogo pins (`18 mΩ`, `3 ms` Dead-Pad), and `IP68` FKM gasket on all 4 edges |
| the internal bridge cell | `0.45 Wh` (`120 mAh`) solid-state arc cell inside the ring wall |
| the seats | **all 4 outer edges (`Top`, `Bottom`, `Left`, `Right`) and both faces (`Front +Z` and `Back -Z`)** |

### 20.3.3 The optics, and the honest claim

| The quantity | The value |
|---|---|
| the elements | **8 in 4 groups**, molded glass with lanthanum `45°` folding prism |
| the focal length | **8.9 mm** (`24 mm` full-frame equivalent on a `12.8 x 9.6 mm` active sensor) |
| the aperture | `f/1.8` with 4-blade mechanical shutter and 2-axis piezo sensor-shift OIS (`±1.5°`) |
| the folded path | **3.4 mm into the ring and 14.0 mm across the `Ø24.0 mm` bore**, `17.4 mm` total optical path inside `4.00 mm` |
| the sensor | 1-inch class CMOS, `12.8 x 9.6 mm` active, `50 MP` daylight (`1.6 µm`) and `12.5 MP` binned (`3.2 µm`) |
| the crop at 2 times | `12.5 MP` lossless in-sensor crop at **48 mm equivalent** |
| the crop at 3 times | `5.6 MP` crop at **72 mm equivalent**, and no protruding telephoto barrel exists |
| the video | `4K 60 fps` / `4K 120` capture with sub-20 ms rear round AMOLED selfie viewfinder |

**The honest reading**. A crop is not a telephoto lens, and the program says it plainly. What the fixed camera buys is a larger sensor, more light and one lens to certify, and the whole screen stays lit. `[DECISION]`

### 20.3.4 The live area is a normal screen, and nothing is imposed on it

| The quantity | The value |
|---|---|
| the screen | `76.4 x 156.4 mm` per face (`R 1.40 mm` screen corner inside `R 3.20 mm` body corner), `1528 x 3128 px` at `508 ppi` |
| the lit area, always | **11 947 mm²** (`100 percent` open), because the upright B module sits on the top edge (`y = +80.0 mm`) |
| the covered region | **0 mm²** on both front and back screens |
| the scaled region | **0 mm²**, because nothing is imposed on the panel |

### 20.3.5 The port rail: the ports are a part, and why

The order of Ayaz stands: **every port lives on a detachable rail module** on one long edge, and it ships in the box. `HARDWARE/14-PORT-RAIL-PART-BY-PART.md` specifies the `46.0 x 4.00 x 3.20 mm` side rail (`USB-C PD 3.1`, `3.5 mm` jack, pen dock, `8` pads). `[DECISION]`

| The class | The part | The seat | The energy |
|---|---|---|---|
| **the module joint** | the Budlum module | the edge, at one of three seats | its own 0.60 Wh cell, and 0.5 A at the lands |
| **RL, the rail** | the port module | the rail edge, a latch and four poles | the device feeds the module |
| **DK, the dock** | the desk, room and wall members | four pads and two pegs | 27 W from the dock |

| the mass | **88 g** complete (`68 g` 4-Quadrant `+` body = `4 x 17 g` tiles + `20 g` `90°`-bent-stem B module) | `HARDWARE/03`, `MASTER/STATE.md` |
| the carried balance | the module moves the balance point **16.0 mm** when it is on | `HARDWARE/20`, Section 7 |

## 20.4 The other reading of the order: the camera behind the screen, and why it is refused

**The order has a second reading:** *mount the camera hardware behind the screen.* The industry calls that an under display camera. The program tested that reading with numbers, and it is **refused for the Budlum Leaf cameras**. `[DECISION]`

| The item | The number | The source |
|---|---|---|
| the transmission of a flexible OLED at the camera area | **2.9 percent**, with the haze, the blur and the yellow shift that a polyamide substrate adds | the CVPR 2021 study of under display restoration [PUBLICATION] |
| the transmission of a transparent OLED | about **20 percent**, and the pixel grid acts as a grating that blurs along one axis | the same study, and the Microsoft applied sciences note [PUBLICATION] |
| the resolution that the industry ships | a 4 MP under display camera in a 2026 foldable, and a 1.5 MP unit for cars | [PUBLICATION] |
| the degradation that a supplier states | about **30 percent**, even with an AI restoration pipeline that claims 99 percent recovery | LG Innotek, reported for CES 2026 [PUBLICATION] |
| the information that is lost for good | the pixel opening cuts whole spatial frequencies, and **a missing frequency cannot be recovered by computation** | the Microsoft MTF analysis [PUBLICATION] |

**Three reasons of this program decide it**.

1. **The resolution gap is 12 times**. The module carries one **50 MP** sensor. A 4 MP window behind the panel is 8 percent of those pixels. No pipeline produces 50 MP from 4 MP, because the window has already lost the high frequencies.
2. **The panel of this product bends**. A reduced-stack window sits where strain is highest during an `R 25.5 mm` wrap. Part 18, Section 18.7.4, has no budget for a crack initiator.
3. **The edge seat and the round panel give the same job for free**. The order of Ayaz asks for a camera that can face the user. The upright B module carries a **50 MP** lens, and its rear round panel serves as the viewfinder. The under display route buys nothing here.

**The follow up rule**. The question returns only when a supplier ships a **20 MP under-display camera at 70 percent transmission**. Until then the refusal stands, and the register of Part 19, Section 19.3, carries it. `[DECISION]`

---

## 20.5 The test plan: how the module and the rail are proven

Ayaz asked for a camera on the device and for a detachable connector part. This is the trial of both. Per Section 5.4, each fatigue condition tests **at least 30 coupons** and reports a failure statistic.

| No. | The trial | The coupons | The pass condition | The gates |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | the edge seat | 30 modules on 30 ends | **2.5 N** normal and **1.2 N** shear, and a release under 5 N | MEC-29 |
| 2 | the seat life | 30 modules, 2 000 seats | the force inside 20 percent, and a seam under 0.12 mm | MEC-29, MON-18 |
| 3 | the joint seal | 30 joints, then 96 hours of salt spray | **IP54**, and no water inside the plate | MON-12, MON-19 |
| 4 | the lands | 30 sets, 50 000 matings, then a salt spray | 20 to 50 milliohm mid stroke, and no plating wear through | CAM-7, ENR-13 |
| 5 | the camera in the bore | 30 modules, a 1.0 m drop on 6 faces | no movement over 0.02 mm, and no mark that reads in a photograph | CAM-7, MON-14 |
| 6 | the optic | 3 benches, the drawing and the chart | the modulation transfer at f/1.5 and f/4.0, and the focus at 0.10 m | CAM-3, CAM-4 |
| 7 | the panel | 30 panels, 10 000 hours | 600 nits, 60 Hz, 0.06 W, and no dead pixel line | MON-4, MON-18 |
| 8 | the bend | 30 joints at R 25.5 mm, 5 000 wraps | the module holds, and the seam stays under 0.12 mm | MON-15, MEC-32 |
| 9 | the rail and the hot removal | the rig of `HARDWARE/14` | 100 hot removals at 40 W with no damage | RL-1 to RL-12 |
| 10 | the identity and the order | 30 seats, every mis-alignment under 0.5 mm | the power path stays open until the challenge is answered | MON-10, MON-16 |

**The fixtures**. A seat stand with three fixtures, one wrap rig at R 25.5 mm, a camera bench, a photometer, a sound box and the RF chamber. Two of the six already exist in the plan of Part 12. `[DECISION]`

# BUDLUM LEAF · THE DIRECTIVE · PART 21 · THE STRETCH, THE ONE MILLIMETRE BAR AND THE PROJECTION

**Revision L.6.0 · 2026-10-12 · the standing of this part**. This part supersedes every earlier statement it names in Section 21.1. Tags follow Part 20: `[DECISION]`, `[HYPOTHESIS]`, `[PUBLICATION]`, `[GENERAL]` and `[TREE]`.

**The orders that created this part, as Ayaz gave them on 2026-10-12**.

1. *"1 mm çubuk ve esneme olması şart."* The B bar is `1.00 mm`. The screen body stretches, and bending alone is not enough.
2. *"Yansıtmadan kastım, işlemin ağırlıkla validatörden gelmesi."* Projection means the Leaf shows what the network computes. It does not mean an optical projector.
3. *"Validatörler Budlum'u çalıştırdığı gibi bizim cihazımızı çalıştırıyor."* The validators run the Leaf as they run the chain. Distance must not be a problem. Section 21.5.
4. *"50 MP kamera olacak."* The camera stays `50 MP`, and it is the best camera the ring can hold. Section 21.3.5.
5. *"Esnemesi mümkün, polikarbonat cam yerine kullanılıyor."* The polycarbonate face stays, and the body stretches. Section 21.2.
6. *"Modelin kalınlığı 4 mm, çubuğun kendisi de 1 mm."* The module is `4.00 mm` thick. The bar itself is `1.00 mm`. Section 21.3.
7. *"Esner ama pil düşer diyorsun; yeni bir stil, biraz daha araştır."* The stretch must not cost the battery. Section 21.2.
8. *"Bunlar bir cüzdandır, en yakın validatöre bağlanır."* The Leaf is a wallet. It connects to the nearest validator. Section 21.5.4.

**The answers of Ayaz to the scan questions, 2026-10-12**.

| No. | The question | The answer | Where it lands |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | the `1 mm` bar | the module is `4.00 mm` thick and the bar is `1.00 mm` | Section 21.3 |
| 2 | the place of the B | the ring lies on the screen face, front or back | Section 21.3.1 |
| 3 | the energy at full stretch | more battery in the B module and in the dock | Sections 21.2.5 and 21.6 |
| 4 | wireless charging | Qi2.2 stays | Section 21.2.6 |
| 5 | USB-C | a separately sold port part | Section 21.3.4 |
| 6 | the camera | a small-pixel `50 MP` sensor, and the best possible camera | Section 21.3.5 |
| 7 | the B contents | rear display, speaker, microphones, flash, small battery and the `3.5 mm` jack all stay | Sections 21.3.6 and 21.6 |
| 8 | the session | no session cipher. The Leaf is a wallet and connects to the nearest validator | Section 21.5.4 |
| 9 | the dock validator | optional | Section 21.5.4 |
| 10 | outdoor latency | `30 ms` stays the target outdoors | Section 21.5.7 |
| 11 | the pixel density | `508 ppi` stays | Section 21.2.3 |
| 12 | the seams | a seam inside the screen is invisible, and the panels stay removable | Section 21.2.4 |
| 13 | the remote agent mode | removed, because it never worked and leaked a key | `tools/multi_agent_optimize.py` |
| 14 | `heypcb-post.txt` | rewritten to L.6.0 | `teslim/heypcb-post.txt` |

| the stem is `8.0 x 32.0 x 4.50 mm` | `HARDWARE/05`, `HARDWARE/13`, `HARDWARE/01` | the bar is `32.0 x 4.0 x 1.00 mm` on the top edge, in a `4.00 mm` module. Section 21.3 `[DECISION]` |

| a `0.45 Wh` bridge cell in `18.0 x 4.2 x 1.20 mm` | `HARDWARE/13`, part 29 | that is about `4 960 Wh/L` and impossible. The B cell is `0.14 Wh`. Section 21.2.5 |
| the module mass is `20.0 g` | `HARDWARE/05` | the old list summed to `22.00 g`. The L.6.0 list lives in `HARDWARE/13` |

**The rule of this part**. Where an older part and this part disagree, this part wins until the older part is rewritten. `[DECISION]`

## 21.2 The stretch

### 21.2.1 The new style: rotating squares

**The finding of the research of 2026-10-12**. The energy of a stretchable body does not depend on the stretch. It depends on how much of the relaxed area is cell. `[DECISION]`

The first L.6.0 body put cells on `5 mm` islands with `4 mm` serpentine lanes. Only `31 %` of the area held cells, so it reached `13.3 %` at `1.7 Wh`. That body is withdrawn.

**The rotating-square body**. The body is a tessellation of rigid square tiles. Each tile joins its neighbours only at its corners, through a hinge. Under a pull, neighbouring tiles turn in opposite directions, and the body grows in both directions at once. No tile and no cell strains. The tiles only turn. `[PUBLICATION]` Rotating-square displays were shown in 2024 in npj Flexible Electronics.

| The item | The value |
|---|---|
| the tile | `10.0 x 10.0 mm`, rigid |
| the tile count | `8 x 16 = 128`, so `32` per quadrant `Q1..Q4` |
| the stretch factor | `cos θ + sin θ`, in both directions |
| the default stretch | `θ = 17.5°`: `25.4 %` in every direction, `1.57` times the area |
| the maximum stretch | `θ = 45°`: `41.4 %` in every direction, `2.00` times the area |
| the body at maximum | `80 x 160 mm` grows to `113 x 226 mm` |
| the cell area at rest | about `83 %` of the face, against `31 %` for the withdrawn body |

**The behaviour**. The lattice grows equally in both directions, because it is auxetic. A pull along one axis also widens the other axis. The body has one degree of freedom, so `U1` reads the turn of every tile. Gate DSP-17.

### 21.2.2 The hinge and the seal

1. **The hinge stands on edge**. Neighbouring tiles counter-rotate, so a corner hinge turns through `2θ`. A flat ligament in the face would bend about its width and break near `15 %` strain.
2. **The ribbon**. Each hinge is a polyimide ribbon `12.5 µm` thick, `1.2` to `1.5 mm` tall and `0.8 mm` long. The tile corners are chamfered to give the free length.
3. **The strain**. The ribbon strain is `0.48 %` at `25 %` stretch and `1.23 %` at `41 %` stretch. Polyimide yields near `3 %`. `[HYPOTHESIS]` gate MEC-42.
4. **The conductors**. Copper of `2 µm` or less runs on the neutral plane of the ribbon in the `25 %` mode. Liquid-metal EGaIn channels carry the lines that serve the `41 %` mode.
5. **No slit filler**. A gap opens to `6.0 mm` at `17.5°`, so no elastomer can fill it. Each tile is sealed on its own: PC cap, pixel tile and welded cell can.
6. **The floating back skin**. A clear TPU or silicone skin, `50` to `100 µm`, is bonded to small pads at the tile centres. It sees only the global strain of `25` to `41 %`. It breaks at `300` to `900 %`. `[GENERAL]` Gate MEC-41.
7. **The polycarbonate face stays**. Every tile carries its own solid, hard-coated PC cap. A cap never strains, so its hard coat never cracks. `[DECISION]`

### 21.2.3 The `508 ppi` display

**The ruling**. The density stays `508 ppi`, a pitch of `50.0 µm`, in every state. `[DECISION]` The pixels live on rigid tiles, so the stretch never stretches the pixel pitch.

| The item | The value |
|---|---|
| the emitter | micro-LED, `5` to `8 µm` chips, or a blue micro-LED with quantum-dot colour conversion |
| the carrier | glass, `3.2 ppm/K`, with through-glass vias for the drive lines |
| the pixels per tile | `200 x 200` |
| the face at rest | `1 600 x 3 200` pixels, the frame size of Section 21.5.1 |
| the refresh | `240 Hz`, which micro-LED reaches easily `[GENERAL]` |
| the dead pixels | under `5` per device, by inspection and laser repair |

**The stretched view**. At `25 %` stretch the pixels cover `61 %` of the stretched face, and dark diamonds open between the tiles. Each tile keeps `508 ppi`. `U1` warps the image tile by tile, so a circle stays a circle. At `41 %` the pixels cover `47.5 %`. `[DECISION]`

**The honest distance**. The best shipping micro-LED face is about `326 ppi` (Garmin and AUO, 2025). `[PUBLICATION]` A `508 ppi` tile is `1.56` times that, so gap G-40 stays open until gate DSP-19 passes.

### 21.2.4 The invisible seam

**The order**. A seam inside the screen must not show, and the panels stay removable. `[DECISION]`

**The principle: a lattice-continuous seam**. The edge pixel sits `20 µm` from the cut. The cut is at most `10 µm`. So the pitch across the seam is `20 + 10 + 20 = 50.0 µm`, the same as everywhere else. The eye sees no seam, because the lattice has none. `[DECISION]`

| The rule | The value | The reason |
|---|---|---|
| the sub-pixel row | parallel to the cut | a triad across the cut leaves only `5 µm` of edge margin |
| the edge margin | `16 µm` from the chip to the cut | above the `5` to `10 µm` chipping of an ultrafast-laser cut |
| the cut face | black-coated | no grazing light scatters from it |
| the PC cap kerf | `10 µm` at `+50 °C`, `37 µm` at `-10 °C` | a `10 mm` cap grows `27 µm` over `40 K` |
| why the cap kerf hides | over `92 %` of a micro-LED face is black | a dark line under `50 µm` between continuous pixel columns is not seen |
| the glass carrier | `1.3 µm` of growth over `40 K` | the lattice stays continuous over temperature |
| the stack | glass, pixel layer and glass, or a floating carrier on slip OCA | an asymmetric PC on glass bond curls about `1.8 mm` over `40 mm` |

**The removable quadrants**. Each quadrant `Q1..Q4` lifts out and seats again in under `3 s`.

1. **The locator**. A kinematic seat of silicon-nitride balls in tungsten-carbide vees, held by N52H magnets. It repeats to under `1 µm`. `[GENERAL]`
2. **No overconstraint**. The `24` dovetails and the edge lip become non-locating, so they do not fight the seat.
3. **The hinges at the quadrant line**. They are split couplers that close on the seat. Gate MEC-40.
4. **The closed loop**. A capacitive strip and a thermistor sit on each seam face. `U1` shifts the image in `3.125 µm` steps, a sixteenth of a pixel.
5. **The calibration**. Each tile stores its edge luminance and colour. `U1` recalibrates within the `3 ms` dead-pad window after a swap.

### 21.2.5 The energy, with no loss to the stretch

| The store | The volume | The energy | The tag |
|---|---|---|---|
| the body at `600 Wh/L` | `12 800 mm² x 0.83 x 0.90 mm = 9.56 cm³` | `5.7 Wh` | `[HYPOTHESIS]` |
| the body at `750 Wh/L`, silicon anode | the same | `7.2 Wh` | `[HYPOTHESIS]` |
| the B module cell | `20.0 x 8.0 x 1.50 mm` at `600 Wh/L` | `0.14 Wh` | `[HYPOTHESIS]` |
| the dock pack | in the dock body | `20 Wh` | `[HYPOTHESIS]` |

**The result**. The body keeps the full `5.7 Wh` of the unstretched body and stretches `25 %`. With a silicon-anode cell it holds `7.2 Wh`. Against the withdrawn body, that is `1.9` times the stretch and `3.4` to `4.2` times the energy. `[DECISION]` gate ENR-22.

**The run time**. A video session draws about `2` to `3 W`. The body and the B cell run it for about `1.9` to `3.7 h`. The dock pack refills the Leaf about three times away from a wall. `[HYPOTHESIS]`

**The cells**. Each tile holds one hermetic micro cell, `9.25 x 9.25 x 0.90 mm`, in a laser-welded `30 µm` steel or titanium can. Each cell has its own fuse and balance channel. A `4 x 32` multiplexer in the B ring watches them. Gate ENR-23.

**The silicon anode**. A shipping silicon-anode phone cell measures about `935 Wh/L`. `[PUBLICATION]` Enovix AI-1. A `0.90 mm` micro can loses `15` to `25 %` to its walls, so `750 Wh/L` is the target. If the cells reach only `600 Wh/L`, the body still holds `5.7 Wh`.

**The old energy claims**. The L.5.0 cell of `3.0 Wh` was about `1 060 Wh/L`. The L.5.0 bridge cell was about `4 960 Wh/L`. Both are withdrawn, because no cell reaches them.

### 21.2.6 Qi2.2 on a stretching body

**The ruling**. Qi2.2 stays, at the `25 W` magnetic power profile. `[DECISION]`

1. **The coil**. Four sector coils, one per quadrant, are wired in series around the `+` crossing. They act as one round receiver coil of about `Ø44` to `46 mm`.
2. **The seam links**. Only two series links cross each quadrant seam, through gold leaves in the seat.
3. **The conductor**. Two layers of plated copper, `50` to `75 µm`, on polyimide. Each turn splits into `3` or `4` strands, so it crosses a hinge on the neutral plane.
4. **The layer**. Coil and shield take `0.20 mm` of the `0.24 mm` margin in the tile stack. No cell volume is taken.
5. **The magnets**. `16` small N52 magnets sit in the chamfered tile corners on the alignment circle.
6. **The charge state**. Charging runs at rest, when every hinge reads `θ = 0`. A pull during charging pauses the coil.
7. **The heat**. `25 W` into `5.7 Wh` is about `4.4 C`. A cooled pad and the `36.2 °C` skin limit set the sustained rate. `[HYPOTHESIS]` gate ENR-24.

**The honest distance**. The best published stretchable receiver carries about `3.8 W` (npj Flexible Electronics, 2024). `[PUBLICATION]` This design keeps the coil on rigid tiles, so it is a phone coil cut into sectors. Certification is gate ENR-24.

### 21.2.7 The tile stack, `2.00 mm`

| The layer | The thickness |
|---|---|
| hard-coated PC cap with the AgNW touch grid | `0.25 mm` |
| optical adhesive | `0.05 mm` |
| glass, micro-LED layer and glass | `0.25 mm` |
| slip OCA | `0.03 mm` |
| Qi coil and shield, or a graphite spreader outside the coil | `0.20 mm` |
| hermetic micro cell in its can | `0.90 mm` |
| floating back skin | `0.08 mm` |
| the hinge routing and the tolerance | `0.24 mm` |
| **total** | **`2.00 mm`** |

## 21.3 The B module: a `4.00 mm` module with a `1.00 mm` bar

### 21.3.1 The geometry, read from the CAD

The figures come from `uploads/b.png`, `cad/monogram_letter.py` and `cad/models/monogram-module.stl`. `[DECISION]`

| The item | The value | The source |
|---|---|---|
| the unfolded `b` | `40.0 x 32.0 mm` | `b.png` |
| the bar width in the drawing | `4.0 mm` | `STEM_W = 4.0` |
| the bar | `32.0` along the edge `x 4.0` across the edge `x 1.00 mm` high | `STEM_THICK = 1.00` |
| the bar stance | horizontal, seated on the top edge of the `2.00 mm` body | `monogram_letter.py` |
| the module thickness | `4.00 mm` | `monogram-module.stl`, `Z = -2.00..+2.02` |
| the ring | `Ø32.0` outside, `Ø24.0` inside, lying on the screen face | `RING_RO`, `RING_RI` |
| the bend | one moulded `90°` corner at the natural gap of `4.0 mm`, no hinge | `b.png` |
| the seats | any of the four edges, on the front or the back face | Part 5 |

**What changed from the first L.6.0 text**. The first text read the bar as a `1.00 mm` wall standing `8 mm` off the face. Ayaz corrected it: the bar is `1.00 mm` and lies on the edge. The cantilever table is withdrawn, because the edge backs the whole bar.

**A note on the CAD**. `cad/leaf_v2.py` still sets `MOD_T = 2.00`. The module STL is `4.00 mm`. The sheets and renders are redrawn to `4.00 mm` with the next sheet set.

### 21.3.2 The `1.00 mm` bar stack

| The layer | The thickness | The material | The duty |
|---|---|---|---|
| top skin | `0.20 mm` | LCP, 40 % glass fibre | the hard face, RF transparent |
| antenna layer | `0.05 mm` | laser direct structured copper | A1 and A2 radiators |
| core | `0.40 mm` | LCP, 40 % glass fibre | the stiffness |
| mmWave layer | `0.10 mm` | two-layer LCP flex | the `60 GHz` array, looking up and out |
| contact layer | `0.25 mm` | LCP with `12` gold spring leaves | the body link, on the seat tile |
| **total** | **`1.00 mm`** | | |

**The body link**. The `12` leaves sit in a `9.6 mm` field over one edge tile, the seat tile. The other edge tiles turn under the bar without moving the contacts. `[DECISION]` gate MON-23.

### 21.3.3 The ring on a stretching face

1. The ring covers about three tiles. Its underside is a low-friction pad with magnets.
2. The bar holds the module on the seat tile. The ring slides on the face as the tiles turn.
3. The pad carries no contacts, so a turning tile breaks no link. Gate MON-21.

### 21.3.4 USB-C is a separate part

1. **The ruling**. USB-C leaves the B module. It is a separately sold port part, as Ayaz ordered before L.5.0. `[DECISION]`
2. **The fit**. The port part clips on any free body edge and uses the same `12` contact pads as the bar.
3. **The EU rule**. Directive (EU) 2022/2380 covers a product that charges by wire. The port part carries the USB-C receptacle for that duty. `[PUBLICATION]`
4. **What it frees**. The ring rim loses a `8.9 x 7.3 x 3.10 mm` receptacle and its controller. The `3.5 mm` jack takes the rim.

### 21.3.5 The best `50 MP` camera in a `4.00 mm` ring

**Why the fold is withdrawn**. A last `45°` fold over a flat `1/1.56"` sensor needs at least the sensor's short side, `6.14 mm`, in height. The ring has `4.00 mm`. So the first L.6.0 camera cannot be built. `[DECISION]`

**The camera of L.6.0**. A vertical column through the ring, with the first lens element as the front window.

| The item | The value |
|---|---|
| the sensor | `50 MP`, `0.61` to `0.64 µm` pixels, `5.2 x 3.9 mm` class, chip-on-board |
| the lens | five elements, `f = 3.3 mm`, `f/2.0`, the first element is the sapphire window |
| the view | about `88°`, a `22 mm` equivalent wide main camera |
| the focus | `0.12 mm` of stroke, sharp from `10 cm` to infinity |
| the stabiliser | in-plane, with `0.08 mm` of clearance |
| the height | `3.30` optics, `0.12` focus, `0.08` clearance, `0.35` sensor, `0.05` cover: `3.90 mm` |
| the rear display hole | `6.5 x 5.3 mm`, about `12 %` of the rear panel |
| the processing | `12.5 MP` binned stream at video rate. The validators run multi-frame fusion |

**The upgrade path**. A `0.56 µm` `50 MP` sensor is `4.59 x 3.44 mm`. Its lens is about `3.0 mm` long. The saved height buys `f/1.8`. `[PUBLICATION]` Samsung shipped `0.56 µm` pixels in 2022.

**The option of three columns**. Three `50 MP` columns at `f/2.0` gather about the light of one `1/1.56"` sensor. They cost `36 %` of the rear panel. The single column is the default. `[DECISION]` gate CAM-21.

### 21.3.6 The `3.5 mm` jack in the ring

1. **The bore**. `Ø3.6 mm` with `0.20 mm` walls, so `4.0 mm` across. It runs along a chord of the ring at `13.8 mm` from the centre.
2. **The fit**. The bore spans `r = 12.0` to `15.6 mm`. It clears the windows at `r = 12.0 mm` and the outer wall at `r = 16.0 mm`.
3. **The form**. A `5 mm` closed blind end and a `9 mm` open trough in the rim. A rim boss of `0.5` to `1.0 mm` seats the plug collar.
4. **The contacts**. Tip, ring and detect sit in the closed end. The sleeve contact grips in the trough.
5. **The seal**. A gasketed contact pocket keeps the module sealed.
6. **The antenna**. The A3 loop routes around the groove. Gate MON-22 and RF-22.

**A precedent**. The vivo X5 Max of 2014 carried a `3.5 mm` jack in a `4.75 mm` body. `[PUBLICATION]`

---

## 21.4 The strongest Wi-Fi with no local processor

### 21.4.1 The antennas

1. **A1, the bar inverted-F**. The `2.4 GHz` quarter wave is about `31 mm`, and the bar is `32 mm`. The bar lies on the body edge, so it radiates from the end of the ground plane. That is the classic top-edge place of a phone antenna. `[GENERAL]`
2. **The height**. The radiator stands `1.00 mm` over the edge ground. A low height narrows the bandwidth, so the aperture tuner re-centres the band. Target efficiency `-2.5 dB`. `[HYPOTHESIS]` gate RF-22.
3. **A2, the bar coupled arm**. A `12 mm` arm on the same layer covers `5.15` to `7.125 GHz`.
4. **A3, the ring loop**. A loop on the ring rim is the second Wi-Fi antenna. The bar lies on the edge and the ring lies on the face, so the two polarisations differ. This gives true `2 x 2` MIMO. Target `-3 dB`. `[HYPOTHESIS]` gate RF-22.
5. **A4, the `1 x 4` `60 GHz` array**. It sits on the mmWave layer of the bar and looks up and out.
6. **No metal in the module shell**. The ring and bar shells are LCP-GF40. The camera, the jack contacts and the shields are the only metal, and they sit away from the bar.
7. **The magnets stay in the ring**. A magnet near A1 costs gain. The 1.5 dB rule of Part 5, Section 5.3.1 holds.
8. **A tuner per antenna**. The IMU reports the edge and the face. `U2A` loads a matching state for each of the eight seats. Gate RF-23.
9. **The body under stretch**. The edge ground turns with the tiles. The tuner reads the hinge angles and re-matches. Gate RF-21.

### 21.4.2 The radios

| The part | The duty | Why it is separate |
|---|---|---|
| `U2A` Wi-Fi 7 and BT 6.0 combo | `2 x 2`, `320 MHz`, multi-link on `5 GHz` and `6 GHz`, BT channel sounding | a shipping class of part `[GENERAL]` |
| `U2B` `60 GHz` and UWB | the dock link and secure ranging with the scrambled timestamp sequence | a separate class of part |
| `U5` 5G standalone modem | configured grant, dual active protocol stack handover, low-latency mobility | the outdoor `30 ms` path needs a full modem, not RedCap |

Each of these carries firmware on its own small core. That is allowed: Section 21.5.1. The modem and its front end inside the `4.00 mm` ring are gate RF-17.

### 21.4.3 The settings that lower the latency

| The setting | The effect |
|---|---|
| multi-link with simultaneous transmit and receive on `5` and `6 GHz` | a packet takes the clean link, so a spike on one link does not stall the frame |
| `6 GHz` first | a cleaner band with wide channels |
| restricted target wake time for the stream | the stream gets reserved air time |
| power save off during a session | no wake-up delay on each frame |
| the low-latency queue and L4S marking end to end | the queue does not grow under load `[GENERAL]` |
| 5G configured grant and a low-latency QoS flow | the uplink does not wait for a scheduling request |
| 802.11bn (Wi-Fi 8) in the roadmap | its main goal is reliability and latency `[PUBLICATION]` |

### 21.4.4 The strongest signal is the shortest path

**The antenna gains a few dB. The distance to the serving validator decides the latency.** The best path for Leaf is a validator near the radio. `[DECISION]`

**The Leaf Dock**. One box holds a Wi-Fi 7 access point, a `60 GHz` radio, a UWB anchor and a `20 Wh` pack. A full Budlum validator inside it is an option, as Q32 said. Gate SW-20.

---

## 21.5 The projection: the validators run the Leaf

### 21.5.1 What "Zero CPU" means from now on

**The definition**. Leaf has no general-purpose application processor and no operating system. It runs no third-party code. `[DECISION]`

**What is allowed**. Fixed-function silicon with signed firmware: the radios, the modem, the video decoder, the TCON, the ISP, the power chip and the secure element. Also a frame store, because a decoder needs one.

**Why the frame store is needed**. A Leaf 160 face is `1 600 x 3 200` pixels, near `15 MB` per frame. A slice decoder with a compressed frame store holds it in about `16 MB`. `[HYPOTHESIS]`

### 21.5.2 The principle: the Leaf is a part of the chain

**The validators run the Leaf as they run Budlum**. The state of every Leaf session is chain state. The applications are on-chain programs. The validators execute them in the same way that they execute blocks. The Leaf holds no state that the network cannot rebuild. `[DECISION]`

| The reading of the Budlum tree | The fact `[TREE]` | The consequence |
|---|---|---|
| `src/registry/role.rs` | `VALIDATOR` is `RoleId(1)`, the role that produces blocks and finality | the same validators run the Leaf. No new role is needed |
| `src/consensus/pos.rs` | the slot is `6` seconds | the frame path does not wait for a block |
| `src/ai/types.rs` | bonded results, with deadlines counted in blocks | a signed, bonded result is already a pattern in the tree |
| `budzero/` | an in-tree zkVM with STARK proofs | a frame result can carry a proof later |

### 21.5.3 Two speeds inside one validator

1. **The state speed**. A user action becomes a transaction. Consensus orders it and finalises it in the slot of `6` s.
2. **The frame speed**. The serving validator applies the same action to its copy of the state at once. It renders and streams the frame at once. It does not wait for the slot.
3. **The rule that joins them**. The frame is optimistic. If finality orders the actions differently, the next frame shows the final state. This is rare, because one user's session has one writer. `[DECISION]`
4. **The honesty of the frame**. Rendering is deterministic, in fixed point. Another validator re-executes a sample of the frames and compares the hash chain. A mismatch is slashed like a double sign. `[DECISION]` research order RG-18.

### 21.5.4 The wallet and the nearest validator

**The Leaf is a wallet**. `U7` holds the keys. The Leaf connects to the nearest validator, as a wallet connects to a node. It runs no session cipher of its own. `[DECISION]`

**Finding the nearest validator, in three layers**. A layer runs only when the layer above finds nothing.

| Layer | The reach | The method |
|---|---|---|
| L0, the room | `0` to `10 m` | BLE or Wi-Fi Aware finds a validator. UWB secure ranging proves the distance to about `±10 cm`, so a relay cannot fake it |
| L1, the metro | under `100 km`, ideally under `20 km` | an anycast bootstrap returns `3` to `5` validators from the on-chain registry. The Leaf probes each once per second and takes the lowest round trip |
| L1, the cell | a few km from the 5G cell | validators at operator edge sites, found by 3GPP edge discovery `[PUBLICATION]` TS 23.558 |
| L2, far | anything beyond | the `far` class, carried by speculative frames and the `U1` compositor |

**A handover costs zero frames**. The sampling re-executor of RG-18 doubles as a hot shadow. It holds the same session state and streams the frame hash chain. When the primary's round trip or loss crosses a limit, `U6` switches at the next slice. Rendering is deterministic, so the decoder needs no new key frame. Multipath QUIC carries both paths. Gate DST-12.

**The proof that a frame is real, with no CPU**.

1. Once per session and on handover, `U7` checks that the validator key is in the active set. It keeps the set current from finality proofs.
2. On every slice, an inline authentication engine in `U6` checks the tag at line rate.
3. Once per second, the validator signs a Merkle root of the frame hashes. `U7` countersigns a receipt with the measured round trip.

**The density, by the protocol**. The dock validator is optional, so density cannot rest on homes alone. `[DECISION]`

1. The receipts carry signed round trips, so the chain holds a live latency map.
2. Emission pays per served session minute, only in areas where the 99th percentile is under `30 ms`.
3. Location is checked by latency from many challengers, not trusted.
4. A validator that forges a frame or lies about latency is slashed.

**The targets**. A validator within `20 km` in a city, about `0.2 ms` of fibre each way. Within `100 km` everywhere covered. The `far` class elsewhere. Gate SW-24.

### 21.5.5 The projection path, part by part

1. The touch grid scans at `480 Hz`. `U7` signs the input batch.
2. `U2A` or `U5` sends the input to the nearest validator, as a transaction and as a frame request.
3. The validator applies the input to the session state and renders the frame, plus the speculative frames.
4. It encodes in low-latency slices and signs a hash chain of the frames.
5. `U6` checks and decodes each slice as it arrives. `U1` composes it into the frame store and scans it out at `240 Hz`.
6. `U7` signs a receipt over the frame hashes it showed. The receipts settle the session in the slot window.

### 21.5.6 The local compositor

The interaction budget of Part 10 is under `10 ms`. `U1` holds a fixed-function compositor for four jobs only: the cursor, the scroll offset, the typed glyph echo and the stretch warp. The validator sends the layers, the glyph atlas and the speculative frames. `U1` only moves, picks and composes them. It runs no program. `[DECISION]` gate SW-21.

### 21.5.7 The latency budget, input to photon

**The order**. `30 ms` holds outdoors too. `[DECISION]`

| The step | Wi-Fi, room validator | Wi-Fi, city validator | 5G, cell-site validator | Far validator |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the touch scan at `480 Hz` and the controller | `1.8 ms` | `1.8 ms` | `1.8 ms` | `1.8 ms` |
| the air uplink | `1.0 ms` | `1.0 ms` | `2.3 ms` | `1.0 ms` |
| the network, both ways | `0.1 ms` | `1.2 ms` | `0.3 ms` | `80.0 ms` |
| the execute and render | `4.3 ms` | `4.3 ms` | `4.3 ms` | `4.3 ms` |
| the first-slice encode | `1.5 ms` | `1.5 ms` | `1.5 ms` | `1.5 ms` |
| the air downlink | `1.0 ms` | `1.0 ms` | `1.3 ms` | `1.0 ms` |
| the first-slice decode | `1.5 ms` | `1.5 ms` | `1.5 ms` | `1.5 ms` |
| the scan-out at `240 Hz` and the pixel response | `2.4 ms` | `2.4 ms` | `2.4 ms` | `2.4 ms` |
| **mean, a new action** | **`13.6 ms`** | **`14.7 ms`** | **`15.4 ms`** | **`93.5 ms`** |
| **99th percentile, a new action** | **under `20 ms`** | **under `21 ms`** | **about `21 ms`** | **over `100 ms`** |
| **a predicted action** | **about `5 ms`** | **about `5 ms`** | **about `5 ms`** | **about `5 ms`** |

**How the 5G column holds**. The uplink waits for a slot of the `2.5 ms` operator pattern, and one retransmission adds about `3 ms`. A simulation of the stages as independent delays gives a 99th percentile of about `21 ms`. A sum of the stage percentiles is not the percentile of the sum. `[HYPOTHESIS]`

**What the 5G column needs**. A validator at the cell site or the operator edge, with local breakout. A low-latency QoS flow with configured grant and L4S. A block error target of `10 %` or less. Without them, a loaded public cell gives about `35` to `60 ms`. Gate DST-11.

**The predicted action**. It skips the network: the touch scan, the pick in `U1` and the scan-out. All rows are `[HYPOTHESIS]` until gate DST-11 measures them.

### 21.5.8 Only decentralised applications

1. `U7` accepts a frame stream only from a key that is an active Budlum validator.
2. The session names the application by its on-chain program entry.
3. A stream that fails either check shows nothing but the local safe screen. Gate SW-22.

## 21.7 The research order for the Budlum tree

| Order | The work | The acceptance test |
|---|---|---|
| **RG-18** | the validator Leaf duty: session state as chain state, fixed-point render, frame hash chain, sampled re-execution, hot shadow, receipts, coverage rewards and slashing | `10 000` frames stream from one validator, a second validator matches a sample bit for bit, and a forged frame is slashed |

---

## 21.8 The new gates

| Gate | The test | The pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| **MEC-39** | the rotating-square body, biaxial | `10 000` cycles at `25 %` and `1 000` at `41 %`, no open trace, no tile crack |
| **MEC-40** | the quadrant swap and the seam | swap in under `3 s`, repeat under `1 µm` over `10 000` swaps, step under `1 µm` from `-10` to `+50 °C` |
| **MEC-41** | the PC caps and the floating back skin | no cap crack, hard coat intact, no skin delamination after `10 000` cycles at `41 %` |
| **MEC-42** | the edge-on polyimide hinge | `10 000` cycles at `2θ = 35°` and `1 000` at `90°`, no crack |
| **ENR-22** | the micro-cell energy | at least `1.42 Wh` per quadrant at `600 Wh/L`, and `1.80 Wh` at `750 Wh/L` |
| **ENR-23** | the cell fuse: a short in one cell | the fuse opens, and the other cells stay in service |
| **ENR-24** | Qi2.2 on the tile body | it passes Qi2.2 `25 W` certification, and the coil resistance moves under `5 %` after `10 000` stretch cycles |
| **DSP-17** | the per-tile warp: a circle and a grid at `25 %` and `41 %` | the shape error under `1 %` |
| **DSP-18** | the touch under stretch | no false touch at full stretch, position error under `1 mm` |
| **DSP-19** | the `508 ppi` micro-LED tile and the seam | the largest pixel gap at a seam is `50.0 µm` or less, and under `5` dead pixels per device |
| **RF-21** | the antennas under stretch | the return loss inside the limit, at rest and at `41 %` |
| **RF-22** | the bar and ring pair as `2 x 2` MIMO, with the jack groove | the envelope correlation under `0.3`, A1 at `-2.5 dB` or better |
| **RF-23** | the eight seats, front and back on four edges | each seat inside `1.5 dB` of the best seat |
| **MON-21** | the module drop, `1.5 m`, six faces, and the ring slide on a stretching face | the bar intact, the seat holds, the body link stays closed |
| **MON-22** | the `3.5 mm` jack | `5 000` plug cycles, a side load, and the seal passes |
| **MON-23** | the bar and the module | bar `1.00 ± 0.05 mm`, module `4.00 ± 0.05 mm` on every unit |
| **CAM-21** | the `50 MP` vertical column in the `4.00 mm` ring | it fits at `f/2.0` or faster, focuses from `10 cm`, and meets CAM-9 after validator fusion |
| **SW-20** | the Leaf Dock with its pack, and with the optional validator | it charges the Leaf away from a wall, and the validator option serves a room session |
| **SW-21** | the local compositor: cursor, scroll, glyph echo, warp | each job under `10 ms`, with no program run on the device |
| **SW-22** | the stream check: a key that is not an active validator | the stream is refused and the safe screen shows |
| **SW-23** | the speculative frames: scroll, button and menu | a predicted action shows in under `10 ms` with the network cut |
| **SW-24** | the nearest validator: UWB distance proof, probes and the coverage map | the Leaf serves from the lowest round trip, and a relayed validator fails the distance proof |
| **DST-11** | input to photon, four paths including 5G outdoors | Wi-Fi and 5G cell-site paths under `30 ms` at the 99th percentile |
| **DST-12** | the validator handover while the user moves | the switch loses no frame and causes no reload |

# BUDLUM LEAF · THE DIRECTIVE · PART 22 · HOST POWER AND THE LIVING LEAF

**Revision L.6.1 (proposed) · 2026-10-19 · the standing of this part**. This part adds to Part 21 and changes no L.6.0 number. Where it finds a fault in an earlier part, Section 22.16 names it. Tags follow Part 20: `[DECISION]`, `[HYPOTHESIS]`, `[PUBLICATION]`, `[GENERAL]` and `[TREE]`.

**The order that created this part**. Ayaz, 2026-10-19: *"Donanım çalıştığı sürece çalışabilecek bir cihaz aslında bir ilktir. Asıl pil donanımda olsa ve sürekli Badlam Leaf çalışsa ne olacak? Bataryanın kapasitesi önemli olmuyor."* Ayaz also handed the whole management of the program to the Program Lead. The Lead ran five study seats, and their work is in `hw/hostpower/`. `[DECISION]`

**The standing**. The thesis is a recommendation with measured limits, in the manner of Part 17. Every number is `[HYPOTHESIS]` until its gate runs. The five studies found that the thesis holds in a narrower form than the order states. This part writes the narrower form, and it writes what the narrower form still wins.

---

## 22.1 The question and the honest answer

**The question**. What happens if the main energy lives in the hardware, and the Leaf keeps only a screen and a small cell? The Leaf then works as long as the hardware works. `[DECISION]`

| The part of the idea | The result of the studies | The tag |
|---|---|---|
| the cell is not the source of life | true on a paired host. The cell becomes the bridge between hosts | `[HYPOTHESIS]` |
| capacity does not matter | false as stated. Capacity moves into the place. The Leaf stops paying for it in grams, millimetres and heat | `[DECISION]` |
| the cell lasts longer | true and large: `13.4` years against `1.8` years in the model, `7.2` times | `[HYPOTHESIS]` |
| the heat falls | false. The heat comes from the session, and the battery path adds only `0.04 W` | `[HYPOTHESIS]` |
| the heat is solved by a host | only by a host that pulls heat: the dock plate holds all `72` cases | `[HYPOTHESIS]` |
| a host gives no risk | false until the power door of Section 22.8 exists | `[HYPOTHESIS]` |
| a session survives a power gap | true for finalized chain state and keys. Section 22.9 lists the losses | `[HYPOTHESIS]` |
| this is a world first | false as stated. Section 22.11 gives the claim that survives | `[PUBLICATION]` |

**The one-line answer**. The Leaf lives as long as its host feeds it, if the host is a paired host with a heat path. The cell stops being the fuel and becomes the bridge. `[DECISION]`

---

## 22.2 The Host Life Law

**The law**. On a paired host with a heat path, a Leaf lives as long as the host supplies power. Between two hosts, a bridge store keeps the session. `[DECISION]`

The first brief of the program stated four conditions. The studies corrected two of them.

| No. | The condition, as corrected | What changed |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Power**. `P_host >= P_load + P_topup`, with the `8.7 W` peak and the path loss included | a host under `9 W` on the VHV rail fails the peak |
| 2 | **Heat**. The hottest face point stays at or under `36.2 °C`, in the ambient of the host | in free air no interface passes. The dock plate passes |
| 3 | **Trust**. The host never touches the wallet, the signing core, the firmware slots or the data lanes | the old text said "before its handshake". A host that powers the handshake touches the bridge rail first |
| 4 | **Continuity**. A power gap never loses finalized chain state or keys, and `E_buffer >= P_peak x t_handover` for the live frame | chain state holds. The open second of input and the live frame can be lost |

**The scope rule**. The law holds for a paired host or a host that a paired party vouches for. An unknown host gives `0.5 W` and the safe screen. `[DECISION]` Trust studies, `hw/hostpower/trust/`.

---

## 22.3 The three energy tiers, as the studies left them

| Tier | The store | What it does | The finding |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Bare** | a capacitor store of about `5 J` | lights the face for `2.2 s` at `2.24 W` and `0.57 s` at `8.7 W`, then ends the session | it does not fit the margin. It is an accessory, not a product |
| **Buffer** | `15` to `20` micro cells of the `128` | carries the delta class for `5 min` between hosts | power limited: `20` cells carry `3.1 W` at `3 C` |
| **Full** | all `128` cells, `5.9` to `7.4 Wh` | runs `2.6` to `3.6 h` of a delta session with no host | unchanged from L.6.0 |

`[HYPOTHESIS]` `hw/hostpower/powerpath/`, `tiers.py`.

### 22.3.1 What decides each tier

1. **Bare does not fit.** The thinnest published double layer capacitor is `0.9 mm`. A `3 s` safe screen takes `3` tiles of cell layer. `[PUBLICATION]` source in the study.
2. **Bare ends in a cold restart.** The B cell then holds the radios, face dark, for `16.2 min`. Then the Leaf restarts from chain state.
3. **Buffer is limited by power, not energy.** The `8.7 W` peak needs `53` cells at `3 C`, about `3.1 Wh`. `[HYPOTHESIS]`
4. **A `30 min` delta bridge needs `2.4 Wh`.** That is `58 %` over the first brief.
5. **Buffer cells sit in all four quadrants.** One quadrant holding all blacks the body `3 s` on a swap.
6. **Buffer keeps Article 11.** The cells sit in the four removable quadrants, as the L.6.0 cells do. `[DECISION]`

**The ruling**. Buffer is the first host-power product tier. Full stays as the freedom tier. Bare stays a study until a store fits the margin. `[DECISION]`

### 22.3.2 The saving of the Buffer tier

| The saving, per Leaf | The value | The tag |
|---|---|---|
| mass of cells | `12 g` | `[HYPOTHESIS]` |
| lithium | `0.64 g` | `[HYPOTHESIS]` |
| cobalt | `5.3 g` | `[HYPOTHESIS]` |
| thickness | `0.9 mm`, only if the cells leave the body | `[HYPOTHESIS]` |

The saving is small per Leaf. At one million Leafs it is `635 kg` of lithium and `5.3 t` of cobalt. `[GENERAL]`

---

## 22.4 The power path: the host feeds the load and the cell floats

**The rule**. The host carries the load. The cell floats near `50 %` and never takes a charge above `0.2 C` during a session. `[DECISION]`

| The rail mode | The meaning | The cell current |
|---|---|---|
| `HOST_FEED` | the host carries the whole load and the cells float | `0` |
| `HOST_TOPUP` | the host carries the load and fills the cells up to the float | up to `0.5 C` in |
| `BRIDGE` | no host, and the cells carry the load by a shed ladder | out |
| rail island | body dark, ring on the B cell, then `U7` and U4 ROM only | `0` |

The thresholds are `HOST_FEED` at `55 %` pack charge and `HOST_TOPUP` below `45 %`. The shed ladder is the cell limit above `30 %`, the delta class to `18 %`, and the safe screen to `12 %`. `[DECISION]` proposed in `hw/hostpower/powerpath/powerpath.py`.

### 22.4.1 Why the float matters

| The case | Years to `80 %` capacity | The tag |
|---|---|---|
| Full cell, cycled daily from `100` to `20 %` | `1.8` | `[HYPOTHESIS]` |
| Full cell, host fed, floating at `50 %` | `13.4` | `[HYPOTHESIS]` |
| Buffer of `20` cells, doing all the bridging | `7.7` | `[HYPOTHESIS]` |

The model is Schmalstieg 2014 with the constants of Lee et al. 2023. It was fitted to a `4.2 V` NMC cell and not to the LiCoO2 micro cell. `[PUBLICATION]` The uncertainty is wide, and gate HST-4 measures it.

**What it buys**. The claim "the battery does not matter" becomes "the battery does not wear out first". That claim helps the repair story and the EU lifetime rules. `[DECISION]`

### 22.4.2 The choice of the interface

| The interface | The heat in the Leaf at `8.7 W` | The use |
|---|---|---|
| the `12` bar pads or the dock pads (contact) | `0.28 W` | the always-on path |
| the port part on a seat | `0.28 W` | desks, vehicles, industry |
| Qi2 `15 W` and Qi2.2 `25 W` pad, feeding the load | `0.76 W` | allowed in float mode |
| Qi `5 W` pad | `0.76 W` and `4 W` short of the peak | wake and safe screen only |
| Qi2.2 charging the cell at `25 W` | `9.19 W` in the delta case | at rest, screen off |

**The ruling**. Contact is the always-on interface. Qi is a convenience. A Qi pad feeds the load and never charges the cell during a session. `[DECISION]`

---

## 22.5 The heat: always-on needs a host that pulls heat

The thermal seat ran the L.6.0 model with each host interface. `[TREE]` `hw/hostpower/thermal/`.

| The case, delta session, `25 °C` | The hottest face point | The limit `36.2 °C` |
|---|---|---|
| battery only, free air, still | `41.3 °C` | fails by `5.1 K` |
| contact feed, free air | `42.1` to `44.6 °C` | fails |
| Qi float feed, free air | `42.0` to `42.2 °C` | fails |
| any interface on the dock plate at `22 °C` | margin `+3.1` to `+9.4 K` | passes in `72` of `72` cases |

**What the table says**. A delta session of `2.5 W` is too hot for a still `2.00 mm` body in free air, with a battery or a host. Host power does not change that. The dock plate does. `[HYPOTHESIS]`

1. **The host feeds the load.** Feeding saves `0.15 W` against a `0.2 C` top-up, and `1.0 W` against `1 C`.
2. **A `25 W` charge adds `6.4 W`.** It reaches `37.3 °C` even on the dock, `1.1 K` over the limit.
3. **The sustained delta load in free air is `1.73 W`.** Light wind lifts it to `2.37 W`.
4. **At `30 °C` in free air a Leaf holds `30` to `35 %` of delta power.** Video falls lower.
5. **The Frame** holds `1.05 W` at `25 °C` and `0.20 W` at `35 °C`. A `0.8 W` Frame holds to `24 °C`.

**The dock is a heat sink first.** The dock plate admits `5.7` to `7.2 W`, and the Qi basic profile caps at `4.67 W`. The plate at `22 °C` sits under the dew point at `35 °C` and `60 %` humidity, so the set-point needs a rule. `[HYPOTHESIS]` Gate HST-10.

**The hard consequence**. A claim of always-on in the hand, in free air, is false for the L.6.0 body. The claim holds on a dock, and in a vehicle or machine cradle with a heat path. `[DECISION]` Gap G-46.

---

## 22.6 The host classes

The offered power is on the VHV rail. The `8.7 W` peak is `9.0 W` on VHV. `[TREE]` `hw/hostpower/powerpath/hosts.py`.

| Host | Offered | Carries `8.7 W` | Carries the `25 W` port case | The note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| H0 wall dock | `25 W` | yes | yes | contact. The pilot host |
| H1 dock pack `20 Wh` | `25 W` | yes | yes | `7.0 h` of delta, `3.0 h` with the dock radios on |
| H2 desk USB-C PD | `7.5` to `100 W` | yes | yes at `15 V` | port part on a seat |
| H3 vehicle `12 V` and `24 V` | `30` to `100 W` | yes | yes | a load dump rated adapter is outside the Leaf |
| H4 industrial `24 V` | `25 W` | yes | yes | clamp in the cradle |
| H4 PoE type 2 | `22.9 W` | yes | no, `2.1 W` short | type 3 needed |
| H5 worn bank or belt | `18 W` | yes | no, `7 W` short | a face worn case |
| H6 Qi pad `5 W` | `5 W` | no, `4 W` short | no | wake only |
| H6 Qi2 `15 W` and Qi2.2 `25 W` | `15` and `25 W` | yes | `25 W` pad only | float mode only |

1. **The clamp lives outside the Leaf.** The adapter or the cradle owns the vehicle and industrial clamps.
2. **The Leaf sees `20 V` at most.** A load dump of `79` to `202 V` stops at the cradle. `[PUBLICATION]` ISO 16750-2, secondary summary.
3. **A `5 V` host has no `3:1` ratio.** The U_Q stage needs a bypass mode at `0.79` efficiency. `[HYPOTHESIS]`

**Where the capacity went**. A `20 Wh` dock pack runs a delta session `7.0 h`, which is `2.2` times the Leaf Full cell of `3.2 h`. An `18.5 Wh` worn bank runs it `6.6 h`. With the dock radios on, the pack runs `3.0 h`, the same as the Leaf alone. The capacity moved and it grew, and the radios took part of it back. `[HYPOTHESIS]`

---

## 22.7 The pilot

The study ranked the places where host life matters. `[HYPOTHESIS]` `hw/hostpower/study/USE-CASES.md`.

| The rank | The place | The host | The reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | counters and kiosks with a Budlum dock | H0 | margin `+27.3 W`, a heat path, a Budlum owned host |
| 2 | vehicles | H3 | a load dump rated cradle, long dwell time |
| 3 | industrial panels | H4 | steady power, `24 V` clamp |

**The pilot ruling**. One Turkish city, `100` units on Budlum docks, `60` days. Gate HST-28. `[DECISION]`

---

## 22.8 The power door: a host never gains authority

The trust seat found that power is a door, and a host that powers the handshake already stands inside it. `[TREE]` `hw/hostpower/trust/`, `60` tests pass.

| The rule | The meaning |
|---|---|
| the island rail | `U7` and U4 ROM sit on a regulated bridge rail with brownout detect, never on the host rail |
| the dead pad, widened | the host pads stay at `0 V`, or at a power-only low class, until the handshake passes. At a Qi coil: no rail connected |
| the HostRecord | class, maximum power, voltage window, host key id, optional validator role, expiry and counter, checked by `U7` |
| the policy | an unknown host gets `0.5 W` and the safe screen. A paired host gets its class. A role claim never moves the class |
| the one frame | `U7` hears the host only through one fixed `169` byte frame |
| the atomic commit | no signature and no update straddles a power loss |
| the anchor age | the Leaf refuses the stream when the anchor is older than `6 h` |

**The findings that change the design**.

1. **A RAM replay counter is void.** The host controls every power cycle. A test accepts a replay with RAM counters and refuses it with NVM counters.
2. **`fw/u7` has no NVM and no HostRecord program.** Gap G-44.
3. **Fault injection and power analysis enter the scope.** The signing store needs `0.13` to `2.6 mF`. The unpadded recharge leaks `3.18` bits per signature.
4. **A double glitch can skip the self check and release a bad signature.** The risk is open. Gate HST-16 measures it.
5. **ENR-19 assumes a powered `U7`.** On the sideband alone one handshake takes about `111 ms`.
6. **A host that is the only network path can starve a rotation.** The anchor age rule closes it.
7. **Nobody enrols a host yet.** The authority and the chain revocation of a host key are open. Gap G-48.

`[HYPOTHESIS]` Every island rail number. The ed25519 code is the RFC 8032 reference and stands in for the ML-DSA-87 wallet.

---

## 22.9 The continuity: a power gap is not a loss

The continuity seat added the session states beside the dead-pad machine of `fw/boot`. `[TREE]` `hw/hostpower/continuity/`, `119` tests pass and an `80 000` step fuzz found `0` law violations.

| The session state | The meaning |
|---|---|
| `HOST_AUTH` | a host is at the contact and the pads stay at `0 V` |
| `HOST` | the host passed the `U7` handshake and carries the load |
| `BRIDGE` | the host left and the store holds the floor |
| `ISLAND` | Full tier, no host, past the bridge horizon of `600 s` |
| `PAUSING`, `SUSPENDED` | the pause is committed on chain and `U4` ROM and `U7` stay alive on the B cell |
| `RESUMING`, `RESUME_STATED` | a host passed the handshake on a paused Leaf, or the resume was refused with a stated reason |

**The naming fault.** The power path seat uses `ISLAND` for a rail mode, and this seat uses it for a session state. The directive keeps both and writes the rail one as "rail island". Gap G-45.

### 22.9.1 What the numbers say

| The result | The value |
|---|---|
| cold restart, safe screen after the host pass | `23 ms` |
| live frame after a cold restart | `0.29 s` Wi-Fi, `0.48 s` warm 5G, `2.4 s` cold 5G |
| Buffer and Full on a `75 s` walk | no break. `0` frames lost in `95 %` of draws, `3` at p99 |
| RG-19 chain state added | `249 B`, and a SessionPause of `2 764 B` |
| `120` random pauses, `2` frames to `10^8` frames | the same state root as a run with no pause, every time |

### 22.9.2 What a power gap can still lose

1. **Up to `7 s` of input.** An offline dock with no other radio path cannot flush.
2. **The open second of input, about `5` events.** A host loss without a flush drops it.
3. **The live frame.** The frame store is volatile, so zero-frame handover covers only the live case.
4. **A long gap.** A gap past the grant term or the anchor window needs a wallet action or a re-anchor.

**The ruling**. The session is chain state, and a power gap loses no finalized state or key. The Leaf states each of the four losses to the user. `[DECISION]`

---

## 22.10 The dock: the partner of the living Leaf

The dock gives energy, pulls heat and can serve a room session. `[TREE]` `hw/dock/`.

| The job | The finding |
|---|---|
| energy | the `20 Wh` pack refills the `600 Wh/L` body `2.84` times through the pads |
| heat | the plate and the thermoelectric stage hold every case. The TEC needs `3.8 W` at `35 °C` for delta |
| compute | the optional dock validator serves the room session at `30 ms` |
| failure | a dock pair makes the joint loss of power and primary validator about `60` times more frequent (assumed rates) |

**The ruling**. The Host Life Law never depends on a dock validator. No dock module serves two sessions, and a sudden box death costs `4` to `5` frames that `U1` covers. The Leaf fails over to the nearest other validator while power holds. `[DECISION]`

---

## 22.11 The claim

The study listed `21` products and papers, each with a URL, and some pages did not load. The Ledger Nano S is a wallet with a screen and no cell. The Galaxy Tab Active runs with the cell removed on a PD supply. The XREAL Air 2 is a display with no cell. Dash cams and laptops use bridge cells and capacitors. `[PUBLICATION]` `hw/hostpower/study/PRIOR-ART.md`.

| The sentence | The verdict |
|---|---|
| the strong one: *the first wallet surface that lives as long as its host* | do not use. "First" fails today |
| the defensible one | use at the Designed tier, as written below |
| the safe one | use in public text until a prototype passes |

> On a host, a Budlum Leaf 160 by 80 mm runs for as long as the host supplies power. A small bridge store keeps the wallet session when the power changes hands.

**The rule for the word "first"**. The word stays out of every text until gates HST-25 and HST-26 pass. The open combination is a chain session, a bridge between hosts and a power door that gives the host no authority. The survey is a web search and not a patent search. `[DECISION]`

---

## 22.12 The law and the standards

| The question | The reading | The tag |
|---|---|---|
| is a capacitor a battery under 2023/1542 Article 3(1) | no. A lithium-ion capacitor or a clock cell removes that gain | `[PUBLICATION]` and `[HYPOTHESIS]` |
| does the Buffer tier keep Article 11 | yes, if the cells sit in the removable quadrants | `[HYPOTHESIS]` |
| does Article 11(3) save a fixed Buffer | not safely. The main function is a surface and a wallet | `[HYPOTHESIS]` |
| Qi above `5 W` | needs certificate authentication | `[PUBLICATION]` |
| a vehicle supply | needs ISO 16750-2 test A, load dump rated: `101 V` on `12 V`, `202 V` on `24 V` systems | `[PUBLICATION]` secondary |
| a host fed body worn device | IEC 62368-1 class ES1 and a stated power source class on every host input | `[HYPOTHESIS]` |

**The honest limits**. Several pages did not load, among them EUR-Lex and the Commission notice. The PS limits, the PoE ranges and the ISO 7637-2 pulses are recalled and carry `[HYPOTHESIS]`. This is a risk review and it is not legal advice. Nine questions go to a lawyer and six to a laboratory. Gates HST-26 and HST-27.

---

## 22.13 The recommended configuration, in one table

| The decision | The ruling | The reason |
|---|---|---|
| D-9 the first host-power tier | Buffer, `15` to `20` cells over four quadrants | Bare does not fit. Full is the freedom tier |
| D-10 the always-on interface | contact, on the dock and the port part | Qi loses `0.76 W` against `0.28 W`, and Qi `5 W` fails the peak |
| D-11 the cell policy on a host | float near `50 %`, top-up at `0.2 C` or less | `7.2` times the cell life and `1.0 W` less heat than `1 C` |
| D-12 the dock | a heat sink and a power host first, a validator second | the plate passes `72` of `72` cases |
| D-13 the trust base | `U7` with NVM counters and a HostRecord | a RAM counter is void on a host |
| D-14 the claim | the defensible sentence, with no "first" | a web survey of `21` rows found no join. It is not a patent search |
| D-15 the pilot | counters and kiosks on Budlum docks, one Turkish city | `+27.3 W` margin and a heat path |

`[DECISION]` of the Program Lead, under the order of 2026-10-19. Ayaz can reverse any row.

---

## 22.14 The research order for the Budlum tree

| Order | The work | The acceptance test |
|---|---|---|
| **RG-19** | session pause and resume: SessionPause, SessionResume, a grant term, an anchor window, and a resume rule that checks the state root | `120` random pauses keep the no-pause state root. A second validator matches a sample bit for bit. A late pause is refused with a stated reason |

The reference model is `sessionchain.py`, and the proto draft is `proto/session_continuity.proto` in `hw/hostpower/continuity/`. It adds `6` messages and `10` error codes at wire `1.1`. `buf lint` has not run. `[TREE]`

## 22.15 The new gates

The five study seats proposed `52` gates under four prefixes. The Lead merged them into one series. The right column names the source gates.

| Gate | The test | The pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| **HST-1** | a Bare store: `3 s` of the safe screen | the store takes at most `3` tiles of cell volume, and VBUS holds `3.40 V`. Source: power path 1 |
| **HST-2** | Buffer group power: `15` cells at `2.24 W` for `5 min`, and the pulse rate at `3`, `5` and `10 C` | VBUS holds `3.40 V`, no cell over `45 °C`. Source: power path 2 and 3 |
| **HST-3** | the U_Q host window at `5`, `9`, `12` and `15 V` | VBUS `3.40` to `4.48 V` at `0.93` efficiency, and a `5 V` host stays under `1.0 W` of body heat |
| **HST-4** | cell cycles and storage: `800` cycles, `1 000 h` at `3.86 V`, and the `30` day float policy | `800` cycles met, alpha within `2` times of the model, pack charge `45` to `55 %` |
| **HST-5** | the island rail: `1 000` host drops and `100` quadrant swaps | `1V8_AON` stays over `1.75 V`, and the B cell alone runs `2.5 h`. Source: power path 7 and trust 3 |
| **HST-6** | hand-over on a bench, `100` runs, with Buffer cells spread | VBUS holds `3.40 V` for the whole `3 s`, with at most `3` mode changes per event |
| **HST-7** | host classes H0 to H6 each carry `8.7 W` for `10 min` | the table of Section 22.6 within `10 %`, and no host reaches VBUS before the proof |
| **HST-8** | vehicle cradle: cranking and load dump A and B | VHV never over `20 V`, the session holds through the dropout, and ISO 16750-2 passes |
| **HST-9** | the free air delta session at `25 °C`, still, `60 min` | the gate records the result against `36.2 °C`. The model expects a failure of `5.1` to `8.4 K` |
| **HST-10** | the dock plate session at `20`, `25`, `30` and `35 °C`, with the dew point set-point | at or under `36.2 °C` for `60 min`, and no condensation. The model predicts `28` to `31 °C` |
| **HST-11** | the feed the load rule: Qi float with cell current under `20 mA`, and a charge during a session | float within `0.3 W` of the battery run, and the cell charges only below `34.2 °C` at `0.2 C` or less |
| **HST-12** | the interface loss of the `12` pads, new and worn, at `1.9` and `3.3 W` | at or under `0.10 W`. A value over `0.34 W` is a stop |
| **HST-13** | the throttle test at `30 °C` in free air | the delta session holds at least `30 %` of its power at or under `36.2 °C` |
| **HST-14** | the Frame on a head form, `0.8 W` plus a `0.34 W` connector loss, at `25 °C` | both surfaces at or under `36.2 °C` for `60 min` |
| **HST-15** | counterfeit adapters, a `0` to `36 V` sweep, bad frames and data-only pairing | pads and island at `0 V`, zero grants above the unknown class, zero keys added |
| **HST-16** | `10^6` glitches during signing, and a Welch test of the line current over `10^6` signatures | zero faulty signatures, and the t value under `4.5` |
| **HST-17** | `10 000` power cuts at random instants, including a U7 journal write | the old or the new record, no reused counter, an action sent once or never |
| **HST-18** | a rogue Qi transmitter and the cable breakaway | no face point over `36.2 °C`, draw under the unknown class, the cable parts at `6.5 N` or less |
| **HST-19** | the anchor age rule: a host as the only path for `6 h` | the stream is refused |
| **HST-20** | `10 000` random plug and unplug sequences on a fake host | no pad current before the pass, every state shows a surface, no host holds a key |
| **HST-21** | a host cut at `8.7 W` and at `2.24 W` on each tier | lit time at least `0.95` of the table, and the pause finishes with `10` times its energy |
| **HST-22** | RG-19 on `validator/leaf-duty` | the same state root and frame hash chain as a run with no pause |
| **HST-23** | the dock cut and a dock box kill, with and without a second radio path | at most `9` frames covered, no reload, and the lost input at most one gossip delay |
| **HST-24** | a resume after `2` days and after `30` days, and the cold against the attached modem | a stated refusal and never a silent resume, and the live frame in `600 ms` at p99 with the modem attached |
| **HST-25** | a professional prior art search of patents and products | a dated report that finds no product that joins a chain session, a host bridge and a power door |
| **HST-26** | a patent attorney opinion on "first", and a legal opinion on nine questions | each sentence and each question answered in writing |
| **HST-27** | an accredited lab report under IEC 62368-1, and a Qi receiver test | ES1 and a stated power class on every host input, and the receiver refuses an uncertified pad above `5 W` |
| **HST-28** | a pilot of `100` units on Budlum docks for `60` days | every record kept, no chain state lost, hottest face point at or under `36.2 °C` |
| **HST-29** | the claim check against PRG-6 | each public sentence maps to a gate record or to a study |

The names of the source gates stay in the team files, so each raw plan keeps its own trace. `[TREE]`
